  "short_description": {
    "id": "Far darrig (fear dearg, \"Lelaki Merah\") adalah peri dalam mitologi Irlandia yang mengenakan mantel dan topi merah dan gemar bercanda keji.",
    "en": "The far darrig (fear dearg, 'Red Man') is a fairy of Irish myth who wears a red coat and cap and loves gruesome practical jokes.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "far-darrig-c01",
      "far-darrig-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Far darrig atau fear dearg adalah jenis peri dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish). Nama far darrig adalah pelafalan Anglophone dari kata Irlandia fear dearg, \"Lelaki Merah\" (Red Man), karena ia dikatakan mengenakan mantel dan topi merah. Mereka kadang disebut Rat Boys karena konon gemuk, berkulit gelap berbulu, bermoncong panjang, dan berekor kurus. Far darrig umumnya digambarkan pendek, kadang tinggi, dan dikatakan dapat mengubah ukurannya; uraian T. Crofton Croker tahun 1832 dari Munster menyebut tingginya sekitar 2,5 kaki, berwajah keriput, berambut kelabu panjang, dan bertopi kerucut.",
      "en": "The far darrig or fear dearg is a kind of fairy in Irish myth. The name is an Anglophone rendering of the Irish fear dearg, 'Red Man', for the red coat and cap he is said to have on. They are sometimes called Rat Boys, being said to be fat, with dark hairy skin, long snouts and thin tails. Usually short though at times tall, the far darrig is said to change size; an 1832 account by T. Crofton Croker from Munster makes them about 2.5 feet tall, wrinkle-faced, long grey-haired and wearing a sugarloaf hat.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "far-darrig-c01",
        "far-darrig-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Fairy and Folk Tales of the Irish Peasantry yang disunting W. B. Yeats, far darrig digolongkan peri penyendiri bersama leprechaun dan clurichaun, dan disebut sibuk dengan lelucon, terutama lelucon keji, misalnya menukar bayi dengan changeling. Menolak permintaan far darrig membawa sial karena mereka sangat kreatif dalam memikirkan hukuman. Menurut artikel fr, ia cenderung memaksa penghuni rumah menerimanya dan memberinya tempat terbaik di dekat perapian, lalu mengeringkan pakaiannya yang busuk di atas tungku; jika ditolak, ia akan terus mengganggu mereka.",
      "en": "In Fairy and Folk Tales of the Irish Peasantry, edited by W. B. Yeats, the far darrig is classed as a solitary fairy with the leprechaun and clurichaun, and is said to busy himself with practical jokes, above all gruesome ones, such as swapping babies for changelings. Refusing a far darrig's request was unlucky, as they were very inventive with punishments. According to the fr article he tends to force householders to take him in and give him the best seat by the fire, then dries his foul clothes over the hearth; if refused, he plagues them without end.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gelert adalah anjing legendaris dalam kisah rakyat Wales yang dibunuh tuannya, Llywelyn, karena disangka memangsa bayinya padahal telah menyelamatkannya dari serigala.",
    "en": "Gelert is a legendary hound of Welsh folk-tale killed by his master Llywelyn, who wrongly thought he had attacked the baby he had saved from a wolf.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gelert-c01",
      "gelert-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gelert adalah anjing legendaris dalam sebuah kisah rakyat Wales (Welsh). Ia dikaitkan dengan desa Beddgelert di Gwynedd, Wales Utara, yang namanya berarti \"makam Gelert\". Memang ada makam di selatan desa, tetapi dibuat oleh pemilik hotel setempat pada akhir abad ke-18 untuk menarik wisatawan, dan desa itu sebenarnya dinamai menurut Santo Gelert, orang suci awal Kekristenan Kelt (Celtic Christianity).",
      "en": "Gelert is a legendary hound of a Welsh folk-tale. He is linked to the village of Beddgelert in Gwynedd, North Wales, whose name means 'Gelert's grave'. A grave does lie just south of the village, but a local hotel owner made it in the late 18th century to attract tourists, and the village was in fact named after Saint Gelert, an early saint of Celtic Christianity.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gelert-c01",
        "gelert-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda, pada abad ke-12 atau ke-13 penguasa Wales Llywelyn ab Iorwerth pulang berburu dan mendapati bayinya hilang, buaian terbalik, dan mulut Gelert berlumuran darah. Mengira anjing itu menyerang anaknya, ia membunuh Gelert dengan pedang, lalu mendengar tangis bayinya yang selamat di bawah buaian di samping serigala mati yang dibunuh Gelert. Llywelyn menyesal, menguburkan anjing itu dengan upacara besar, dan tak pernah tersenyum lagi. Kisah ini variasi motif \"Faithful Hound\" (Aarne–Thompson tipe 178A). Menurut artikel de, Gelert adalah hadiah dari Raja John dari Inggris, dan versi tertua motif ini berasal dari India.",
      "en": "By the legend, in the 12th or 13th century the Welsh ruler Llywelyn ab Iorwerth comes home from hunting to find his baby gone, the cradle upset and Gelert's mouth smeared with blood. Thinking the dog had killed the child, he runs Gelert through with his sword, then hears his baby crying safe beneath the cradle beside a dead wolf Gelert had killed. Full of remorse, Llywelyn buries the hound with great ceremony and never smiles again. The tale is a version of the 'Faithful Hound' motif (Aarne–Thompson type 178A). According to the de article Gelert was a gift from King John of England, and the oldest version of the motif comes from India.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gelert-c02",
        "gelert-c03",
        "gelert-c04",
        "gelert-c05",
        "gelert-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "statement": {
        "id": "Gelert hadiah dari Raja John (König Johann Ohneland) dari Inggris (England).",
        "en": "Gelert was a gift from King John (König Johann Ohneland) of England."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gelert-c06",
      "source_id": "gelert-s2",
      "quote": "Das zugrundeliegende Sagenmotiv eines überstürzt zu Unrecht getöteten Tieres, obwohl es sich seinem Menschen gegenüber loyal verhalten hat, ist in Europa, im Orient, Süd- und Ostasien weit verbreitet. Die älteste Version stammt aus Indien.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Motif hewan setia yang dibunuh tergesa-gesa tersebar di Eropa, Timur, Asia; versi tertua dari India (Indien).",
        "en": "The motif of a loyal animal hastily killed is widespread in Europe and Asia; the oldest version is from India (Indien)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gelert-c07",
      "source_id": "gelert-s1",
      "quote": "Despite the similarity, the village was named for Saint Gelert, an early saint in Celtic Christianity.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Desa dinamai Saint Gelert (Santo Gelert), Kekristenan Kelt (Celtic Christianity).",
        "en": "The village was named for Saint Gelert of Celtic Christianity."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Gogmagog adalah raksasa legendaris Matter of Britain yang menurut Geoffrey of Monmouth dilempar dari tebing oleh Corineus dalam pertandingan gulat di Cornwall.",
    "en": "Gogmagog is a legendary giant of the Matter of Britain whom, according to Geoffrey of Monmouth, Corineus hurled off a cliff in a wrestling match in Cornwall.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gogmagog (juga Goemagot, Goemagog, Goëmagot, dan Gogmagoc) adalah raksasa legendaris dalam Matter of Britain. Menurut Historia Regum Britanniae karya Geoffrey of Monmouth (abad ke-12), ia raksasa penghuni Cornwall yang dilempar dari tebing dalam pertandingan gulat dengan Corineus, tokoh asal nama Cornwall dan rekan Brutus dari Troya (Brutus of Troy). Tradisi kemudian menjadikannya keturunan Albina serta kepala dan raksasa terbesar di Albion. Patung Gogmagog dan Corineus yang dipakai dalam pawai Inggris kemudian menjadi patung penjaga di Guildhall, London, dan dikenal sebagai \"Gog and Magog\".",
      "en": "Gogmagog (also Goemagot, Goemagog, Goëmagot and Gogmagoc) is a legendary giant of the Matter of Britain. In Geoffrey of Monmouth's 12th-century Historia Regum Britanniae he is a giant living in Cornwall who is flung from a cliff while wrestling Corineus, namesake of Cornwall and companion of Brutus of Troy. Later tradition made him a descendant of Albina and the chief and largest giant of Albion. Effigies of Gogmagog and Corineus used in English pageants later became guardian figures at Guildhall in London, popularly called 'Gog and Magog'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gogmagog-c01",
        "gogmagog-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya umumnya dianggap berasal dari tokoh Alkitab Gog dan Magog, tetapi Peter Roberts (1811) berpendapat itu rusakan dari Cawr-Madog, \"raksasa atau pejuang agung Madog\", sedangkan menurut artikel id Manley Pope (1862) menganggapnya kesalahan penyebutan Gawr Madoc. Dalam terjemahan Giles atas Geoffrey (Old English Chronicles, Gutenberg), Goëmagot digambarkan setinggi dua belas hasta dan begitu kuat sehingga sekali goyang mencabut pohon ek seperti tongkat hazel. Ia mematahkan tiga rusuk Corineus, tetapi Corineus memanggulnya ke puncak batu karang dan melemparkannya ke laut; tempat jatuhnya disebut Lam Goëmagot, \"Lompatan Goëmagot\".",
      "en": "His name is usually traced to the biblical Gog and Magog, but Peter Roberts (1811) took it as a garbled Cawr-Madog, 'Madog the giant or great warrior', and per the id article Manley Pope (1862) saw it as a mis-rendering of Gawr Madoc. In Giles's translation of Geoffrey (Old English Chronicles, Gutenberg), Goëmagot is twelve cubits tall and so strong he could pull up an oak at one shake as if it were a hazel wand. He breaks three of Corineus's ribs, but Corineus carries him to the top of a high rock and hurls him into the sea; the spot is called Lam Goëmagot, 'Goëmagot's Leap'.",

        "gogmagog-c07",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gogmagog-c01",
      "source_id": "gogmagog-s1",
      "quote": "was a legendary giant in the Matter of Britain. According to Geoffrey of Monmouth's Historia Regum Britanniae (\"The History of The Kings of Britain\", 12th century), he was a giant inhabitant of Cornwall who was thrown off a cliff during a wrestling match with Corineus (the legendary namesake of Cornwall and companion of Brutus of Troy).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gogmagog (Goemagot, Goemagog, Goëmagot, Gogmagoc) raksasa Matter of Britain; Geoffrey of Monmouth, Historia Regum Britanniae: raksasa Cornwall dilempar dari tebing oleh Corineus, rekan Brutus dari Troya (Brutus of Troy); di Britania Raya. Abad ke-12.",
        "en": "Gogmagog (Goemagot, Goemagog, Goëmagot, Gogmagoc) is a giant of the Matter of Britain; in Geoffrey of Monmouth's Historia Regum Britanniae a Cornish giant thrown off a cliff by Corineus, companion of Brutus of Troy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gogmagog-c02",
      "source_id": "gogmagog-s1",
      "quote": "Later tradition expanded on this story, with Gogmagog described as a descendant of Albina, and the chieftain and largest of the giants found by Brutus and his men inhabiting the land of Albion. The effigies of Gogmagog and Corineus, used in English pageantry and later instituted as guardian statues at Guildhall in London, eventually earned the familiar names \"Gog and Magog\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi kemudian: keturunan Albina, raksasa terbesar Albion; patung di Guildhall, London (English), disebut Gog and Magog.",
        "en": "Later tradition: descendant of Albina, the largest giant of Albion; effigies at Guildhall, London, called Gog and Magog."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gogmagog-c03",
      "source_id": "gogmagog-s1",
      "quote": "The name Gogmagog is commonly considered to be derived from the biblical characters Gog and Magog; however, Peter Roberts, author of an 1811 English translation of the Welsh chronicle Brut Tysilio (itself a translation of Geoffrey of Monmouth's Historia Regum Britanniae), argued that it was a corruption of Cawr-Madog",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama dari Gog dan Magog Alkitab; Peter Roberts (1811): rusakan Cawr-Madog.",
        "en": "The name is from the biblical Gog and Magog; Peter Roberts (1811): a corruption of Cawr-Madog."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gogmagog-c04",
      "source_id": "gogmagog-s3",
      "quote": "Nama \"Gogmagog\" sering dihubungkan dengan karakter Alkitab Gog dan Magog; namun Manley Pope, pengarang terjemahan Inggris 1862 dari kronik Wales Brut y Brenhinedd (karya itu sendiri merupakan terjemahan \"Historia Regum Britanniae\" karya Monmouth) menyatakan bahwa nama tersebut merupakan kesalahan penyebutan dari Gawr Madoc (Madoc Agung).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel id, Manley Pope (1862) menyebut nama itu kesalahan penyebutan Gawr Madoc (Madoc Agung).",
        "en": "Per the id article, Manley Pope (1862) said the name was a mis-rendering of Gawr Madoc (Madoc Agung)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gogmagog-c05",
      "source_id": "gogmagog-s2",
      "quote": "Among the rest was one detestable monster, named Goëmagot, in stature twelve cubits, and of such prodigious strength that at one shake he pulled up an oak as if it had been a hazel wand.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Giles: Goëmagot setinggi dua belas hasta, mencabut pohon ek seperti tongkat hazel; Old English Chronicles, Gutenberg.",
        "en": "Giles's translation: Goëmagot twelve cubits tall, pulling up an oak like a hazel wand."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gogmagog-c06",
      "source_id": "gogmagog-s2",
      "quote": "hurled down the savage monster into the sea; where falling on the sides of craggy rocks, he was torn to pieces, and coloured the waves with his blood. The place where he fell, taking its name from the giant's fall, is called Lam Goëmagot, that is, Goëmagot's Leap, to this day.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Corineus melemparnya ke laut; tempatnya Lam Goëmagot, Lompatan Goëmagot (Goëmagot's Leap).",
        "en": "Corineus hurled him into the sea; the place is Lam Goëmagot, Goëmagot's Leap."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gogmagog-c07",
      "source_id": "gogmagog-s2",
      "quote": "broke three of his ribs, two on his right side and one on his left. At which Corineus, highly enraged, roused up his whole strength, and snatching him upon his shoulders, ran with him",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Goëmagot mematahkan tiga rusuk Corineus, yang lalu memanggulnya.",
        "en": "Goëmagot broke three of Corineus's ribs; Corineus then carried him on his shoulders."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Knocker adalah makhluk mitos mirip gnome yang hidup di bawah tanah dalam cerita rakyat Cornwall dan Devon, penghuni tambang yang mengetuk dinding untuk memperingatkan penambang.",
    "en": "The knocker is a mythical gnome-like underground creature of Cornish and Devon folklore, a mine-dweller that knocks on walls to warn miners.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "knocker-folklore-c01",
      "knocker-folklore-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Knocker, Knacker, atau Tommyknocker (AS) adalah makhluk mitos mirip gnome yang hidup di bawah tanah dalam cerita rakyat Cornwall (Cornish) dan Devon. Padanannya di Wales (Welsh) adalah coblyn, dan ia berkerabat dekat dengan leprechaun Irlandia (Irish), kloker dari Kent (Kentish), dan brownie Inggris dan Skotlandia (English and Scottish). Orang Cornwall menggambarkannya bertinggi sekitar 61 cm, berkepala besar tak seimbang, berlengan panjang, berkulit keriput, berjanggut putih, berpakaian penambang mini, dan suka mengusili seperti mencuri perkakas dan makanan penambang.",
      "en": "The Knocker, Knacker or Tommyknocker (US) is a mythical gnome-like underground being of Cornish and Devon folklore. Its Welsh counterpart is the coblyn, and it is close kin to the Irish leprechaun, the Kentish kloker and the English and Scottish brownie. The Cornish picture it about 61 cm tall, with an oversized head, long arms, wrinkled skin and white whiskers, dressed as a tiny miner and given to random mischief such as stealing miners' tools and food left lying about.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knocker-folklore-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penambang Cornwall percaya knocker kecil menuntun mereka ke urat timah yang kaya, dan kemudian mengetuk dinding tambang untuk memperingatkan runtuhan. Umumnya dianggap baik, mereka juga usil, menyembunyikan perkakas dan memadamkan lilin. Satu tafsiran menganggap mereka arwah orang Yahudi yang bekerja di tambang pada abad ke-11 dan ke-12, tafsiran lain arwah korban tambang; sebagai ungkapan terima kasih penambang melempar gigitan terakhir pasty mereka ke dalam tambang. Pada 1820-an penambang Wales dan Cornwall membawa kisah Tommyknocker ke Pennsylvania dan kemudian ke Colorado, Nevada, dan California. Menurut artikel de, nama mereka berasal dari kebiasaan mengetuk dinding tambang.",
      "en": "Cornish miners believed the little knockers led them to rich tin lodes, and later that they knocked on mine walls to warn of collapse. Mostly thought kindly, they were also pranksters who hid tools and snuffed candles. Some say they are the ghosts of Jewish miners of the 11th and 12th centuries, another the spirits of those killed underground; in thanks miners tossed the last bite of their pasties into the mine. In the 1820s Welsh and Cornish miners carried Tommyknocker tales to Pennsylvania and later to Colorado, Nevada and California. According to the de article their name comes from their habit of knocking on mine walls.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knocker-folklore-c02",
        "knocker-folklore-c03",
        "knocker-folklore-c04",
        "knocker-folklore-c05",
        "knocker-folklore-c08",
        "knocker-folklore-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "url": "https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knockers",
      "title": "Knockers",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (de)",
      "published": null,

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "knocker-folklore-c01",
      "source_id": "knocker-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The Knocker, Knacker, or Tommyknocker (US) is a mythical, subterranean, gnome-like creature in Cornish and Devon folklore. The Welsh counterpart is the coblyn. It is closely related to the Irish leprechaun, Kentish kloker and the English and Scottish brownie. The Cornish describe the creature as a little person 2 ft 0 in (0.61 m) tall",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Knocker (Knacker, Tommyknocker, AS) makhluk bawah tanah mirip gnome cerita rakyat Cornwall (Cornish) dan Devon; Wales (Welsh) coblyn; kerabat leprechaun Irlandia (Irish), kloker Kent (Kentish), brownie Inggris dan Skotlandia (English and Scottish); 61 cm; usil mencuri perkakas (Coblyn, Leprechaun, Brownie); di Britania dan AS.",
        "en": "The Knocker (Knacker, Tommyknocker) is a subterranean gnome-like creature of Cornish and Devon folklore; Welsh coblyn; kin to the Irish leprechaun, Kentish kloker, English and Scottish brownie; 0.61 m tall."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knocker-folklore-c02",
      "source_id": "knocker-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Cornish miners believed that the diminutive Knockers beckoned them toward finding rich veins of tin. As miners changed from independent, family-owned operators to hired laborers for large industrialized companies, there was an increased concern for safety, reflected in the knockers' new role. They knocked on the mine walls to warn of impending collapse.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penambang Cornwall (Cornish): knocker menuntun ke urat timah, mengetuk untuk memperingatkan runtuhan.",
        "en": "Cornish miners: knockers led them to tin and knocked to warn of collapse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knocker-folklore-c03",
      "source_id": "knocker-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Generally considered benevolent, they were also tricksters who would hide tools and extinguish candles. They are similar to the coblynau of Welsh miners.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Umumnya baik, tetapi usil: menyembunyikan perkakas, memadamkan lilin; mirip coblynau Wales (Welsh).",
        "en": "Generally benevolent but tricksters hiding tools and snuffing candles; like Welsh coblynau."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knocker-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "knocker-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "One interpretation holds that they are mine-spirits, believed to be the ghosts of the Jews who worked the mines in the 11th and 12th centuries; another view is that they are the spirits of those killed in a mine. To show appreciation, and to avoid future peril, the miners cast the last bite of their tasty pasties into the mines for the Knockers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Arwah orang Yahudi (Jews) penambang abad ke-11/12 atau korban tambang; penambang melempar gigitan pasty. (Jewish)",
        "en": "Ghosts of Jews who mined in the 11th–12th centuries or of mine victims; miners cast pasty bites for them. (Jewish)"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knocker-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "knocker-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "In the 1820s, immigrant Welsh and Cornish miners brought tales of the Tommyknockers and their theft of unwatched items and warning knocks to western Pennsylvania.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "1820-an: penambang Wales dan Cornwall (Welsh, Cornish) membawa kisah Tommyknocker ke Pennsylvania; di AS.",
        "en": "In the 1820s Welsh and Cornish miners brought Tommyknocker tales to Pennsylvania."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knocker-folklore-c06",
      "source_id": "knocker-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Der Name leitet sich von ihrer Angewohnheit ab, an die Wände von Bergwerken zu klopfen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama dari kebiasaan mengetuk (klopfen) dinding tambang (Bergwerken).",
        "en": "The name comes from knocking (klopfen) on mine (Bergwerken) walls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knocker-folklore-c07",
      "source_id": "knocker-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Knockers, auch Knacker, Bwca (walisisch), Bucca (kornisch), Coblyn (Wales), Tommyknockers (USA), ist der Name von Kobolden oder Berggeistern in Wales oder Cornwall.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Knockers (Knacker, Bwca, Bucca, Coblyn, Tommyknockers) kobold atau roh gunung (Berggeistern) di Wales atau Cornwall; Púca.",
        "en": "Knockers (Knacker, Bwca, Bucca, Coblyn, Tommyknockers) are kobolds or mountain spirits (Berggeistern) of Wales or Cornwall."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "knocker-folklore-c08",
      "source_id": "knocker-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Cornish miners, much sought after in the years following the gold and silver rushes, brought them to Colorado, Nevada, and California.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penambang Cornwall membawa kisah ke Colorado, Nevada, California (Amerika Barat).",
        "en": "Cornish miners brought them to Colorado, Nevada and California."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lambton Worm adalah legenda County Durham di Inggris Timur Laut tentang John Lambton dan cacing raksasa (naga) yang meneror desa-desa sekitar Sungai Wear.",
    "en": "The Lambton Worm is a legend of County Durham in North-East England about John Lambton and a giant worm (dragon) that terrorised the villages around the River Wear.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lambton-worm-c01",
      "lambton-worm-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lambton Worm adalah legenda dari County Durham di Inggris Timur Laut (North-East England), Britania Raya (United Kingdom). Kisahnya berlangsung di sekitar Sungai Wear dan telah diadaptasi dari tradisi tertulis dan lisan menjadi pantomim dan lagu. Ceritanya tentang John Lambton, pewaris Lambton Estate, dan pertarungannya dengan cacing raksasa (naga) yang meneror desa-desa setempat. John muda yang bandel membolos gereja pada hari Minggu untuk memancing di Sungai Wear dan menangkap makhluk kecil mirip belut atau lamprey dengan sembilan lubang di tiap sisi kepalanya yang mirip salamander, lalu membuangnya ke sebuah sumur.",
      "en": "The Lambton Worm is a legend of County Durham in North-East England, United Kingdom. Set around the River Wear, it has passed from written and oral tradition into pantomime and song. It tells of John Lambton, heir to the Lambton Estate, and his fight with a giant worm (dragon) that was terrorising the villages. As a rebellious youth John skipped church one Sunday to fish in the Wear and pulled out a little eel- or lamprey-like thing whose salamander head bore nine holes along each side, which he then dropped down a well.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lambton-worm-c01",
        "lambton-worm-c02",
        "lambton-worm-c03",
        "lambton-worm-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "John kemudian melupakannya dan, sebagai penebusan masa mudanya, ikut Perang Salib. Cacing itu tumbuh sangat besar, sumur menjadi beracun, dan ternak mulai hilang; cacing yang sudah dewasa keluar dari sumur dan melilit sebuah bukit, dalam kebanyakan versi sampai tujuh kali. Sepulangnya, John meminta nasihat seorang perempuan bijak atau penyihir dekat Durham, yang menyuruhnya melapisi baju zirahnya dengan mata tombak dan bertarung di Sungai Wear; setelah membunuh cacing itu ia harus membunuh makhluk hidup pertama yang dilihatnya, atau keluarganya dikutuk sembilan generasi tidak mati di ranjang. Menurut artikel de, legenda ini salah satu kisah naga Britania paling terkenal dan terperinci, dengan unsur keagamaan yang kuat.",
      "en": "John then forgets it and, as penance for his wild youth, joins the Crusades. The worm grows huge, the well turns poisonous and livestock start to vanish; the grown worm leaves the well and coils round a hill, in most versions seven times. Back home, John consults a wise woman or witch near Durham, who tells him to stud his armour with spearheads and fight in the River Wear; after killing the worm he must kill the first living thing he sees, or his family will be cursed for nine generations not to die in their beds. According to the de article the legend is one of the best-known and most detailed British dragon tales, marked by strong religious elements.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lambton-worm-c07",
        "lambton-worm-c04",
        "lambton-worm-c08",
        "lambton-worm-c05",
        "lambton-worm-c06"
      ]

    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c01",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s1",
      "quote": "The Lambton Worm is a legend from County Durham in North-East England in the United Kingdom. The story takes place around the River Wear, and is one of the area's pieces of folklore, having been adapted from written and oral tradition into pantomime and song formats.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lambton Worm legenda County Durham, Inggris Timur Laut (North-East England), Britania Raya (United Kingdom), Sungai Wear (River Wear); pantomim dan lagu.",
        "en": "The Lambton Worm is a legend of County Durham, North-East England, United Kingdom, set by the River Wear; adapted into pantomime and song."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c02",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s1",
      "quote": "The story revolves around John Lambton, an heir of the Lambton Estate, County Durham (in ceremonial Tyne and Wear), and his battle with a giant worm (dragon) that had been terrorising the local villages.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "John Lambton, pewaris Lambton Estate, melawan cacing raksasa (naga) peneror desa.",
        "en": "John Lambton, heir of the Lambton Estate, fights a giant worm (dragon) terrorising villages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c03",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s1",
      "quote": "The story states that the young John Lambton was a rebellious character who missed church one Sunday to go fishing in the River Wear. In many versions of the story, while walking to the river, or setting up his equipment, John receives warnings from an old man",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "John Lambton muda bandel, membolos gereja hari Minggu (Sunday) untuk memancing di Sungai Wear.",
        "en": "Young John Lambton missed church one Sunday to fish in the River Wear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c04",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s1",
      "quote": "Eventually, the worm grows extremely large and the well becomes poisonous. The villagers start to notice livestock going missing and discover that the fully-grown worm has emerged from the well and coiled itself around a local hill.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cacing tumbuh besar, sumur beracun, ternak hilang, cacing melilit bukit (Worm Hill, Fatfield).",
        "en": "The worm grows huge, the well turns poisonous, livestock vanish, the worm coils round a hill (Worm Hill, Fatfield) seven times."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c05",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s1",
      "quote": "She tells him to cover his armour in spearheads and fight the worm in the River Wear, where it now spends its days wrapped around a great rock. The witch also tells John that after killing the worm he must then kill the first living thing he sees, or else his family will be cursed for nine generations and will not die in their beds.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penyihir (dekat Durham): zirah bermata tombak, bertarung di Sungai Wear; bunuh makhluk pertama atau dikutuk sembilan generasi.",
        "en": "The witch (near Durham): spearhead-studded armour, fight in the Wear; kill the first living thing or nine generations are cursed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c06",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s2",
      "quote": "Die Sage ist eine der bekanntesten und detailreichsten britischen Drachensagen und wird in zahlreichen Märchenbüchern, einer Oper, einem Film und einem Volkslied behandelt. Auffallend im Vergleich zu anderen Drachensagen Großbritanniens sind die starken religiösen Elemente dieser Erzählung.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lindwurm von Lambton salah satu kisah naga Britania (britischen Drachensagen) paling terkenal, dengan unsur keagamaan kuat (religiösen Elemente).",
        "en": "The Lindwurm von Lambton is one of the best-known British dragon tales (britischen Drachensagen), with strong religious elements (religiösen Elemente)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c07",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s1",
      "quote": "John then forgets about the creature and eventually grows up. As a penance for his rebellious early years, he joins the Crusades.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "John melupakannya dan ikut Perang Salib (Crusades) sebagai penebusan.",
        "en": "John forgets it and joins the Crusades as penance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c08",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s1",
      "quote": "In most versions of the story, the worm is large enough to wrap itself around the hill seven times.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kebanyakan versi cacing melilit bukit tujuh kali.",
        "en": "In most versions the worm wraps itself around the hill seven times."

    {
      "id": "lambton-worm-c09",
      "source_id": "lambton-worm-s1",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Les Lavandières atau Midnight Washerwomen adalah tiga perempuan tua pencuci dalam mitologi Kelt yang pada tengah malam mencuci kain kafan orang yang akan meninggal.",
    "en": "Les Lavandières, the Midnight Washerwomen, are three old washerwomen of Celtic myth who at midnight wash the shrouds of those about to die.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "les-lavandieres-c01",
      "les-lavandieres-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Les Lavandières, atau Midnight Washerwomen, adalah tiga perempuan tua pencuci dalam mitologi Kelt (Celtic mythology). Namanya dalam bahasa-bahasa Kelt antara lain kannerezed noz di Brittany dan Bean nighe dalam bahasa Gaelik (Gaelic); mereka juga dikenal dalam cerita rakyat Kelt Iberia sebagai Las Lavanderas di Cantabria, As lavandeiras di Galicia, Les Llavanderes di Asturias, dan Bruxas lavadeiras di Portugal. Menurut mitos Brittany, ketiganya pergi ke tepi air pada tengah malam untuk mencuci kain kafan orang yang akan meninggal, atau menurut mitologi Kelt mencuci pakaian berdarah mereka; mereka mungkin berhubungan dengan dewi tiga kematian dan pembantaian.",
      "en": "Les Lavandières, the Midnight Washerwomen, are three old washerwomen of Celtic myth. Their Celtic names include kannerezed noz in Brittany and Bean nighe in Gaelic; Iberian Celtic folklore knows them as Las Lavanderas in Cantabria, As lavandeiras in Galicia, Les Llavanderes in Asturias and Bruxas lavadeiras in Portugal. In Breton lore the three go to the water at midnight to wash shrouds for the soon-to-die, or in Celtic myth the bloodied clothes of those about to die; they may be linked with the triple goddess of death and slaughter.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "les-lavandieres-c01",
        "les-lavandieres-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para pencuci ini kecil, berpakaian hijau, dan berkaki berselaput, mungkin sebab mereka juga disebut cannard noz (\"bebek malam\") dalam cerita rakyat Breton. Pada abad ke-19 kepercayaan ini sangat hidup di Brittany dan Normandy serta tercatat di banyak wilayah Prancis (France) lain, dan ditulis oleh Guy de Maupassant, Victor Hugo, hingga George Sand. Pencuci Breton tidak suka diganggu: mereka meminta pejalan membantu memeras kain, mematahkan lengan yang membantu dengan enggan dan menenggelamkan yang menolak. Menurut artikel fr, George Sand menyebut mereka arwah ibu pembunuh anak yang terkutuk mencuci sesuatu yang dari dekat ternyata mayat bayi.",
      "en": "The washerwomen are small, dressed in green and web-footed, which may be why Breton folklore also calls them cannard noz ('night ducks'). In the 19th century the belief was very much alive in Brittany and Normandy and attested in many other parts of France, and was written up by Guy de Maupassant, Victor Hugo and George Sand. Breton washerwomen hate being disturbed: they ask passers-by to help wring the linen, breaking the arms of those who help grudgingly and drowning those who refuse. According to the fr article George Sand called them the souls of infanticidal mothers, cursed to wash something that up close proves to be a child's corpse.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "les-lavandieres-c03",
        "les-lavandieres-c07",
        "les-lavandieres-c04",
        "les-lavandieres-c05",
        "les-lavandieres-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "id": "Nechtan adalah tokoh mitologi Irlandia yang dikaitkan dengan mata air sumber Sungai Boyne, Nechtan's Well atau Well of Wisdom, dan suami dewi Boann.",
    "en": "Nechtan is a figure of Irish myth tied to the spring at the source of the River Boyne, Nechtan's Well or the Well of Wisdom, and husband of the goddess Boann.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nechtan adalah tokoh mitologi Irlandia (Irish) yang dikaitkan dengan mata air penanda sumber Sungai Boyne (River Boyne), yang dikenal sebagai Nechtan's Well atau Well of Wisdom. Ia suami Boann, dewi yang namanya menjadi nama Sungai Boyne, dan diyakini nama lain bagi Nuada. Menurut Georges Dumézil, namanya mungkin sekerabat dengan dewa Romawi-Britania Nodens, dewa Romawi Neptunus, dan dewa Persia dan Weda Apam Napat. Nechtan sering dikaitkan dengan kuda, dan dalam bahasa Irlandia namanya dipakai bergantian dengan Elcmar.",
      "en": "Nechtan is an Irish mythological figure linked with the spring at the source of the River Boyne, called Nechtan's Well or the Well of Wisdom. He was husband of Boann, the goddess who gave the Boyne its name, and is thought to be another name for Nuada. Georges Dumézil suggested his name may be related to the Romano-British Nodens, the Roman Neptunus and the Persian and Vedic Apam Napat. Nechtan is often tied to horses, and in Irish his name is used interchangeably with Elcmar.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nechtan-c01",
        "nechtan-c02",
        "nechtan-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia tinggal di Síd Nechtain dunia lain, wujud mitos dari Carbury Hill. Hanya Nechtan dan tiga pembawa cawannya, Flesc, Lam, dan Luam, yang boleh mendatangi Tobar Segais atau \"Well of Wisdom\", tempat sembilan pohon hazel suci menjatuhkan buahnya yang membawa kebijaksanaan dan tempat Salmon of Wisdom berenang. Dalam Book of Invasions, Nechtan membunuh Cairpre lalu dibunuh oleh Sigmall, cucu Midir. Menurut artikel de, Boand menodai mata air rahasianya di síd dengan berjalan melawan arah matahari lalu tenggelam di dalamnya, dan dari situ lahir Sungai Boyne.",
      "en": "He dwelt in the otherworldly Síd Nechtain, the mythic form of Carbury Hill. Only Nechtan and his three cup-bearers Flesc, Lam and Luam could visit the Tobar Segais or 'Well of Wisdom', where nine sacred hazels dropped their wisdom-bearing nuts and the Salmon of Wisdom swam. In the Book of Invasions Nechtan killed Cairpre and was killed in turn by Sigmall, Midir's grandson. According to the de article Boand profaned his secret spring in the síd by walking against the sun and drowned in it, and so the Boyne was born.",

      "quote": "In the Book of Invasions Nechtan killed Cairpre and was in turn killed by Sigmall, the grandson of Midir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Book of Invasions: Nechtan membunuh Cairpre, dibunuh Sigmall cucu Midir.",
        "en": "Book of Invasions: Nechtan killed Cairpre and was killed by Sigmall, Midir's grandson."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nechtan-c06",
      "source_id": "nechtan-s2",
      "quote": "auch Elcmar [ 'elkvar] („Der Schreckliche“) ist eine Sagengestalt aus dem Mythologischen Zyklus der keltischen Mythologie Irlands. Sein Name weist wahrscheinlich auf eine alte Wassergottheit hin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nechtan (Elcmar, Der Schreckliche) tokoh Siklus Mitologi Irlandia (Irlands); kemungkinan dewa air (Wassergottheit).",
        "en": "Nechtan (Elcmar, Der Schreckliche) is a figure of the Irish (Irlands) Mythological Cycle; probably an old water deity (Wassergottheit)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nechtan-c07",
      "source_id": "nechtan-s2",
      "quote": "Diese habe die geheime Quelle Nechtans im Síd durch Herumgehen gegen den Sonnenlauf (tuaithbel) entweiht, worauf sie darin ertrunken sei. So sei der Fluss Boand (Boyne) entstanden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Boand (Boann) menodai mata air rahasia Nechtan di síd (Síd), tenggelam; lahir Sungai Boyne (Fluss Boand).",
        "en": "Boand (Boann) profaned Nechtan's secret spring in the síd, drowned, and the Boyne (Fluss Boand) arose."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nechtan-c08",
      "source_id": "nechtan-s1",
      "quote": "He inhabited the otherworldly Síd Nechtain, the mythological form of Carbury Hill.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tinggal di Síd Nechtain, wujud mitos Carbury Hill.",
        "en": "He lived in the otherworldly Síd Nechtain, mythic form of Carbury Hill."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Shishiga adalah makhluk perempuan penghuni rawa atau hutan dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, mirip roh hutan, yang mengganggu orang dan mendatangkan sial kepada pemabuk.",
    "en": "The shishiga is a female swamp or forest creature of Russian folklore, akin to the wood-goblin, that harasses people and brings misfortune to drunkards.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "shishiga-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Shishiga (Rusia: шишига, juga leshenka) adalah makhluk perempuan penghuni rawa atau hutan dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi Rusia (Russian), mirip roh hutan. Ia digambarkan putih, telanjang, dan berambut kusut, dan dianggap mengganggu orang serta mendatangkan sial kepada pemabuk. Makhluk yang sama penting dalam mitologi bangsa Komi: ia konon tinggal di Sungai Kama dan sering naik ke tepi untuk menyisir rambutnya, dan siapa pun yang melihatnya akan segera tenggelam atau mati karena sebab lain.",
      "en": "The shishiga (Russian шишига, also leshenka) is a female swamp or forest being of Russian folklore and myth, similar to the wood-goblin. She is pictured white, naked and dishevelled, and is held to pester people and bring bad luck to drunkards. The same creature matters in Komi mythology: she is said to live in the Kama River and often comes ashore to comb her hair, and anyone who sees her soon drowns or dies some other way.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shishiga-c01",
        "shishiga-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel fr, chichiga digambarkan sebagai makhluk perempuan kecil bungkuk yang tinggal di antara alang-alang, lebih suka anak sungai kecil dan kolam, tidur sepanjang siang dan hanya muncul saat senja; ia juga membawa pergi anak-anak yang terlalu dekat dengan air deras. Menurut artikel ru, dalam mitologi Slavia Timur kata шишига dipakai untuk berbagai roh jahat yang tinggal di balik tungku, di hutan, di rawa, atau di pemandian, dan di Simbirsk setan disebut Shishiga.",
      "en": "According to the fr article the chichiga is a small hunchbacked female creature living among reeds, preferring little streams and ponds, sleeping through the afternoon and appearing only at dusk; she also carries off children who come too close to rushing water. According to the ru article, in East Slavic myth шишига names various unclean spirits living behind the stove, in the forest, the swamp or the bathhouse, and in Simbirsk province the devil was called Shishiga.",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Shishiga (шишига, leshenka) makhluk perempuan rawa atau hutan cerita rakyat Rusia (Russian), mirip roh hutan; putih, telanjang, kusut; mengganggu pemabuk; di Rusia.",

      "id": "shishiga-c02",
      "source_id": "shishiga-s1",
      "quote": "The same creature is important in the mythology of Komi people. She supposedly lives in the Kama River and often goes to the shore to comb her hair. Everybody who sees her will soon drown or die by another cause.",

      "source_id": "shishiga-s3",
      "quote": "Шиши́га, шишо́к (муж. шиш, шишиган, шишимора-кикимора) — в восточнославянской мифологии нечистая сила, живущая за печкой, в лесу (лешачиха, леший) и на болоте (болотник), в бане (банник), в овине (овинный домовой); бес.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",

        "en": "Per the ru article, in East Slavic (восточнославянской) myth шишига is an unclean spirit behind the stove, in the forest (леший), swamp or bathhouse; шишимора-кикимора."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ukobach adalah iblis tingkat rendah dalam Dictionnaire Infernal karya Jacques Collin de Plancy, digambarkan bertubuh menyala merah dan dianggap penemu kembang api serta seni menggoreng.",
    "en": "Ukobach is a lower-order demon in Jacques Collin de Plancy's Dictionnaire Infernal, shown with a flaming red body and said to have invented fireworks and frying.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ukobach-c01",
      "ukobach-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ukobach termasuk dalam daftar iblis Dictionnaire Infernal, buku demonologi karya Jacques Collin de Plancy yang pertama terbit pada 1818 dan menggambarkan iblis-iblis dalam hierarki. Edisi paling terkenal buku itu tahun 1863 memuat 69 ilustrasi karya Louis Le Breton tentang rupa beberapa iblis. Menurut artikel es, dalam Dictionnaire Infernal Ukobach atau Urobach digambarkan sebagai iblis tingkat rendah yang selalu tampil dengan tubuh menyala merah, bermata dan bertelinga besar, dan sering membawa panci penuh bara atau besi panas.",
      "en": "Ukobach appears among the demons of the Dictionnaire Infernal, a demonology by Jacques Collin de Plancy first published in 1818 that sets out demons in hierarchies. Its best-known edition, of 1863, carried 69 illustrations by Louis Le Breton showing how several demons looked. According to the es article, the Dictionnaire Infernal describes Ukobach or Urobach as a lesser demon always shown with a flaming red body, big eyes and ears, and often a pan of embers or hot iron.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ukobach-c01",
        "ukobach-c02",
        "ukobach-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel es, ia dikatakan penemu kembang api dan seni menggoreng makanan, dan ditugasi Beelzebub menjaga \"minyak di kuali-kuali neraka\". Collin de Plancy sendiri mula-mula, karena pengaruh Voltaire, tidak percaya takhayul. Menurut artikel ja, ilustrasi Louis Le Breton dalam edisi ke-6 menggambarkannya berkepala besar, berekor kurus seperti sapi, dan memegang semacam wajan menyala.",
      "en": "According to the es article he is said to have invented fireworks and the art of frying food, and was charged by Beelzebub with keeping the 'oil in the infernal cauldrons'. Collin de Plancy himself, under Voltaire's influence, at first disbelieved superstition. According to the ja article, Louis Le Breton's illustration in the sixth edition gives him a big head, a thin cow-like tail and something like a blazing frying pan.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ukobach-c04",
        "ukobach-c05",
        "ukobach-c06"
      ]
    }

      "title": "ウコバク",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ja)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ja",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ukobach-c01",
      "source_id": "ukobach-s1",
      "quote": "The Dictionnaire Infernal (English: \"Infernal Dictionary\" ) is a book on demonology, describing demons organised in hierarchies. It was written by Jacques Collin de Plancy and first published in 1818.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dictionnaire Infernal (Infernal Dictionary) buku demonologi karya Jacques Collin de Plancy, terbit 1818; memuat Ukobach; di Prancis.",
        "en": "The Dictionnaire Infernal is a demonology by Jacques Collin de Plancy, first published in 1818, listing Ukobach."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ukobach-c02",
      "source_id": "ukobach-s1",
      "quote": "perhaps the most famous is the 1863 edition, which included 69 illustrations by Louis Le Breton depicting the appearances of several of the demons.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Edisi 1863 memuat 69 ilustrasi Louis Le Breton.",
        "en": "The 1863 edition had 69 illustrations by Louis Le Breton."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ukobach-c03",
      "source_id": "ukobach-s2",
      "quote": "En el Dictionnaire Infernal de Jacques Auguste Simon Collin de Plancy, 181 , Ukobach o Urobach es descrito como un demonio de orden inferior. Siempre se muestra con un cuerpo llameante de color rojo, grandes ojos y oídos, y a menudo una cacerola llena de brasas o hierro caliente.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Dictionnaire Infernal (Jacques Auguste Simon Collin de Plancy), Ukobach atau Urobach iblis tingkat rendah (demonio de orden inferior), tubuh menyala merah, mata dan telinga besar, panci bara.",
        "en": "In the Dictionnaire Infernal (Jacques Auguste Simon Collin de Plancy) Ukobach or Urobach is a lower-order demon (demonio de orden inferior) with a flaming red body, big eyes and ears, a pan of embers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ukobach-c04",
      "source_id": "ukobach-s2",
      "quote": "Se dice que es el inventor de los fuegos artificiales y del arte de freír los alimentos. Está encargado por Belcebú para mantener el \"aceite en las calderas infernales\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penemu kembang api (fuegos artificiales) dan seni menggoreng; ditugasi Beelzebub (Belcebú) menjaga minyak kuali neraka (calderas infernales).",
        "en": "Inventor of fireworks (fuegos artificiales) and frying; charged by Beelzebub (Belcebú) with the oil of the infernal cauldrons (calderas infernales)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ukobach-c05",
      "source_id": "ukobach-s1",
      "quote": "Influenced by Voltaire, Collin de Plancy initially did not believe in superstition.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dipengaruhi Voltaire, Collin de Plancy mula-mula tak percaya takhayul.",
        "en": "Influenced by Voltaire, Collin de Plancy at first disbelieved superstition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ukobach-c06",
      "source_id": "ukobach-s3",
      "quote": "『地獄の辞典』第6版で追加されたルイ・ル・ブルトンによる挿絵では、大きな頭に細い牛のような尾を持ち、手に燃え盛るフライパンのようなものを持った姿で描かれている。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel ja, ilustrasi Louis Le Breton (ルイ・ル・ブルトン) dalam edisi ke-6 (第6版) Dictionnaire Infernal (地獄の辞典) menggambarkannya berkepala besar, berekor kurus seperti sapi, memegang semacam wajan menyala.",
        "en": "Per the ja article, Louis Le Breton's (ルイ・ル・ブルトン) illustration in the 6th edition (第6版) of the Dictionnaire Infernal (地獄の辞典) shows a big head, a thin cow-like tail and a blazing frying-pan-like object."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bean-nighe (\"perempuan pencuci\") adalah roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia, pertanda kematian yang mencuci pakaian orang yang akan meninggal di sungai sepi.",
    "en": "The bean-nighe ('washerwoman') is a female spirit of Scottish folklore, an omen of death who washes the clothes of the soon-to-die in lonely streams.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bean-nighe-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bean-nighe (Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic untuk \"perempuan pencuci\") adalah roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia (Scottish folklore), dianggap pertanda kematian dan utusan dari Dunia Lain (Otherworld). Ia sejenis ban-sìth (Irlandia/Irish: bean sídhe, dianglisisasi banshee) yang menghantui sungai sepi dan mencuci pakaian orang yang akan meninggal; Les Lavandières adalah sebutan Prancis (French) untuk para pencuci malam ini. Ia juga disebut nigheag, \"pencuci kecil\", nigheag na h-ath, \"pencuci kecil di arungan\", dan nigheag bheag a bhroin, \"pencuci kecil kesedihan\".",
      "en": "The bean-nighe (Scottish Gaelic for 'washerwoman') is a female spirit of Scottish folklore, held to be an omen of death and a messenger of the Otherworld. She is a kind of ban-sìth (Irish bean sídhe, anglicised banshee) haunting lonely streams and washing the clothes of the soon-to-die; Les Lavandières is the French name for these night washerwomen. Other names for her are nigheag ('little washer'), nigheag na h-ath (the little washer at the ford) and nigheag bheag a bhroin (the little washer of sorrow).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bean-nighe-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia terlihat di tempat sepi di tepi sungai atau kolam, mencuci darah dari linen dan kain kubur orang yang akan meninggal; sifatnya berbeda menurut daerah, dan ada yang menyebutnya dapat memberi pengetahuan atau mengabulkan permintaan bila didekati dengan hati-hati. Mnathan-nighe konon arwah perempuan yang meninggal saat melahirkan dan harus bekerja sampai hari ajal mereka semestinya tiba. Di Mull dan Tiree, orang yang mendekatinya dari belakang dan mengaku anak susuannya akan diberi pengetahuan yang diinginkan. Menurut artikel de, ban-sìth dan bean sídhe berasal dari bahasa Irlandia Kuno ben síde, \"perempuan peri\".",
      "en": "She is seen in lonely spots beside a stream or pool washing blood from the linen and grave-clothes of those about to die; her traits vary by place, and some traditions say she can impart knowledge or grant wishes if approached with care. Mnathan-nighe are said to be spirits of women who died in childbirth, bound to their task until the day they would have died naturally. On Mull and Tiree, one who approaches her from behind and claims to be her foster-child will be told whatever he wishes to know. According to the de article ban-sìth and bean sídhe come from Old Irish ben síde, 'woman of the fairies'.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "bean-nighe-c03",
        "bean-nighe-c04",
        "bean-nighe-c06",
        "bean-nighe-c05"

      "id": "bean-nighe-c03",
      "source_id": "bean-nighe-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that mnathan-nighe (the plural of bean-nighe ) are the spirits of women who died giving birth and are doomed to perform their tasks until the day their lives would have normally ended.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Mnathan-nighe are spirits of women who died in childbirth."

    },
    {
      "id": "bean-nighe-c04",
      "source_id": "bean-nighe-s1",
      "quote": "On the Isles of Mull and Tiree she was said to have unusually long breasts that interfere with her washing so she throws them over her shoulders and lets them hang down her back. Those who see her must not turn away, but quietly approach from behind so that she is not aware.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "On Mull and Tiree one approaches her quietly from behind."

      "source_id": "bean-nighe-s2",
      "quote": "Sowohl das irische bean sídhe als auch das schottisch-gälische ban-sìth (beide bedeuten „Frau der Sídhe“ - Feenfrau oder Friedensfrau) stammen aus dem Altirischen ben síde mit der Bedeutung „Frau der Feen“",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bean sídhe (irische) dan ban-sìth (schottisch-gälische) dari Irlandia Kuno (Altirischen) ben síde, perempuan peri (Frau der Feen).",
        "en": "Irish (irische) bean sídhe and Scottish Gaelic ban-sìth come from Old Irish (Altirischen) ben síde, woman of the fairies (Frau der Feen)."
      }

      "id": "bean-nighe-c06",
      "source_id": "bean-nighe-s1",
      "quote": "He should then take hold of one of her breasts, put it in his mouth, and claim to be her foster-child (see Milk kinship). She will then impart to him whatever knowledge he desires.",

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Beast of Dean adalah hewan cerita rakyat Inggris yang konon hidup di Forest of Dean, Gloucestershire, dan diburu pada awal abad ke-19 sebagai babi hutan raksasa.",
    "en": "The Beast of Dean is an animal of English folklore said to live in the Forest of Dean, Gloucestershire, hunted in the early 19th century as a giant boar.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "beast-of-dean-c01",
      "beast-of-dean-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Beast of Dean adalah nama hewan dari cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore) yang konon hidup, atau pernah hidup, di Forest of Dean, hutan kuno yang luas yang dibatasi Sungai Severn dan Wye di Gloucestershire, Inggris (England). Meski ada beberapa upaya menemui, menangkap, atau membunuhnya pada awal abad ke-19, tidak ada bukti ilmiah keberadaannya. Kisah-kisahnya tampaknya berasal dari abad ke-18 atau awal abad ke-19.",
      "en": "The Beast of Dean is an animal of English folklore said to live, or once to have lived, in the Forest of Dean, a large ancient woodland between the rivers Severn and Wye in Gloucestershire, England. Despite several early 19th-century attempts to find, capture or kill it, no scientific evidence of it has been found. The tales seem to date from the 18th or early 19th century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "beast-of-dean-c01",
        "beast-of-dean-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada 1802 para petani desa Parkend melakukan ekspedisi untuk menangkap dan membunuhnya, tetapi tidak menemukan apa pun; hewan yang mereka buru dilaporkan babi hutan yang cukup besar untuk merobohkan pohon dan pagar tanaman. Babi hutan memang masih dapat ditemukan di Forest of Dean hingga kini, tetapi banyak rujukan abad ke-20 dan ke-21 berasal dari para kriptozoolog. Pada 2007 serial televisi Primeval menampilkannya kembali sebagai gorgonopsid. Menurut artikel bg, ia juga dikenal sebagai \"moose-pig\".",
      "en": "In 1802 farmers from the village of Parkend set out to catch and kill it but found nothing; the animal they sought was said to be a boar big enough to fell trees and hedges. Wild boar still live in the Forest of Dean, but many 20th- and 21st-century references come from cryptozoologists. In 2007 the TV series Primeval reimagined it as a gorgonopsid. According to the bg article it is also known as the 'moose-pig'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "beast-of-dean-c03",
        "beast-of-dean-c04",
        "beast-of-dean-c05",
        "beast-of-dean-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "beast-of-dean-c01",
      "source_id": "beast-of-dean-s1",
      "quote": "The Beast of Dean is the name of an animal from English folklore that is said to live, or to have once lived, in the Forest of Dean: a large, \"ancient woodland\" bounded by the rivers Severn and Wye in Gloucestershire, England. Despite several attempts to encounter, capture, or kill the beast in the early 19th century, no scientific evidence has been found",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beast of Dean hewan cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore) di Forest of Dean, Severn dan Wye, Gloucestershire, Inggris (England); tanpa bukti ilmiah; di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "The Beast of Dean is an English folklore animal of the Forest of Dean between the Severn and Wye, Gloucestershire, England; no scientific evidence."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beast-of-dean-c02",
      "source_id": "beast-of-dean-s1",
      "quote": "Folktales alleging the beast existed in the forest appear to have their origins in the 18th or early 19th century.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya berasal dari abad ke-18 atau awal abad ke-19.",
        "en": "The tales date from the 18th or early 19th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beast-of-dean-c03",
      "source_id": "beast-of-dean-s1",
      "quote": "Farmers from the village of Parkend undertook an expedition to capture and kill the creature in 1802 but did not find anything resembling the creature. The animal they were hunting was reported to be a boar large enough to fell trees and hedges.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "1802: petani Parkend berburu; babi hutan yang merobohkan pohon dan pagar.",
        "en": "1802: Parkend farmers hunted a boar large enough to fell trees and hedges."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beast-of-dean-c04",
      "source_id": "beast-of-dean-s1",
      "quote": "Wild boar can still be found in the Forest of Dean to this day, but many 20th and early 21st Century references to the creature are made by \"cryptozoologists\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Babi hutan masih ada; rujukan abad ke-20/21 dari kriptozoolog.",
        "en": "Wild boar still live there; 20th–21st Century references come from cryptozoologists."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beast-of-dean-c05",
      "source_id": "beast-of-dean-s1",
      "quote": "In 2007, the science-fiction television series Primeval, re-imagined the Beast of Dean as a living gorgonopsid that arrived in the present day through a wormhole leading to the Permian Period.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "2007: serial Primeval menampilkannya sebagai gorgonopsid.",
        "en": "2007: the series Primeval reimagined it as a gorgonopsid."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beast-of-dean-c06",
      "source_id": "beast-of-dean-s2",
      "quote": "Чудовището от Дийн или както още е известно \"moose-pig\" е криптид, за който се предполага, че живее в гората Дийн във Великобритания.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bg, Чудовището от Дийн (moose-pig) kriptid (криптид) di hutan Dean, Britania Raya (Великобритания).",
        "en": "Per the bg article, Чудовището от Дийн (moose-pig) is a cryptid (криптид) of the Forest of Dean, Great Britain (Великобритания)."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cŵn Annwn (\"anjing-anjing Annwn\") adalah anjing hantu dari Annwn, dunia lain mitologi Wales, yang dikaitkan dengan Perburuan Liar dan pertanda kematian.",
    "en": "The Cŵn Annwn ('hounds of Annwn') are spectral hounds of Annwn, the Welsh otherworld, linked with the Wild Hunt and seen as death omens.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cwn-annwn-c01",
      "cwn-annwn-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Wales (Welsh), Cŵn Annwn (\"anjing-anjing Annwn\"; tunggal Ci Annwn) adalah anjing hantu dari Annwn, dunia lain Wales. Mereka dikaitkan dengan sejenis Perburuan Liar (Wild Hunt) yang dipimpin Arawn, raja Annwn dalam Pwyll Pendefig Dyfed (Cabang Pertama Mabinogi), atau Gwyn ap Nudd dalam tradisi abad pertengahan kemudian. Di Wales mereka dikaitkan dengan angsa yang bermigrasi, konon karena suara angsa di malam hari mirip gonggongan anjing.",
      "en": "In Welsh myth and folklore the Cŵn Annwn ('hounds of Annwn'; singular Ci Annwn) were the ghostly hounds of Annwn, the Welsh otherworld. They are tied to a form of the Wild Hunt led either by Arawn, king of Annwn in Pwyll Pendefig Dyfed (the First Branch of the Mabinogi), or by Gwyn ap Nudd in later medieval lore. In Wales they were linked with migrating geese, whose night honking sounds like barking.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cwn-annwn-c01",
        "cwn-annwn-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Wales, geraman mereka paling keras saat masih jauh dan makin lirih saat mendekat, dan kedatangan mereka umumnya pertanda kematian. Annwn dalam tradisi Wales adalah tempat kelimpahan dan awet muda, bukan tempat hukuman, tetapi orang Kristen menyebut mereka \"Hounds of Hell\" dan menganggapnya milik Setan. Mereka kadang ditemani nenek menakutkan bernama Mallt-y-Nos, \"Matilda Malam\", dan juga disebut Cŵn Mamau (\"Anjing para Ibu\"). Menurut artikel de, anjing-anjing ini bertelinga merah dan bertubuh putih seperti hantu, dan salah satunya, Drudwyn, menangkap babi hutan Twrch Trwyth di bawah Mabon ap Modron.",
      "en": "Welsh folklore says their growl is loudest when far off and softer as they come near, and their coming is usually a death portent. The Annwn of Welsh tradition is a place of plenty and eternal youth, not punishment, yet Christians called them the 'Hounds of Hell' and took them to be Satan's. They are sometimes accompanied by a fearsome crone, Mallt-y-Nos, 'Matilda of the Night', and are also called Cŵn Mamau ('Hounds of the Mothers'). According to the de article these hounds have red ears and white, ghostly bodies, and one of them, Drudwyn, caught the boar Twrch Trwyth under Mabon ap Modron.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Donn Cúailnge, Banteng Cokelat dari Cooley, adalah banteng pejantan yang sangat subur dalam Siklus Ulster yang diperebutkan dalam Táin Bó Cúailnge.",
    "en": "Donn Cúailnge, the Brown Bull of Cooley, is an extremely fertile stud bull of the Ulster Cycle over whom the Táin Bó Cúailnge was fought.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam Siklus Ulster (Ulster Cycle) mitologi Irlandia (Irish), Donn Cúailnge, Banteng Cokelat dari Cooley (Brown Bull of Cooley), adalah banteng pejantan yang sangat subur yang menjadi pokok perebutan dalam Táin Bó Cúailnge (Cattle Raid of Cooley). Sebuah prakisah abad ke-9 menuturkan bahwa penyair Senchán Torpéist mengumpulkan para penyair Irlandia, tetapi tak seorang pun tahu kisah Táin secara utuh, hingga hantu Fergus mac Róich muncul kepada putranya Muirgen dan menceritakannya.",
      "en": "In the Ulster Cycle of Irish myth Donn Cúailnge, the Brown Bull of Cooley, was a hugely fertile stud bull and the prize fought over in the Táin Bó Cúailnge (Cattle Raid of Cooley). A ninth-century foretale says the poet Senchán Torpéist gathered Ireland's poets, yet none knew the whole Táin, until the ghost of Fergus mac Róich appeared to his son Muirgen and told it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "donn-cuailnge-c01",
        "donn-cuailnge-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia semula seorang lelaki bernama Friuch, penggembala babi milik Bodb Dearg, raja síd Munster, yang berselisih dengan Rucht, penggembala babi raja síd Connacht. Keduanya bertarung sambil berubah ke berbagai wujud hewan dan manusia, akhirnya menjadi dua cacing yang ditelan dua sapi dan lahir kembali sebagai dua banteng, Donn Cuailnge dan Finnbhennach (\"Bertanduk Putih\"). Ketika Medb mendapati bahwa Finnbhennach membuat suaminya Ailill lebih kaya darinya, ia bertekad memiliki Donn Cuailnge dan, setelah Dáire membatalkan kesepakatan, mengerahkan pasukan menyerbu Ulster. Menurut artikel fr, banteng ini memiliki kecerdasan seperti manusia.",
      "en": "He was first a man named Friuch, swineherd to Bodb Dearg, king of the Munster síd, who quarrelled with Rucht, swineherd to the king of the Connacht síd. They fought through many animal and human shapes, until, as two worms, each was eaten by a cow and born again as a bull: Donn Cuailnge and Finnbhennach ('White-horned'). When Medb found that owning Finnbhennach made her husband Ailill richer than her, she set out to get Donn Cuailnge and, after Dáire backed out of the deal, raised an army against Ulster. According to the fr article the bull had human-like intelligence.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "donn-cuailnge-c03",
        "donn-cuailnge-c04",
        "donn-cuailnge-c07",
        "donn-cuailnge-c06"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "In the Ulster Cycle of Irish mythology Donn Cúailnge, the Brown Bull of Cooley, was an extremely fertile stud bull over whom the Táin Bó Cúailnge (Cattle Raid of Cooley) was fought.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Siklus Ulster (Ulster Cycle) mitologi Irlandia (Irish), Donn Cúailnge, Brown Bull of Cooley, banteng pejantan subur, pokok Táin Bó Cúailnge (Cattle Raid of Cooley); di Irlandia. Banteng Cokelat dari Cooley.",
        "en": "In the Ulster Cycle of Irish myth Donn Cúailnge, the Brown Bull of Cooley, was a fertile stud bull fought over in the Táin Bó Cúailnge (Cattle Raid of Cooley)."

    {
      "id": "donn-cuailnge-c02",
      "source_id": "donn-cuailnge-s1",
      "quote": "In the 6th century, the poet Senchán Torpéist gathered the poets of Ireland together to see if any of them knew the story of the Táin Bó Cúailnge, but they all only knew parts of it.       "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Senchán Torpéist mengumpulkan penyair Irlandia (Ireland); hantu Fergus mac Róich menceritakan Táin kepada Muirgen.",
        "en": "Senchán Torpéist gathered Ireland's poets; Fergus mac Róich's ghost told the Táin to Muirgen."

    },
    {
      "id": "donn-cuailnge-c03",
      "source_id": "donn-cuailnge-s1",
      "quote": "He was originally a man named Friuch, a pig-keeper, who worked for Bodb Dearg, king of the Munster sidh.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Semula Friuch, penggembala babi Bodb Dearg (Munster); bertarung dengan Rucht (Connaught) dalam berbagai wujud, jadi cacing, lahir kembali sebagai Donn Cuailnge dan Finnbhennach (White-horned). Connacht; Bertanduk Putih.",
        "en": "Once Friuch, swineherd of Bodb Dearg (Munster); fought Rucht (Connaught) through many forms, became worms, reborn as Donn Cuailnge and Finnbhennach (White-horned)."
      }
    },
    {

      "source_id": "donn-cuailnge-s1",

      "statement": {

      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    "id": "Glashtyn adalah makhluk legenda cerita rakyat Manx, goblin yang keluar dari air atau berwujud kuda muda, kadang disamakan dengan kuda air cabbyl-ushtey.",
    "en": "The glashtyn is a legendary creature of Manx folklore, a goblin that comes out of the water or takes the shape of a colt, sometimes equated with the water horse cabbyl-ushtey.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Glashtyn (Inggris Manx/Manx English: glashtin, glashtan, atau glashan; Manx: glashtin atau glashtyn) adalah makhluk legenda dari cerita rakyat Manx. Glashtin dikatakan goblin yang keluar dari habitat airnya untuk berhubungan dengan penduduk pulau; yang lain mengatakan ia berwujud kuda muda, atau menyamakannya dengan kuda air setempat cabbyl-ushtey, dan satu sumber menyebutnya banteng air (tarroo-ushtey), setengah sapi setengah kuda. Kata glashtin diduga berasal dari kata Kelt (Celtic) glais, \"sungai kecil\" atau kadang laut.",
      "en": "The glashtyn (Manx English glashtin, glashtan or glashan; Manx glashtin or glashtyn) is a legendary being of Manx folklore. The glashtin is said to be a goblin that leaves its watery home to mix with the islanders; others say it takes the form of a colt, or equate it with the local water horse cabbyl-ushtey, and one source makes it a water-bull (tarroo-ushtey), half cow and half horse. The word glashtin is thought to come from Celtic glais, 'stream', or at times the sea.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "glashtyn-c01",
        "glashtyn-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Beberapa kisah menuturkan ia mengejar perempuan, yang lolos dengan memotong ujung roknya; dalam satu versi modern perempuan itu selamat berkat kokok ayam jantan, dan glashtin yang menyamar sebagai lelaki tampan terbongkar oleh telinga kudanya. Kamus Moore (1924) mengartikan glashan sebagai \"goblin berbulu\". Menurut artikel fr, Alastyn atau Glashtyn hanya keluar pada malam hari dan dapat berwujud anak kuda kelabu; dalam wujud manusia ia pemuda sangat tampan bermata berkilau, berkulit gelap, dan berambut ikal, hanya dapat dikenali dari telinganya yang runcing seperti telinga kuda.",
      "en": "Some tales tell of it chasing women, who escape by cutting free the hem of their skirts; in one modern version the woman is saved by a cock's crowing, and the glashtin, posing as a handsome man, is betrayed by his horse's ears. Moore's dictionary (1924) glosses glashan as 'hairy goblin'. According to the fr article the Alastyn or Glashtyn comes out only at night and can take the shape of a grey colt; in human form it is a very handsome young man with shining eyes, dark skin and curly hair, known only by its ears, pointed like a horse's.",

        "glashtyn-c05",

    {
      "id": "glashtyn-c05",
      "source_id": "glashtyn-s2",
      "quote": "L’Alastyn, aussi connu sous les noms de Glashtyn et de Cabyll-ushtey, est, dans le folklore de l'île de Man, une créature humanoïde qui sort régulièrement de l'eau et peut prendre l'apparence d'un poulain gris. Il ne sort que la nuit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alastyn (Glashtyn, Cabyll-ushtey) makhluk humanoid folklore Isle of Man (île de Man), keluar dari air, berwujud anak kuda kelabu (poulain gris), keluar malam (nuit).",
        "en": "The Alastyn (Glashtyn, Cabyll-ushtey) is a humanoid of Isle of Man (île de Man) folklore that leaves the water, takes the form of a grey colt (poulain gris), and comes out only at night (nuit)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "glashtyn-c06",
      "source_id": "glashtyn-s2",
      "quote": "Sous sa forme humaine, il apparait comme un très beau jeune homme avec des yeux brillants, une peau sombre et des cheveux bouclés. Il ne peut être distingué des êtres humains que par ses oreilles, qui restent pointues comme celles d'un cheval.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wujud manusia: pemuda tampan, mata berkilau, kulit gelap, rambut ikal; telinga runcing seperti kuda (cheval).",
        "en": "In human form a handsome youth with shining eyes, dark skin and curly hair; ears pointed like a horse's (cheval)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Oilliphéist adalah ular laut atau monster mirip naga dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Irlandia, yang konon mengukir alur Sungai Shannon.",
    "en": "The Oilliphéist is a sea serpent or dragon-like monster of Irish myth and folklore said to have carved the course of the River Shannon.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "oillipheist-c01",
      "oillipheist-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Oilliphéist (dari oll \"besar\" dan péist \"cacing, binatang ajaib, monster, reptil\") adalah ular laut atau monster mirip naga dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Irlandia (Irish). Monster-monster ini diyakini menghuni banyak danau dan sungai di Irlandia (Ireland), dan ada banyak legenda tentang orang suci dan pahlawan yang melawan mereka. Dalam satu kisah, Sionnan, cucu Manannán mac Lir, membuat marah Salmon of Knowledge dengan melemparinya batu, dan ikan itu memanggil seekor Oilliphéist yang akhirnya membunuhnya.",
      "en": "Its name joining oll, 'great', to péist, a word for worm, beast or monster, the Oilliphéist is a dragon-like sea serpent of Irish legend. Such monsters were believed to live in many Irish lakes and rivers, and many legends tell of saints and heroes fighting them. One tale tells how Sionnan, a granddaughter of Manannán mac Lir, pelted the Salmon of Knowledge with stones; in revenge the fish summoned an Oilliphéist, which killed her.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "oillipheist-c01",
        "oillipheist-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah lain, seekor Oilliphéist mengukir alur Sungai Shannon dan danau-danau di dekatnya ketika mendengar bahwa Santo Patrick datang untuk mengusirnya: ia berangkat dari Shannon Pot, membentuk Lough Allen, dan melahap domba serta sapi penduduk. Penduduk dekat Athleague melawannya dipimpin peniup pipa mabuk bernama O'Rourke, yang ditelan monster itu tetapi terus bermain pipa di dalam perutnya selama berhari-hari hingga dimuntahkan. Menurut artikel pt, Ollphéist adalah sekelompok monster laut yang kadang digambarkan bercirikan naga, ular, atau serangga, di selatan disebut Ollpiasta, dan kadang dapat terbang atau berbicara.",
      "en": "In another tale an Oilliphéist carves the course of the Shannon and nearby lakes on hearing that Saint Patrick has come to drive it out: setting off from Shannon Pot, it forms Lough Allen and devours the locals' sheep and cattle. People near Athleague stand against it led by a drunken piper named O'Rourke, whom it swallows; he keeps piping in its stomach for days until it coughs him up. According to the pt article Ollphéist names a group of sea monsters sometimes given dragon, snake or even insect traits, called Ollpiasta in the south, and at times able to fly or speak.",
