    {
      "id": "oillipheist-c01",
      "source_id": "oillipheist-s1",
      "quote": "'great' and péist 'worm, fabulous beast, monster, reptile') is a sea serpent or dragon-like monster in Irish mythology and folklore.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Oilliphéist (oll besar, péist cacing/monster) ular laut atau monster mirip naga mitologi Irlandia (Irish); di Irlandia.",
        "en": "The Oilliphéist (oll great, péist worm/monster) is a sea serpent or dragon-like monster of Irish mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oillipheist-c02",
      "source_id": "oillipheist-s1",
      "quote": "These monsters were believed to inhabit many lakes and rivers in Ireland and there are many legends of saints and heroes fighting them. One story has a girl named Sionnan, grand daughter of Manannán mac Lir, angered the Salmon of Knowledge by throwing stones at it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menghuni danau dan sungai Irlandia (Ireland); Sionnan cucu Manannán mac Lir membuat marah Salmon of Knowledge.",
        "en": "They live in Irish lakes and rivers; Sionnan, granddaughter of Manannán mac Lir, angered the Salmon of Knowledge."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oillipheist-c03",
      "source_id": "oillipheist-s1",
      "quote": "In one story, an Oilliphéist cuts the route of the River Shannon (as well as adjacent lakes) when it hears that Saint Patrick has come to drive out it and its kind. It started its journey in Shannon Pot, then carved out Lough Allen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Oilliphéist mengukir Sungai Shannon (River Shannon) saat Santo Patrick (Saint Patrick) datang; dari Shannon Pot, membentuk Lough Allen.",
        "en": "An Oilliphéist carves the River Shannon when Saint Patrick comes; from Shannon Pot it carves Lough Allen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oillipheist-c04",
      "source_id": "oillipheist-s1",
      "quote": "The monster swallows O'Rourke but he survives, continuing to play the pipe inside the Oilliphéist's stomach for several days. The monster becomes so annoyed with Ó Ruairc's music that it coughs him up and spits him out.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menelan O'Rourke (Ó Ruairc) yang terus bermain pipa di perutnya, lalu memuntahkannya.",
        "en": "It swallows O'Rourke (Ó Ruairc), who keeps piping in its stomach, then spits him out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oillipheist-c05",
      "source_id": "oillipheist-s2",
      "quote": "Ollphéist é um grupo de monstros marinhos, por vezes descritos  com características de dragões, cobras ou mesmo de insectos, referidos como monstros nas lendas e folclore gaélicos. Chamam-se 'Ollpiasta' no sul.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ollphéist sekelompok monster laut (monstros marinhos) bercirikan naga, ular, serangga, dalam folklore Gaelik (gaélicos); di selatan Ollpiasta.",
        "en": "Ollphéist is a group of sea monsters (monstros marinhos) with dragon, snake or insect traits in Gaelic (gaélicos) lore; Ollpiasta in the south."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oillipheist-c06",
      "source_id": "oillipheist-s2",
      "quote": "Por vezes possuem poderes especiais, como a virtude do voo ou da fala. Normalmente habitam lagos e, mais raramente, rios ou o oceano.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang dapat terbang atau berbicara; tinggal di danau, sungai, laut.",
        "en": "Sometimes able to fly or speak; they live in lakes, rivers or the ocean."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oillipheist-c07",
      "source_id": "oillipheist-s1",
      "quote": "It then went through Leitrim village and Carrick-on-Shannon, devouring the resident's sheep and cattle.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Melahap domba dan sapi penduduk Leitrim dan Carrick-on-Shannon.",
        "en": "It devoured residents' sheep and cattle at Leitrim and Carrick-on-Shannon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "oillipheist-c08",
      "source_id": "oillipheist-s1",
      "quote": "The people near Athleague were angered by the loss of their livestock and took a stand against the beast, led by a drunken piper named O'Rourke (Ó Ruairc).",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penduduk Athleague melawannya dipimpin peniup pipa mabuk O'Rourke.",
        "en": "People near Athleague, led by the drunken piper O'Rourke, stood against it."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sea Mither adalah makhluk mitos cerita rakyat Orkney yang tinggal di laut pada musim panas, mengurung nuckelavee, dan setiap musim semi berperang dengan Teran, roh badai musim dingin.",
    "en": "Sea Mither is a mythical being of Orcadian folklore who lives in the sea in summer, confines the nuckelavee, and each spring battles Teran, spirit of winter storms.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sea-mither-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sea Mither, atau Mither of the Sea, adalah makhluk mitos cerita rakyat Orkney (Orcadian folklore) yang tinggal di laut pada musim panas, ketika ia mengurung nuckelavee yang jahat di kedalaman laut. Setiap musim semi ia bertarung dengan musuh bebuyutannya Teran, roh legenda Orkney yang dapat menimbulkan badai musim dingin hebat, untuk menguasai laut dan cuaca. Akhirnya ia mengalahkan Teran dan mengirimnya ke dasar laut, tetapi usaha itu melelahkannya hingga pada musim gugur Teran merebut kendali lagi. Kisah Sea Mither dan Teran termasuk legenda tertua Orkney, mungkin diciptakan untuk menjelaskan perubahan cuaca.",
      "en": "In the folklore of Orkney, Sea Mither (the Mither of the Sea) is a spirit who lives in the sea through the summer, when she keeps the demonic nuckelavee bound in the ocean depths. Every spring she fights her arch-enemy Teran, another Orkney spirit able to raise fierce winter storms, for mastery of sea and weather. In the end she beats Teran and banishes him to the deep, but the effort wears her out until in autumn Teran seizes control again. Tales of Sea Mither and Teran are among Orkney's oldest legends, perhaps devised to explain the whims of the weather.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sea-mither-c01",
        "sea-mither-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Shetland, para nelayan memohon perlindungan Sea Mither dari Iblis. Mither adalah varian Skotlandia (Scots) untuk \"mother\", dan nama Teran dalam dialek Orkney berarti \"amarah besar\". Keduanya tak terlihat oleh manusia; pertarungan musim semi mereka, yang disebut \"Vore tullye\", menimbulkan angin kencang dan laut bergolak. Selama musim panas ia juga memberi makhluk laut kemampuan berkembang biak dan menghangatkan serta menenangkan laut. Menurut artikel ru, kisah Sea Mither dan Teran termasuk legenda Orkney tertua yang diketahui.",
      "en": "In Shetland, fishermen ask Sea Mither to protect them from the Devil. Mither is the Scots form of 'mother', and Teran in Orkney dialect means 'furious anger'. Both are invisible to people; their spring struggle, the 'Vore tullye', brings gales and heaving seas. Through the summer she also gives sea creatures the power to breed and warms and calms the waters. According to the ru article the tales of Sea Mither and Teran are among the oldest known Orkney legends.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sea-mither-c03",
        "sea-mither-c05",
        "sea-mither-c04",
        "sea-mither-c07",
        "sea-mither-c08",
        "sea-mither-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "modern_depictions": [],

    {
      "id": "sea-mither-s1",

      "id": "sea-mither-s2",
      "url": "https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9C%D0%B0%D1%82%D1%8C_%D0%BC%D0%BE%D1%80%D1%8F",
      "title": "Мать моря",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ru)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ru",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sea-mither-c01",
      "source_id": "sea-mither-s1",
      "quote": "Sea Mither, or Mither of the Sea, is a mythical being of Orcadian folklore that lives in the sea during summer, when she confines the demonic nuckelavee to the ocean depths. Each spring she battles with her arch-enemy Teran, another spirit of Orcadian legend capable of causing severe winter storms, to gain control of the seas and the weather.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sea Mither (Mither of the Sea) makhluk cerita rakyat Orkney (Orcadian folklore), di laut saat musim panas, mengurung nuckelavee (Nuckelavee); setiap musim semi melawan Teran; di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "Sea Mither (Mither of the Sea) is an Orcadian folklore being in the sea in summer, confining the nuckelavee; each spring she battles Teran."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-mither-c02",
      "source_id": "sea-mither-s1",
      "quote": "Eventually Sea Mither overcomes Teran and sends him to the depths of the ocean, but the effort of keeping him confined there along with her other benevolent labours during the summer exhaust her, until in the autumn Teran takes advantage of her weakness to wrest control from her once again. Stories of the Sea Mither and Teran are among Orkney's oldest legends",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengalahkan Teran, kelelahan, Teran merebut kendali di musim gugur; legenda tertua Orkney.",
        "en": "She defeats Teran, is exhausted, and Teran retakes control in autumn; among Orkney's oldest legends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-mither-c03",
      "source_id": "sea-mither-s1",
      "quote": "In Shetland, fishermen petition Sea Mither to afford them protection from the Devil.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Shetland nelayan memohon perlindungan dari Iblis (Devil).",
        "en": "In Shetland fishermen seek her protection from the Devil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-mither-c04",
      "source_id": "sea-mither-s1",
      "quote": "Both spirits are invisible to humans. Teran is her arch-enemy and the pair fight bitterly, often for weeks as she tries to gain control.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keduanya tak terlihat; bertarung berminggu-minggu.",
        "en": "Both are invisible; they fight for weeks; the Vore tullye."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-mither-c05",
      "source_id": "sea-mither-s1",
      "quote": "Mither is defined in the Dictionary of the Older Scottish Tongue as the Scots variant of \"mother\", which may particularly reflect oral Orcadian use. The name of her opponent, Teran, is local Orkney dialect meaning \"furious anger\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mither varian Skotlandia (Scots) untuk mother; Teran dalam dialek Orkney berarti amarah besar.",
        "en": "Mither is the Scots variant of mother; Teran in Orkney dialect means furious anger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-mither-c06",
      "source_id": "sea-mither-s2",
      "quote": "Истории о Матери моря и Теране относятся к числу самых старых известных оркнейских легенд",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel ru, kisah Мать моря dan Teran (Теране) termasuk legenda Orkney (оркнейских) tertua.",
        "en": "Per the ru article, the tales of Мать моря and Teran (Теране) are among the oldest Orkney (оркнейских) legends."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-mither-c07",
      "source_id": "sea-mither-s1",
      "quote": "The period of the spring combat between the pair is termed the \"Vore tullye\" or the \"spring struggle\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pertarungan musim semi disebut Vore tullye (spring struggle).",
        "en": "The spring fight is called the Vore tullye (spring struggle)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-mither-c08",
      "source_id": "sea-mither-s1",
      "quote": "During summer months the Sea Mither also keeps the demonic nuckelavee creature confined, and undertakes benevolent labours: she empowers aquatic creatures with the ability to reproduce; warms and calms the seas",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di musim panas ia memberi makhluk laut kemampuan berkembang biak, menghangatkan dan menenangkan laut.",
        "en": "In summer she lets sea creatures breed and warms and calms the seas."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Tidak ada gambar dengan lisensi yang diperiksa."
    },
    {

    },
    {

      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

    "id": "Telo adalah dewa Kelt, roh pemberi nama kota Toulon di Var, dewa mata air suci yang di sekitarnya permukiman kuno itu tumbuh.",

    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Telo adalah dewa Kelt (Celtic), roh yang namanya menjadi nama Toulon di departemen Var. Ia dewa mata air suci yang di sekitarnya permukiman kuno itu tumbuh. Serangkaian persembahan untuk Telo berasal dari Périgueux; pada tiga di antaranya Telo dipanggil bersama dewa lain, yaitu dewi Sianna (dalam tiga prasasti) dan Vesunna (dalam satu prasasti).",
      "en": "Telo is a Celtic god, the spirit who gave his name to Toulon in the Var. He was the deity of the holy spring around which the ancient settlement grew up. Several dedications to him were found at Périgueux; three of them name him alongside other deities, the goddess Sianna (three times) and Vesunna (once).",
      "claim_ids": [

        "telo-c02"

    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel fr, Télo adalah dewa Kelt, roh pemberi nama Toulon, dan dewa mata air suci tempat koloni kuno itu didirikan; pada tiga persembahan dari Périgueux ia dipanggil bersama dewi Stanna, dengan rumusan \"Deo Telo et deae Stannae\".   "short_description": {
    "id": "Naga putih (Y Ddraig Wen) adalah lambang dalam mitologi Wales yang dikaitkan dengan bangsa Anglo-Saxon, musuh naga merah bangsa Briton.",
    "en": "The white dragon (Y Ddraig Wen) is a symbol of Welsh myth associated with the Anglo-Saxons, foe of the red dragon of the Britons.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "white-dragon-c01",
      "white-dragon-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga putih (Wales/Welsh: Y Ddraig Wen) adalah lambang dalam mitologi Wales yang dikaitkan dengan bangsa Anglo-Saxon. Penggunaan tertuanya sebagai lambang Anglo-Saxon terdapat dalam Historia Brittonum: di Dinas Emrys, raja Briton Vortigern mencoba membangun benteng, tetapi setiap malam temboknya runtuh. Seorang anak tanpa ayah memberi tahu raja tentang dua naga di bawah bukit; setelah digali, keduanya bertarung dan naga merah akhirnya mengalahkan naga putih. Anak itu menjelaskan bahwa naga putih melambangkan bangsa Saxon dan naga merah bangsa Briton (Celtic Britons).",
      "en": "The white dragon (Welsh Y Ddraig Wen) is a symbol in Welsh myth tied to the Anglo-Saxons. Its earliest use as their emblem is in the Historia Brittonum: at Dinas Emrys the British king Vortigern tries to build a fort, but every night its walls fall down. A fatherless boy tells the king of two dragons under the hill; once dug up they fight, and the red dragon finally defeats the white. The boy explains that the white dragon stands for the Saxons and the red for the Celtic Britons.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "white-dragon-c01",
        "white-dragon-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah ini diulang dalam History of the Kings of Britain karya Geoffrey of Monmouth (sekitar 1136), yang menjadikan anak itu Merlin muda; Historia Brittonum dan karya Geoffrey adalah satu-satunya teks abad pertengahan yang memakai naga putih sebagai lambang bangsa Inggris. Kisah serupa naga putih dan merah juga ada dalam Lludd and Llefelys, tanpa makna Briton atau Saxon. Dalam terjemahan Giles atas Geoffrey (Old English Chronicles, Gutenberg), Merlin bernubuat: \"celakalah naga merah\", karena sarangnya akan direbut naga putih, \"yang melambangkan bangsa Saxon\".",
      "en": "The story is retold in Geoffrey of Monmouth's History of the Kings of Britain (c. 1136), where the boy becomes the young Merlin; the Historia Brittonum and Geoffrey are the only medieval texts using the white dragon as a symbol of the English. A similar tale of white and red dragons appears in Lludd and Llefelys, without the Briton or Saxon meaning. In Giles's translation of Geoffrey (Old English Chronicles, Gutenberg), Merlin prophesies 'woe to the red dragon', whose lairs the white dragon, 'which signifies the Saxons', will seize.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "white-dragon-c03",
        "white-dragon-c04",
        "white-dragon-c05",
        "white-dragon-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {

      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/37848/pg37848-images.html",
      "title": "https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/37848/pg37848-images.html",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "www.gutenberg.org",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "other",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "white-dragon-c01",
      "source_id": "white-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The white dragon (Welsh: Y Ddraig Wen ) is a symbol associated in Welsh mythology with the Anglo-Saxons. Origin of tradition [edit] The earliest usage of the white dragon as a symbol of the Anglo-Saxons is found in the Historia Brittonum.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga putih (Wales/Welsh: Y Ddraig Wen) lambang mitologi Wales untuk Anglo-Saxon; tertua dalam Historia Brittonum; di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "The white dragon (Welsh Y Ddraig Wen) is a Welsh symbol of the Anglo-Saxons; earliest in the Historia Brittonum."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-dragon-c02",
      "source_id": "white-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The boy tells the king of the two dragons. Vortigern excavates the hill, freeing the dragons. They continue their fight and the red dragon finally defeats the white dragon. The boy tells Vortigern that the white dragon symbolises the Saxons and that the red dragon symbolises the Celtic Britons.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Dinas Emrys, Vortigern (raja Briton, Britons) menggali bukit; naga merah (Welsh dragon) mengalahkan naga putih; putih = Saxon (Saxons), merah = Briton (Celtic Britons).",
        "en": "At Dinas Emrys Vortigern digs up the hill; the red dragon defeats the white; white = Saxons, red = Celtic Britons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-dragon-c03",
      "source_id": "white-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "The story is repeated in Geoffrey of Monmouth's fictional History of the Kings of Britain (c. 1136). In this telling the boy is identified as the young Merlin. The Historia Brittonum and History of the Kings of Britain are the only medieval texts to use the white dragon as a symbol of the English.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Diulang Geoffrey of Monmouth (History of the Kings of Britain, sekitar 1136), anak itu Merlin; satu-satunya teks abad pertengahan, lambang bangsa Inggris (English).",
        "en": "Retold by Geoffrey of Monmouth (History of the Kings of Britain, c. 1136) with the boy as Merlin; the only medieval texts using it for the English."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-dragon-c04",
      "source_id": "white-dragon-s1",
      "quote": "A similar story of white and red dragons fighting is found in the medieval romance Lludd and Llefelys, although in this case the dragons are not used to symbolize Britons or Saxons.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah serupa dalam Lludd and Llefelys, tanpa makna Briton (Britons) atau Saxon (Saxons).",
        "en": "A similar story in Lludd and Llefelys, without Briton or Saxon meaning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-dragon-c05",
      "source_id": "white-dragon-s2",
      "quote": "the two serpents are two dragons; the red serpent is your dragon, but the white serpent is the dragon of the people who occupy several provinces and districts of Britain, even almost from sea to sea",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Giles atas Nennius: ular putih naga bangsa yang menduduki Britania (Britain); ular merah naga raja.",
        "en": "Giles's translation of Nennius: the white serpent is the dragon of the people occupying Britain; the red is the king's."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "white-dragon-c06",
      "source_id": "white-dragon-s2",
      "quote": "\"Woe to the red dragon, for his banishment hasteneth on. His lurking holes shall be seized by the white dragon, which signifies the Saxons whom you invited over; but the red denotes the British nation, which shall be oppressed by the white.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nubuat Merlin (Old English Chronicles, Gutenberg; Giles): celakalah naga merah (Woe to the red dragon); naga putih melambangkan Saxon (Saxons).",
        "en": "Merlin's prophecy (Giles): woe to the red dragon; the white dragon signifies the Saxons."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cath Palug (\"Kucing Palug\") adalah kucing raksasa dalam mitologi Wales yang dikaitkan dengan legenda Arthurian, lahir dari babi Henwen dan menghantui Anglesey sampai diburu Kay.",
    "en": "Cath Palug ('Palug's Cat') is a monstrous cat of Welsh myth tied to Arthurian legend, born of the sow Henwen, haunting Anglesey until Kay hunted it.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cath-palug-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Cath Palug (juga Cath Paluc, Cath Balug, Cath Balwg, harfiah \"Kucing Palug\") adalah kucing raksasa dalam mitologi Wales (Welsh) yang dikaitkan dengan legenda Arthurian. Dilahirkan di Gwynedd oleh babi Henwen dari Cornwall, kucing itu menghantui Pulau Anglesey sampai Kay datang memburunya. Di luar Wales, lawannya dialihkan menjadi Raja Arthur sendiri atau, kadang, pahlawan lain seperti Ogier the Dane; dalam sastra Prancis (French) namanya Chapalu. Nama Wales itu mungkin berarti \"kucing pencakar\", sedangkan bentuk Prancis Chapalu dapat diurai menjadi chat \"kucing\" + palu \"rawa\", \"kucing rawa\".",
      "en": "Known as Cath Palug, Cath Paluc, Cath Balug or Cath Balwg, that is 'Palug's Cat', this is a monstrous feline of Welsh myth linked with Arthurian legend. Born in Gwynedd to the sow Henwen of Cornwall, it haunted the Isle of Anglesey until Kay came to hunt it down. Outside Wales its foe became King Arthur himself or now and then other heroes such as Ogier the Dane; in French literature it is Chapalu. The Welsh name may mean 'scratching cat', while the French Chapalu splits into chat 'cat' + palu 'bog', 'the bog cat'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cath-palug-c01",
        "cath-palug-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Cath Palug selalu ditempatkan dekat air, seperti danau Lac du Bourget dan Danau Jenewa di Prancis, atau laut di Wales. Menurut Welsh Triads (akhir abad ketiga belas), ia dilahirkan sebagai anak kucing oleh babi putih besar Henwen di batu hitam di Llanfair, dibuang ke laut, menyeberangi Selat Menai, dan dibesarkan putra-putra Palug di Anglesey tanpa menyadari ia akan menjadi salah satu dari tiga wabah besar pulau itu. Dalam puisi Wales Kuno Pa Gur di Black Book of Carmarthen, Cai (Kay) tersirat bertarung dan membunuhnya. Menurut artikel fr, dalam La Bataille Loquifer Chapalu lahir dari peri Brunehaut dan berusaha kembali menjadi manusia, dan beberapa teks serta sebuah mosaik menggambarkan pertarungan Raja Arthur melawannya.",
      "en": "Cath Palug is always set near water, such as Lac du Bourget and Lake Geneva in France, or the sea in Wales. In the Welsh Triads (late thirteenth century) it was born as a kitten to the great white sow Henwen at the black rock in Llanfair, cast into the sea, crossed the Menai Strait and was raised by the sons of Palug on Anglesey, unaware it would become one of the island's three great plagues. In the Old Welsh poem Pa Gur in the Black Book of Carmarthen, Cai (Kay) is implied to fight and kill it. According to the fr article, in La Bataille Loquifer Chapalu is born of the fairy Brunehaut and seeks to become human again, and some texts and a mosaic show King Arthur fighting it.",

        "cath-palug-c04",

      ]

      "quote": "was a monstrous cat in Welsh mythology associated with Arthurian legend.       "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cath Palug (Cath Paluc, Cath Balug, Cath Balwg; Kucing Palug) kucing raksasa mitologi Wales (Welsh), legenda Arthurian; lahir di Gwynedd dari Henwen dari Cornwall; menghantui Anglesey (Isle of Anglesey) sampai Kay memburunya; di luar Wales lawannya Raja Arthur (King Arthur) atau Ogier the Dane; Chapalu (French, Prancis); di Britania dan Prancis.",
        "en": "Cath Palug (Palug's Cat) was a monstrous cat of Welsh myth, born in Gwynedd to Henwen of Cornwall, haunting Anglesey until Kay hunted it; elsewhere its foe is King Arthur or Ogier the Dane."

    {
      "id": "cath-palug-c02",
      "source_id": "cath-palug-s1",

      "source_id": "cath-palug-s1",
      "quote": "According to this source, it started life as a kitten (lit \"whelp\" ), given birth by the great white sow Henwen at the black rock in Llanfair [cy].       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Welsh Triads: lahir dari Henwen di Llanfair, dibuang ke laut, menyeberangi Selat Menai (Menai Strait) ke Anglesey (Ynys Môn), dibesarkan putra Palug; tiga wabah pulau.",
        "en": "Welsh Triads: born to Henwen at Llanfair, cast into the sea, crossed the Menai Strait to Anglesey (Ynys Môn), raised by Palug's sons; one of the island's three plagues."

      "source_id": "cath-palug-s2",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ceffyl Dŵr adalah kuda air dalam cerita rakyat Wales, padanan kelpie Skotlandia, yang memikat pelancong untuk menungganginya lalu menjatuhkan mereka hingga tewas.",
    "en": "The ceffyl dŵr is a water horse of Welsh folklore, counterpart of the Scottish kelpie, that lures travellers to ride it and then drops them to their deaths.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ceffyl-dwr-c01",
      "ceffyl-dwr-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ceffyl Dŵr (juga ceffyl dwfr atau ceffyl-dŵr) adalah kuda air dalam cerita rakyat Wales (Welsh). Ia paling sering dianggap padanan kelpie Skotlandia (Scottish), tetapi juga dibandingkan dengan púca Irlandia (Irish), each uisge Irlandia dan Skotlandia, dan glaistyn Manx. Ia dikatakan terutama menghuni kolam gunung dan air terjun, meski juga sungai dan pantai. Ia diyakini roh yang berwujud kuda, biasanya kelabu atau hitam bersurai putih, kadang bercahaya atau bersayap, untuk memikat pelancong yang lengah agar menungganginya.",
      "en": "Welsh folklore tells of the ceffyl dŵr, also written ceffyl dwfr or ceffyl-dŵr, a horse of the waters. Usually it is matched with the Scottish kelpie, though it has also been compared with the Irish púca, the each uisge and the Manx glaistyn. It mostly haunts mountain pools and waterfalls, though rivers and seashores too. People took it for a spirit in horse shape, most often grey or black with a pale mane, at times shining or with wings, that tempted careless travellers to climb on.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ceffyl-dwr-c01",
        "ceffyl-dwr-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Setelah ditunggangi, ia membawa penunggangnya sangat jauh dengan cepat atau terbang ke langit lalu menguap menjadi udara atau kabut, menjatuhkan penunggangnya hingga tewas. Wataknya berbeda menurut daerah: di Wales Utara sering dianggap jahat dengan mata menyala, sedangkan di Wales Selatan lebih positif, paling buruk pengganggu dan paling baik penolong pelancong yang lelah. Di beberapa daerah ia dapat berubah menjadi kambing yang menyeruduk, atau di Vale of Clwyd menjadi katak yang melompat ke punggung orang dan mencekik mereka. Menurut artikel de, namanya berarti \"kuda air\" (ceffyl \"kuda\", dŵr \"air\"), dan ia tidak sejahat kelpie atau each uisge.",
      "en": "Once mounted it either carries the rider a great distance at speed or soars into the sky and dissolves into air or mist, dropping the rider to death. Its temper varies by region: in North Wales it was often wicked, with fiery eyes, while in South Wales it was viewed more kindly, at worst a nuisance and at best a help to weary travellers. In some places it could become a goat that charged people, or in the Vale of Clwyd a frog that leapt onto people's backs and choked them. According to the de article its name means 'water horse' (ceffyl 'horse', dŵr 'water'), and it is less vicious than the kelpie or each uisge.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ceffyl-dwr-c03",
        "ceffyl-dwr-c04",
        "ceffyl-dwr-c05",
        "ceffyl-dwr-c06"
      ]
    }

      "id": "ceffyl-dwr-c01",
      "source_id": "ceffyl-dwr-s1",
      "quote": "is a water horse in Welsh folklore. It is most commonly considered a counterpart to the Scottish kelpie, but has also been compared to the Irish púca, the Irish and Scottish each uisge, and the Manx glaistyn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ceffyl Dŵr (ceffyl dwfr) kuda air cerita rakyat Wales (Welsh), padanan kelpie (Kelpie) Skotlandia (Scottish), dibandingkan púca Irlandia (Irish), each uisge (Each-uisge), glaistyn Manx (Glashtyn); di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "The ceffyl dŵr (ceffyl dwfr) is a Welsh water horse, counterpart of the Scottish kelpie, compared with the Irish púca, each uisge and the Manx glaistyn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ceffyl-dwr-c02",
      "source_id": "ceffyl-dwr-s1",
      "quote": "The ceffyl dŵr is said to inhabit mostly mountain pools and waterfalls, though it has been known to frequent rivers and seashores as well. It was believed to be a spirit assuming the shape of a horse, usually grey or black with a white mane, sometimes glowing or winged, to entice unwary travellers to ride him.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menghuni kolam gunung dan air terjun; roh berwujud kuda kelabu atau hitam bersurai putih, kadang bersayap.",
        "en": "Lives in mountain pools and waterfalls; a spirit as a grey or black white-maned horse, sometimes winged."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ceffyl-dwr-c03",
      "source_id": "ceffyl-dwr-s1",
      "quote": "Once mounted it would either carry its rider great distances very quickly, or fly into the sky, then evaporate into air or mist, dropping the unfortunate rider to their death.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Membawa penunggang jauh atau terbang lalu menguap, menjatuhkannya hingga tewas.",
        "en": "Carries the rider far or flies and evaporates, dropping the rider to death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ceffyl-dwr-c04",
      "source_id": "ceffyl-dwr-s1",
      "quote": "In North Wales he was often seen as malevolent, described with fiery eyes and a dark, forbidding presence, whereas in South Wales he was generally more positively seen as, at worst, a pest to travellers, and at best a great aid to tired travellers and often luminescent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Wales Utara (North Wales) jahat bermata api; di Wales Selatan (South Wales) lebih baik.",
        "en": "In North Wales malevolent with fiery eyes; in South Wales more positive."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ceffyl-dwr-c05",
      "source_id": "ceffyl-dwr-s1",
      "quote": "Sometimes it took the form of a goat and charged at people with enough force to cause serious injury. In the Vale of Clwyd a ceffyl dŵr often transformed into a frog which hopped on people's backs and choked them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berubah menjadi kambing yang menyeruduk atau katak di Vale of Clwyd yang mencekik orang.",
        "en": "It became a goat charging people, or a frog in the Vale of Clwyd that choked people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ceffyl-dwr-c06",
      "source_id": "ceffyl-dwr-s2",
      "quote": "Ceffyl dŵr bedeutet wörtlich aus dem Walisischen übersetzt „Wasserpferd“ (ceffyl – Pferd und dŵr – Wasser). Es ist verwandt mit den anderen weitverbreiteten Wasserpferden in der keltischen Mythologie wie dem each uisge (so die schottisch-gälische Namensform, irisch-gälisch each uisce, anglisierte irische Form aughisky) oder dem Kelpie, aber nicht so bösartig wie diese.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ceffyl dŵr berarti kuda air (Wasserpferd); kerabat each uisge dan Kelpie, tapi tak sejahat mereka.",
        "en": "Ceffyl dŵr means water horse (Wasserpferd); kin to the each uisge and Kelpie but less vicious."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fuath (\"kebencian\") adalah golongan roh jahat dalam cerita rakyat Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia dan Irlandia, terutama roh air.",
    "en": "Fuath ('hatred') is a class of malevolent spirits in Scottish Highland and Irish folklore, especially water spirits.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fuath-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fuath (Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic, harfiah \"kebencian\"; jamak fuathan) adalah golongan roh jahat dalam cerita rakyat Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia (Scottish Highland) dan Irlandia (Irish), terutama roh air. Istilah ini dijelaskan sebagai golongan umum roh yang menghuni laut, sungai, air tawar, atau loch laut, dengan beberapa \"subspesies\"; kata itu berasal dari Irlandia Kuno (Old Irish) fúath, \"benci, rupa\", dan juga diartikan \"hantu\". Nama lain golongan ini adalah arrachd atau fuath-arrachd.",
      "en": "Fuath (Scottish Gaelic, literally 'hatred'; plural fuathan) is a class of malevolent spirits in Scottish Highland and Irish folklore, especially water spirits. The term has been explained as a general class of spirits haunting the sea, rivers, fresh water or sea lochs, with several 'subspecies'; it comes from Old Irish fúath, 'hate, likeness', and is also glossed 'ghost' or 'spectre'. Another name for the class is arrachd or fuath-arrachd.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fuath-c01",
        "fuath-c03",
        "fuath-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Sutherland ada Moulin na Vaugha, \"Penggilingan Fuath\", yang dihantui fuath dan putranya, brollachan yang tak berbentuk; fuath yang pernah terlihat di sana adalah banshee tanpa hidung berambut kuning bergaun sutra hijau, yang ditundukkan dengan baja (penusuk dan jarum jahit) tetapi berubah menjadi gumpalan seperti ubur-ubur saat disinari cahaya. Mereka kadang kawin dengan manusia, dan keturunannya tumbuh surai dan ekor. Menurut artikel de, fuath digambarkan berbulu kuning lebat atau hanya bersurai panjang di punggung, berselaput di jari kaki, berekor berduri, tak berhidung, dan biasanya berpakaian hijau, warna peri.",
      "en": "In Sutherland stood Moulin na Vaugha, 'Mill of the Fuath', haunted by a fuath and her son, the formless brollachan; one fuath seen there was a noseless banshee with yellow hair in a green silk dress, subdued with steel (an awl and a sewing needle) but turning into a jellyfish-like mass when light was shone on it. They sometimes marry humans, whose offspring grow a mane and tail. According to the de article fuathan are covered in shaggy yellow fur or have just a long mane down the back, webbed toes, a spiked tail and no nose, and usually wear green, the fairies' colour.",

      "id": "fuath-c04",
      "source_id": "fuath-s1",
      "quote": "The Scottish Gaelic term fuath has been explained to mean 'hatred' or 'aversion', derived from Old Irish fúath 'hate, likeness'. The term is also glossed to mean 'ghost' or 'spectre'. An alternative name for this class of monsters is the arrachd or fuath-arrachd.",

      "statement": {

      "id": "fuath-c07",
      "source_id": "fuath-s2",
      "quote": "In manchen Beschreibungen sind sie bedeckt mit einem zottigen, gelben Fell, in anderen haben sie nur eine lange Mähne entlang ihrem Rücken. Sie haben Schwimmhäute zwischen den Zehen, einen Schwanz mit Stacheln und keine Nase. Sie tragen für gewöhnlich Grün, die Farbe der Feen",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bulu kuning lebat (gelben Fell) atau surai; jari kaki berselaput, ekor berduri, tanpa hidung; berpakaian hijau (Grün), warna peri (Feen).",
        "en": "Shaggy yellow fur (gelben Fell) or a mane; webbed toes, spiked tail, no nose; wear green (Grün), the fairies' (Feen) colour."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fuath-c08",
      "source_id": "fuath-s1",
      "quote": "In Sutherland was the so-called Moulin na Vaugha/Fouadh, ‘Mill of the Fuath', haunted by the fuath and her son, the amorphous brollachan.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Moulin na Vaugha (Penggilingan Fuath) di Sutherland dihantui fuath dan putranya brollachan (Brollachan).",
        "en": "Moulin na Vaugha (Mill of the Fuath) in Sutherland was haunted by a fuath and her son the brollachan."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Henwen (\"Si Tua Putih\") adalah babi betina dalam legenda Wales yang menurut Welsh Triads melahirkan Cath Palug serta gandum, lebah, serigala, dan elang.",
    "en": "Henwen ('Old White') is a sow of Welsh legend who, by the Welsh Triads, gave birth to Cath Palug as well as wheat, bees, a wolf and an eagle.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "henwen-c01",
      "henwen-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Henwen, yang berarti \"Si Tua Putih\" (Old White), adalah babi betina dalam legenda Wales (Welsh) yang menurut Welsh Triads melahirkan Cath Palug, kucing raksasa yang dilawan Cai (Sir Kay) atau Raja Arthur (King Arthur). Menurut triad \"Three Powerful Swineherds of the Isle of Britain\", babi itu dipelihara oleh Coll putra Collfrewy, penggembala babi Dallwyr Dallben, di Lembah Dallwyr di Cornwall. Babi itu akan melahirkan, tetapi menurut nubuat hal itu pertanda buruk bagi Pulau Britania, sehingga ia dikejar sampai terjun ke laut di Penrhyn Awstin, Cornwall, lalu muncul lagi di Aber Tarogi di Gwent Is-coed.",
      "en": "Henwen, meaning 'Old White', is a sow of Welsh legend who, by the Welsh Triads, bore Cath Palug, the monstrous cat fought by Cai (Sir Kay) or King Arthur. In the triad 'Three Powerful Swineherds of the Isle of Britain' she was kept by Coll son of Collfrewy, swineherd to Dallwyr Dallben, in the Glen of Dallwyr in Cornwall. She was about to give birth, which prophecy said boded ill for the Isle of Britain, so she was hunted until she leapt into the waves off Penrhyn Awstin in Cornwall, landing again at Aber Tarogi in Gwent Is-coed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "henwen-c01",
        "henwen-c02",
        "henwen-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di berbagai tempat ia kemudian melahirkan keturunan, sebagian membawa berkah dan sebagian bencana: sebutir gandum dan seekor lebah di Maes Gwenith, Gwent; jelai dan lebah di Llonion; anak serigala dan anak elang di bukit Cyferthwch, Snowdonia; dan seekor anak kucing di bawah Batu Hitam di Llanfair, Arfon, yang dibuang ke Selat Menai dan menjadi Cath Palug. Don Carleton menduga kisah Henwen adalah alegori perburuan Arthur terhadap seorang pemimpin agama perempuan. Menurut artikel fr, Henwen juga diartikan \"Leluhur Suci\", dan ia membawa jelai dan gandum hitam ke Wales.",
      "en": "She then bore offspring in various places, some a blessing and some a bane: a grain of wheat and a bee at Maes Gwenith in Gwent; barley and a bee at Llonion; a wolf-cub and an eaglet on the hill of Cyferthwch in Snowdonia; and a kitten under the Black Rock at Llanfair in Arfon, cast into the Menai Strait, which became Cath Palug. Don Carleton suggests the Henwen tale is an allegory of Arthur harrying a female religious leader. According to the fr article Henwen is also glossed 'the Sacred Ancestor', and she brought barley and rye to Wales.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "henwen-c04",
        "henwen-c07",
        "henwen-c08",
        "henwen-c05",
        "henwen-c06",
        "henwen-c09"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "henwen-c01",
      "source_id": "henwen-s1",
      "quote": "Henwen, meaning \"Old White\", is in Welsh legend a sow (female pig) which according to the Welsh Triads gave birth to Cath Palug, a monstrous cat depicted as combating with either Cai (Sir Kay) or King Arthur of Arthurian Legends.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Henwen (Old White, Si Tua Putih) babi betina legenda Wales (Welsh), menurut Welsh Triads ibu Cath Palug yang dilawan Cai (Sir Kay) atau Raja Arthur (King Arthur); di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "Henwen (Old White) is a sow of Welsh legend who per the Welsh Triads bore Cath Palug, fought by Cai (Sir Kay) or King Arthur."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "henwen-c02",
      "source_id": "henwen-s1",
      "quote": "According to the triad \"Three Powerful Swineherds of the Isle of Britain\", the sow was kept by one Coll, son of Collfrewy, a pigkeeper for Dallwyr Dallben (also spelt Dallweir). The variant Red Book of Hergest (RBH) and White Book of Rhydderch (WBR) texts add that the Dallwyr held a valley named after him, the Glen of Dallwyr in Cornwall.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Three Powerful Swineherds of the Isle of Britain: dipelihara Coll putra Collfrewy untuk Dallwyr Dallben; Lembah Dallwyr (Glen of Dallwyr), Cornwall.",
        "en": "Three Powerful Swineherds of the Isle of Britain: kept by Coll son of Collfrewy for Dallwyr Dallben; the Glen of Dallwyr in Cornwall."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "henwen-c03",
      "source_id": "henwen-s1",
      "quote": "The sow was ready to give birth, but this boded ill for the Isle of Britain, according to prophecy, so she was chased until she plunged into the sea at Penrhyn Awstin in Cornwall. The sow eventually re-emerges on land at Aber Tarogi in Gwent Is-coed (a subdivision of Gwent).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kelahirannya pertanda buruk bagi Pulau Britania (Isle of Britain); dikejar ke laut di Penrhyn Awstin, muncul di Aber Tarogi, Gwent Is-coed.",
        "en": "Her birthing boded ill for the Isle of Britain; chased into the sea at Penrhyn Awstin, she re-emerged at Aber Tarogi, Gwent Is-coed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "henwen-c04",
      "source_id": "henwen-s1",
      "quote": "At Wheat Field (Maes Gwenith) in Gwent, a grain of wheat and a bee At Llonion in Pembroke/Dyfed, a grain of barley and a bee",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Maes Gwenith (Wheat Field), Gwent: gandum dan lebah; di Llonion: jelai dan lebah.",
        "en": "At Maes Gwenith (Wheat Field) in Gwent a grain of wheat and a bee; at Llonion barley and a bee."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "henwen-c05",
      "source_id": "henwen-s1",
      "quote": "Don Carleton suggests that the tale of Henwen is an allegorical account of the harrying of a female religious leader across south-west Britain by Arthur.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Don Carleton: alegori perburuan Arthur terhadap pemimpin agama perempuan.",
        "en": "Don Carleton: an allegory of Arthur harrying a female religious leader."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "henwen-c06",
      "source_id": "henwen-s2",
      "quote": "Henwen (« la Vieille Blanche », « l'Ancêtre Sacrée ») est, dans la légende arthurienne, une truie fabuleuse, née au Pays de Galles.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Henwen (Vieille Blanche; Ancêtre Sacrée, Leluhur Suci) babi betina ajaib (truie fabuleuse) legenda Arthurian, lahir di Wales (Pays de Galles); Hen Wen. (Sacred Ancestor)",
        "en": "Henwen (Vieille Blanche; Ancêtre Sacrée) is a fabulous sow (truie fabuleuse) of Arthurian legend born in Wales (Pays de Galles). (Sacred Ancestor)"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "henwen-c07",
      "source_id": "henwen-s1",
      "quote": "At the Hill of Cyferthwch in Snowdonia (Eryri); a wolf-cub and eaglet At Llanfair in Arfon under the Black Rock (Maen Du), a kitten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di bukit Cyferthwch, Snowdonia: anak serigala dan anak elang; di Llanfair, Arfon, di bawah Batu Hitam: anak kucing.",
        "en": "At the Hill of Cyferthwch in Snowdonia a wolf-cub and eaglet; at Llanfair in Arfon under the Black Rock a kitten."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "henwen-c08",
      "source_id": "henwen-s1",
      "quote": "The swineherd took the kitten and cast in into the Menai Strait. Then on the isle of Anglesey (Welsh: Ynys Môn ; or Mona), which is across the strait, the sons of Palug reared the cat which became the Cath Palug.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anak kucing dibuang ke Selat Menai, dibesarkan putra Palug di Anglesey menjadi Cath Palug.",
        "en": "The kitten was cast into the Menai Strait and raised by Palug's sons on Anglesey as Cath Palug."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "henwen-c09",
      "source_id": "henwen-s2",
      "quote": "elle s'échappe dans la mer en revenant périodiquement sur la côte pour apporter l'orge et le seigle au Pays de Galles, ou accoucher du Chat Palug.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membawa jelai (orge) dan gandum hitam (seigle) ke Wales atau melahirkan Chat Palug.",
        "en": "She brings barley (orge) and rye (seigle) to Wales or gives birth to the Chat Palug."
      }
    }
  ],

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Latis adalah nama dua dewa Kelt yang dipuja di Britania Romawi, seorang dewi (Dea Latis) dan seorang dewa (Deus Latis), masing-masing hanya dikenal dari satu prasasti di Cumbria.",
    "en": "Latis is the name of two Celtic deities of Roman Britain, a goddess (Dea Latis) and a god (Deus Latis), each known from a single inscription in Cumbria.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "latis-c01",
      "latis-c02",
      "latis-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam politeisme Kelt (Celtic) kuno, Latis adalah nama dua dewa yang dipuja di Britania Romawi (Roman Britain): seorang dewi (Dea Latis) dan seorang dewa (Deus Latis), masing-masing hanya dikenal dari satu prasasti. Persembahan untuk Dea Latis ditemukan di benteng Romawi Birdoswald di Cumbria, Inggris (England), pada 1873, berbunyi \"DIE LATI\" (\"untuk dewi Latis\"), dan batunya kini di Carlisle Museum; ia mungkin dikaitkan dengan sungai-sungai di dekatnya.",
      "en": "In ancient Celtic polytheism Latis is the name of two deities worshipped in Roman Britain: a goddess (Dea Latis) and a god (Deus Latis), each known from a single inscription. Dea Latis's dedication turned up in 1873 at the Roman fort of Birdoswald in Cumbria, England; it reads 'DIE LATI' ('for the goddess Latis'), and the stone is now in Carlisle Museum; she may have been linked with the nearby rivers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "latis-c01",
        "latis-c02",
        "latis-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Persembahan untuk Deus Latis ditemukan pada batu altar di benteng Romawi Aballava, Burgh-by-Sands (juga di Cumbria), berbunyi \"DEO LATI LVCIVS VRSEI\", dan ditemukan dekat gambar dewa bertanduk serta persembahan lain untuk dewa Belatucadros. Nama Latis mungkin berhubungan dengan kata Proto-Kelt *lati- \"minuman keras\", *lat- \"hari\", atau *lāto- \"nafsu\". Menurut artikel fr, Latis dikenal sebagai dewi air dan bir, dan nama kota benteng Latiscum dekat Vix di Burgundy utara mungkin merujuk salah satu dewa ini.",
      "en": "An altar-stone for Deus Latis, from Aballava fort at Burgh-by-Sands, likewise in Cumbria, bears the words 'DEO LATI LVCIVS VRSEI' and lay close to a carving of a horned god and to a second altar, dedicated to Belatucadros. The name Latis may relate to Proto-Celtic *lati- 'liquor', *lat- 'day' or *lāto- 'lust'. According to the fr article Latis is known as a goddess of water and beer, and the name of the hill-fort town Latiscum near Vix in northern Burgundy may refer to one of these deities.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "latis-c08",
        "latis-c04",
        "latis-c05",
        "latis-c06",
        "latis-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "latis-c01",

      "quote": "In ancient Celtic polytheism, Latis is the name of two Celtic deities worshipped in Roman Britain. One is a goddess (Dea Latis), the other a god (Deus Latis), and they are both known from a single inscription each.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam politeisme Kelt (Celtic), Latis nama dua dewa di Britania Romawi (Roman Britain): Dea Latis dan Deus Latis, satu prasasti masing-masing; di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "In Celtic polytheism Latis names two deities of Roman Britain: Dea Latis and Deus Latis, one inscription each."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "latis-c02",
      "source_id": "latis-s1",
      "quote": "The dedication to Dea Latis was found at Birdoswald Roman Fort in Cumbria, England, in 1873. It reads simply: DIE LATI For the goddess Latis.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Persembahan Dea Latis di Birdoswald Roman Fort, Cumbria, Inggris (England), 1873: DIE LATI.",
        "en": "The Dea Latis dedication at Birdoswald Roman Fort, Cumbria, England, 1873: DIE LATI."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "latis-c03",
      "source_id": "latis-s1",
      "quote": "The stone is now in the Carlisle Museum. She may have been associated with the nearby rivers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Batu di Carlisle Museum; mungkin dikaitkan dengan sungai.",
        "en": "The stone is in the Carlisle Museum; perhaps linked with nearby rivers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "latis-c04",
      "source_id": "latis-s1",
      "quote": "The altar-stone to Deus Latis was found near an image of a horned god and another dedication to the god Belatucadros.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Altar Deus Latis ditemukan dekat gambar dewa bertanduk dan persembahan untuk Belatucadros.",
        "en": "The Deus Latis altar (DEO LATI LVCIVS VRSEI) at Aballava, Burgh-by-Sands, lay near a horned god and a dedication to Belatucadros."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "latis-c05",
      "source_id": "latis-s1",
      "quote": "The name 'Latis' may conceivably be related to the Proto-Celtic words *lati- meaning 'liquor', *lat- 'day', or *lāto- 'lust'.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama dari Proto-Kelt (Proto-Celtic) *lati- minuman keras, *lat- hari, *lāto- nafsu.",
        "en": "The name may relate to Proto-Celtic *lati- liquor, *lat- day, or *lāto- lust."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "latis-c06",
      "source_id": "latis-s2",
      "quote": "Latis est connue comme la déesse de l'eau et de la bière. Elle a peut-être été associée aux rivières voisines.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Latis dewi air dan bir (déesse de l'eau et de la bière).",
        "en": "Latis is known as goddess of water and beer (déesse de l'eau et de la bière)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "latis-c07",
      "source_id": "latis-s2",
      "quote": "Le nom de la cité fossile de Latiscum élevée sur une butte isolée dans une boucle de la Seine près de Vix dans le nord Bourgogne",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kota Latiscum dekat Vix, Burgundy (Bourgogne) utara, mungkin merujuk dewa ini.",
        "en": "The town of Latiscum near Vix in northern Burgundy (Bourgogne) may refer to these deities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "latis-c08",
      "source_id": "latis-s1",
      "quote": "The dedication to Deus Latis, recovered on an altar-stone at the Roman fort of Aballava, Burgh-by-Sands (also in Cumbria) reads: DEO LATI LVCIVS VRSEI",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Persembahan Deus Latis di Aballava, Burgh-by-Sands: DEO LATI LVCIVS VRSEI.",
        "en": "The Deus Latis dedication at Aballava, Burgh-by-Sands: DEO LATI LVCIVS VRSEI."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Peg Powler adalah nenek sihir dan roh air dalam cerita rakyat Inggris yang menghuni Sungai Tees dan menyeret anak-anak ke dalam air.",
    "en": "Peg Powler is a hag and water spirit of English folklore who lives in the River Tees and drags children into the water.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "peg-powler-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Peg Powler adalah nenek sihir dan roh air dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore) yang menghuni Sungai Tees (River Tees). Seperti Grindylow, Jenny Greenteeth, dan Nelly Longarms, ia menyeret anak-anak ke dalam air bila terlalu dekat ke tepi, dan dipakai orang tua untuk menakuti anak agar berkelakuan baik. Folklorist abad kesembilan belas William Henderson menggambarkannya berambut hijau dengan \"hasrat tak terpuaskan akan nyawa manusia\". Buih di bagian tertentu Sungai Tees disebut \"Peg Powler's suds\" atau \"Peg Powler's cream\".",
      "en": "Peg Powler is a hag and water spirit of English folklore living in the River Tees. Like the Grindylow, Jenny Greenteeth and Nelly Longarms, she pulls children into the water if they stray too near the edge, and parents invoked her to frighten children into good behaviour. The 19th-century folklorist William Henderson gives her green hair and 'an insatiable desire for human life'. Locals name the froth drifting on stretches of the Tees after her: her suds, or her cream.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "peg-powler-c01",
        "peg-powler-c02",
        "peg-powler-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Makhluk serupa bernama Nanny Powler menghantui Sungai Skerne, anak sungai Tees; Michael Denham menganggapnya saudari atau putri Peg Powler. Elliott O'Donnell dalam Ghosts, Helpful and Harmful (1924) menggambarkannya sebagai roh yang memikat lelaki ke Sungai Tees dengan menjelma perempuan muda cantik berambut hijau dan berpura-pura tenggelam. Menurut artikel fr, ia menangkap pergelangan kaki orang yang terlalu dekat sungai, terutama anak nakal, dan di Mickleton, Teesdale, ia disebut High Green Ghost.",
      "en": "A similar being called Nanny Powler haunts the River Skerne, a tributary of the Tees; Michael Denham thought her Peg Powler's sister or daughter. Elliott O'Donnell, in Ghosts, Helpful and Harmful (1924), makes her a spirit luring men into the Tees by appearing as a lovely green-haired young woman pretending to drown. According to the fr article she seizes the ankles of those who wander too close to the river, naughty children above all, and around Mickleton in Teesdale she is called the High Green Ghost.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "peg-powler-c04",
        "peg-powler-c05",
        "peg-powler-c06"
      ]

    {
      "id": "peg-powler-c02",
      "source_id": "peg-powler-s1",
      "quote": "The 19th century folklorist William Henderson describes Peg Powler as having green hair and \"an insatiable desire for human life\" and she is said to lure people into the river to drown or be devoured.",

    {
      "id": "peg-powler-c03",
      "source_id": "peg-powler-s1",
      "quote": "The foam or froth which is often seen floating on certain parts of the Tees is called \"Peg Powler's suds\" or \"Peg Powler's cream\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Foam on the Tees is called Peg Powler's suds or cream."

    },
    {
      "id": "peg-powler-c04",
      "source_id": "peg-powler-s1",
      "quote": "A similar creature named Nanny Powler is said to haunt the River Skerne, a tributary of the Tees. Michael Denham regards her as either the sister or daughter of Peg Powler.",

    {
      "id": "peg-powler-c05",
      "source_id": "peg-powler-s1",
      "quote": "Elliott O'Donnell paints a somewhat different picture of Peg Powler in his 1924 book Ghosts, Helpful and Harmful. He describes her as a spirit who lures men and boys to their doom in the River Tees by appearing as a beautiful young woman with green hair",
      "locator": null,

        "en": "Elliott O'Donnell (Ghosts, Helpful and Harmful, 1924): appears as a lovely green-haired young woman pretending to drown."

    {
      "id": "peg-powler-c06",
      "source_id": "peg-powler-s2",
      "quote": "Elle saisit les chevilles de ceux qui errent trop près de la rivière, particulièrement les enfants méchants, et les tire sous l'eau puis les noie. Dans la région de Mickleton, dans le Teesdale, elle est désignée sous le nom du Grand Fantôme Vert (High Green Ghost).",

      "statement": {

    "id": "Souconna adalah dewi Kelt Sungai Saône di Chalon-sur-Saône, yang dikenal dari prasasti persembahan.",
    "en": "Souconna is a Celtic goddess of the river Saône at Chalon-sur-Saône, known from votive inscriptions.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "souconna-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Souconna adalah dewi Kelt (Celtic), dewa Sungai Saône di Chalon-sur-Saône, yang dimohon dalam prasasti. Menurut artikel de, sungai itu sejak abad ke-4 Masehi bernama Souconna, sedangkan nama lebih tuanya Arar. Pada 1912 sebuah prasasti persembahan untuk dewi ini ditemukan di Chalon-sur-Saône, Burgundy; prasasti lain bernama sama dekat Sagonne (Cher) mungkin merujuk dewa lain karena jaraknya yang jauh.",
      "en": "Souconna is a Celtic goddess, the deity of the Saône at Chalon-sur-Saône, invoked in inscriptions. According to the de article the river bore the name Souconna from the 4th century AD, its older name being Arar. In 1912 a votive inscription to her was found at Chalon-sur-Saône in Burgundy; another bearing the name near Sagonne (Cher) may refer to a different deity given the distance.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "souconna-c01",
        "souconna-c02",
        "souconna-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ammianus Marcellinus menyebut sungai itu Sauconna, mungkin dari bentuk Kelt yang berarti \"air yang tidur\", karena alirannya yang lambat. Menurut artikel fr, ia digambarkan di depan perahu sambil menuang air ke cawan, atau duduk seperti naiad meminum air dari tangannya; pada abad ke-1 ia ditempatkan di bawah perlindungan Augustus, dan ia mungkin dipuja karena air penyembuhnya yang suci.",
      "en": "Ammianus Marcellinus calls the river Sauconna, perhaps from a Celtic form meaning 'sleeping water', for its slow current. According to the fr article she is shown before a boat pouring water into a cup, or seated like a naiad drinking from her hand; in the 1st century she was placed under the protection of Augustus, and she was probably honoured for her sacred healing waters.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "souconna-c01",
      "source_id": "souconna-s1",
      "quote": "Souconna is a Celtic goddess, the deity of the river Saône at Chalon-sur-Saône, to whom epigraphic invocation was made.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Souconna dewi Kelt (Celtic), dewa Sungai Saône di Chalon-sur-Saône; di Prancis.",
        "en": "Souconna is a Celtic goddess of the river Saône at Chalon-sur-Saône."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "souconna-c02",
      "source_id": "souconna-s2",
      "quote": "Souconna war in der keltischen Mythologie die weibliche Gottheit des Flusses Saône. Dieser Fluss trug ab dem 4. Jahrhundert n. Chr. den Namen Souconna, der ältere Name für ihn war Arar",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Souconna dewi Sungai Saône dalam mitologi Kelt (keltischen); nama sungai sejak abad ke-4 Masehi; nama lama Arar.",
        "en": "Souconna was the goddess of the Saône in Celtic (keltischen) myth; the river's name from the 4th century AD; older name Arar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "souconna-c03",
      "source_id": "souconna-s2",
      "quote": "Im Jahre 1912 wurde eine Weiheinschrift für diese Göttin in Chalon-sur-Saône (Burgund) gefunden (ILTG 314). Ein weiterer Epigraph in der Nähe von Sagonne (Département Cher, frühere römische Provinz Gallia Aquitania) trägt zwar auch diesen Namen, es dürfte sich aber wegen der großen Distanz zwischen den beiden Fundorten möglicherweise um eine andere Gottheit handeln.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "1912: prasasti di Chalon-sur-Saône (Burgund, Burgundy); prasasti lain dekat Sagonne (Cher) mungkin dewa lain.",
        "en": "1912: an inscription at Chalon-sur-Saône (Burgund); another near Sagonne (Cher) may be a different deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "souconna-c04",
      "source_id": "souconna-s2",
      "quote": "Bei Ammianus Marcellinus wird der Fluss als Sauconna bezeichnet, möglicherweise in Ableitung von Sac’hauna oder Sac’hoon (keltisch: „schlafendes Wasser“), wegen seiner langsamen Flussgeschwindigkeit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ammianus Marcellinus: Sauconna, mungkin dari Sac’hauna/Sac’hoon (keltisch), air yang tidur (schlafendes Wasser).",
        "en": "Ammianus Marcellinus: Sauconna, perhaps from Sac’hauna/Sac’hoon (keltisch), sleeping water (schlafendes Wasser)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "souconna-c05",
      "source_id": "souconna-s3",
      "quote": "L’image de la divinité des eaux, vit Souconna représentée, devant une embarcation versant de l’eau dans une coupelle, ou assise comme la naïade buvant l’eau de sa main",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Souconna digambarkan di depan perahu menuang air ke cawan, atau duduk seperti naiad (naïade).",
        "en": "Souconna is shown before a boat pouring water into a cup, or seated like a naiad (naïade)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "souconna-c06",
      "source_id": "souconna-s3",
      "quote": "Au Ie siècle, Souconna est mise sous la protection d’Auguste",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada abad ke-1 di bawah perlindungan Augustus (Auguste).",
        "en": "In the 1st century placed under the protection of Augustus (Auguste)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "souconna-c07",
      "source_id": "souconna-s3",
      "quote": "Cette déesse a probablement été vénérée pour ses eaux curatives sacrées de la Gaule pendant des millénaires.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mungkin dipuja karena air penyembuh suci (eaux curatives sacrées) Galia.",
        "en": "Probably honoured for her sacred healing waters (eaux curatives sacrées) of Gaul."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Water bull (tarbh-uisge) adalah makhluk mitologi Skotlandia penghuni danau di padang belantara, umumnya lebih ramah daripada kuda air tetapi juga amfibi dan dapat berubah wujud.",
    "en": "The water bull (tarbh-uisge) is a Scottish mythological creature of moorland lochs, usually friendlier than the water horse but likewise amphibious and shapeshifting.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "water-bull-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Water bull, juga disebut tarbh-uisge dalam bahasa Gaelik Skotlandia (Scottish Gaelic), adalah makhluk mitologi Skotlandia (Scottish) yang mirip tarroo ushtey dari Manx dan tarbh-uisce dari Irlandia (Irish). Umumnya dianggap penghuni malam danau (loch) di padang belantara, ia biasanya lebih ramah daripada padanannya, kuda air, tetapi memiliki kemampuan amfibi dan berubah wujud yang serupa. Ia dikatakan kawin dengan sapi biasa, dan keturunannya dapat dikenali dari telinganya yang kecil; menurut sebagian mitos, anak sapi itu harus dibunuh saat lahir dengan cara apa pun selain ditenggelamkan.",
      "en": "The water bull, called tarbh-uisge in Scottish Gaelic, is a Scottish mythological creature akin to the Manx tarroo ushtey and the Irish tarbh-uisce. Usually seen as a night-dwelling resident of moorland lochs, it is generally kinder than its horse counterpart, the water horse, though it has the same amphibious and shape-changing powers. It is said to breed with ordinary cattle, its calves known by their small ears; in some myths such calves must be killed at birth by any means except drowning.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "water-bull-c01",
        "water-bull-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Leksikografer Edward Dwelly menerjemahkan tarbh-uisge sebagai \"banteng air, banteng laut atau sapi\" (ajaib); tarbh berarti banteng dan uisge berarti air. Kepercayaan akan water bull bertahan di Skotlandia setidaknya hingga akhir abad ke-19. Ia dapat berubah menjadi manusia dan hidup di darat maupun air; ia bisa menjadi binatang hitam jahat yang mengerikan, tetapi tidak sejahat each-uisge, dan bisa pula ramah dan menolong. Menurut artikel fr, di wilayah utara anak sapi keturunannya justru dianggap berkualitas unggul.",
      "en": "The lexicographer Edward Dwelly renders tarbh-uisge as 'water bull, sea bull or cow' (fabulous); tarbh means bull and uisge water. Belief in water bulls lasted in Scotland until at least the late 19th century. It can take human form and live on land or in water; it may be a monstrous, wicked black beast, though less nasty than the each-uisge, or friendly and helpful. According to the fr article, in northern areas its half-bred calves are on the contrary prized as superior.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "water-bull-c03",

      ]

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Abcán adalah penyair dan musisi kerdil Tuatha Dé Danann dalam mitologi Irlandia, yang memiliki perahu perunggu berlayar timah.",
    "en": "Abcán is the dwarf poet and musician of the Tuatha Dé Danann in Irish myth, owner of a bronze boat with a tin sail.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "abcan-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish), Abcán (ejaan modern: Abhcán) adalah penyair dan musisi kerdil Tuatha Dé Danann, dewa-dewa Kelt (Celtic) awal Irlandia (Ireland). Ia dikatakan memiliki perahu perunggu dengan layar timah. Menurut artikel es, ia pertama kali muncul dalam mitos Ruad dan disebut dewa mitologi Irlandia yang melayani Tuatha Dé Danann.",
      "en": "Irish myth makes Abcán, spelled Abhcán today, a dwarf who served as poet and musician to the Tuatha Dé Danann, Ireland's early Celtic gods. He was said to own a bronze boat with a tin sail. According to the es article he first appears in the myth of Ruad and is called a god of Irish myth serving the Tuatha Dé Danann.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "abcan-c01",
        "abcan-c04",
        "abcan-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah kematian dewi Ruad, Abcán adalah kerdil yang menyeberangkannya dari Dunia Lain ke dunia ini agar ia dapat merayu manusia bernama Aed Srónmár; suara nyanyian putri duyung, atau dalam sebagian versi musik dari gundukan peri, membuatnya melompat ke air dan tenggelam. Dalam kisah lain Abcán ditangkap pahlawan Cúchulainn, tetapi membebaskan diri dengan memainkan nina bobo yang begitu memikat hingga sang pejuang tertidur. Ia banyak kemiripannya, dan mungkin nama lain, dengan musisi kerdil Fer Í.",
      "en": "In the tale of the goddess Ruad's death, Abcán is the dwarf who ferries her from the Otherworld to this one so she can seduce a mortal, Aed Srónmár; the singing of mermaids, or in some versions music from a fairy mound, makes her leap into the water and drown. In another story the hero Cúchulainn captures Abcán, who frees himself by playing lullabies so irresistible that the warrior falls asleep. He closely resembles the dwarf musician Fer Í and may simply be the same figure under a different name.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "abcan-c02",
        "abcan-c03",
        "abcan-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "abcan-c01",
      "source_id": "abcan-s1",
      "quote": "In Irish mythology, Abcán (modern spelling: Abhcán) was the dwarf poet and musician of the Tuatha Dé Danann, the early Celtic divinities of Ireland. He was said to have a bronze boat with a tin sail.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish), Abcán (Abhcán) penyair dan musisi kerdil Tuatha Dé Danann, dewa Kelt (Celtic) Irlandia (Ireland); perahu perunggu berlayar timah; di Irlandia.",
        "en": "In Irish mythology Abcán (Abhcán) was the dwarf poet and musician of the Tuatha Dé Danann, early Celtic gods of Ireland; he had a bronze boat with a tin sail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "abcan-c02",
      "source_id": "abcan-s1",
      "quote": "In the story of the death of the goddess Ruad, Abcán is the dwarf that ferries her from the Otherworld to this one so that she can seduce the human, Aed Srónmár.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Abcán menyeberangkan Ruad dari Dunia Lain (Otherworld) untuk merayu Aed Srónmár.",
        "en": "Abcán ferries Ruad from the Otherworld to seduce Aed Srónmár."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "abcan-c03",
      "source_id": "abcan-s1",
      "quote": "In another story, Abcán is captured by the hero, Cúchulainn. He frees himself by playing lullabies so irresistible that the warrior goes to sleep. Abcán has much in common with, and may be another name for, the dwarf musician Fer Í.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ditangkap Cúchulainn, bebas dengan nina bobo; mungkin Fer Í.",
        "en": "Captured by Cúchulainn, he escapes with lullabies; perhaps Fer Í."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "abcan-c04",
      "source_id": "abcan-s2",
      "quote": "Abcán (ortografía moderna: Abhcán) es un dios de la mitología irlandesa que aparece por primera vez en el mito de Ruad.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel es, Abcán dewa (dios) mitologi Irlandia (irlandesa), pertama muncul dalam mitos Ruad.",
        "en": "Per the es article Abcán is a god (dios) of Irish (irlandesa) myth, first appearing in the myth of Ruad."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "abcan-c05",
      "source_id": "abcan-s1",
      "quote": "The sounds of mermaids singing, or in some versions, music from a fairy mound cause her to leap into the water and drown.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nyanyian putri duyung atau musik gundukan peri membuat Ruad tenggelam.",
        "en": "Mermaid song or fairy-mound music makes Ruad leap in and drown."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "abcan-c06",
      "source_id": "abcan-s2",
      "quote": "Se le describe como un poeta y músico enano al servicio de los Tuatha Dé Danann, las primeras deidades celtas de Irlanda.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penyair dan musisi kerdil (poeta y músico enano) yang melayani Tuatha Dé Danann.",
        "en": "A dwarf poet and musician (poeta y músico enano) serving the Tuatha Dé Danann."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Adar Llwch Gwin adalah burung legenda dalam tradisi Wales yang sangat besar dan cerdas, sering dikaitkan dengan griffin, milik pejuang Drudwas ap Tryffin.",
    "en": "The Adar Llwch Gwin are legendary birds of Welsh tradition, very large and intelligent and often linked with the griffin, belonging to the warrior Drudwas ap Tryffin.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "adar-llwch-gwin-c01",
      "adar-llwch-gwin-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Adar Llwch Gwin adalah makhluk legenda dalam tradisi Wales (Welsh) yang digambarkan sebagai burung-burung sangat besar dan cerdas, sering dianggap berkerabat dengan griffin. Burung-burung itu diberikan kepada pejuang bernama Drudwas ap Tryffin oleh istri perinya. Namanya berasal dari kata Wales adar (\"burung\"), llwch (\"debu\"), dan gwin (\"anggur\"). Mereka konon memahami bahasa manusia dan mematuhi perintah apa pun dari tuannya.",
      "en": "The Adar Llwch Gwin are legendary beings of Welsh tradition, pictured as huge, clever birds often thought related to the griffin. The warrior Drudwas ap Tryffin received them as a gift from his fairy wife. The name comes from the Welsh adar ('bird'), llwch ('dust') and gwin ('wine'). They were said to understand human speech and obey any command their master gave.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adar-llwch-gwin-c01",
        "adar-llwch-gwin-c02",
        "adar-llwch-gwin-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ketika Drudwas hendak bertarung dengan Arthur, ia memerintahkan burung-burung itu membunuh orang pertama yang memasuki medan perang; namun Arthur terlambat, dan burung-burung itu menyerang Drudwas sendiri ketika ia masuk lebih dulu, lalu mencabiknya. Dalam puisi Wales abad pertengahan kemudian, frasa Adar Llwch Gwin dipakai untuk segala jenis burung pemangsa termasuk elang dan alap-alap, juga untuk orang pemberani. Menurut legenda, burung-burung itu menyanyikan sebuah lagu untuk meratapi kematian tuannya, liriknya dikaitkan dengan Llywarch Hen. Menurut artikel br, mereka adalah burung roh (evned speredek) yang sering dibandingkan dengan griffin dalam mitologi Britania.",
      "en": "When Drudwas was about to fight Arthur he ordered the birds to kill the first man to enter the battle; but Arthur was delayed, and the birds turned on Drudwas himself as he stepped onto the field first, tearing him apart. In later medieval Welsh poetry the phrase Adar Llwch Gwin came to mean all birds of prey, hawks and falcons included, and brave men too. Legend says the birds sang a song mourning their master's death, its words attributed to Llywarch Hen. According to the br article they are spirit birds (evned speredek) often likened to the griffin in British myth.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "adar-llwch-gwin-c06",
        "adar-llwch-gwin-c08",
        "adar-llwch-gwin-c07"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Beithir adalah makhluk besar mirip ular atau naga dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia yang tinggal di gua gunung dan memiliki sengat beracun.",
    "en": "The beithir is a large snakelike creature or dragon of Scottish folklore that lives in mountain caves and has a venomous sting.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "beithir-c01",
      "beithir-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia (Scottish folklore), beithir adalah makhluk besar mirip ular atau naga. Kata Gaelik Skotlandia (Scottish Gaelic) beithir diartikan beragam sebagai \"ular\", \"kilat\", dan \"petir\", dan ia juga disebut beithir-nimh (\"ular berbisa\") dan nathair; menurut James MacKillop kata itu juga dapat berarti \"binatang buas\" dan mungkin berasal dari kata Norse untuk \"beruang\". Ia digambarkan sebagai \"jenis ular terbesar dan paling mematikan\", atau naga tanpa sayap maupun napas api.",
      "en": "Scottish folklore knows the beithir as a huge serpent-like beast or dragon. The Scottish Gaelic word beithir is glossed as 'serpent', 'lightning' and 'thunderbolt', and it is also called beithir-nimh ('venomous serpent') and nathair; according to James MacKillop the word may also mean 'wild beast' and come from the Norse for 'bear'. Accounts call it the biggest and deadliest of serpents, or a dragon without wings or fiery breath.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "beithir-c01",
        "beithir-c02",
        "beithir-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Beithir tinggal di gua-gua gunung dan lembah (corrie) dan memiliki sengat beracun; orang yang tersengat harus segera menuju air terdekat, dan jika ia sampai lebih dulu daripada beithir ia sembuh, tetapi jika monster itu tiba lebih dulu ia celaka. Beithir dianggap salah satu fuath. Jika seekor ular biasa dibunuh, kepalanya harus dipisahkan cukup jauh dari tubuhnya dan dihancurkan, kalau tidak kedua bagian akan menyatu lagi dan ular itu hidup kembali sebagai beithir. Menurut artikel de, sebuah gunung di selatan Glencoe bernama \"Ben Vair\" atau \"Ben Vehir\" konon mengenang beithir yang berlindung di sana.",
      "en": "The beithir dwells in mountain caves and corries and has a venomous sting; someone stung must head for the nearest water, and if he reaches it before the beithir he is cured, but if the monster gets there first he is doomed. The beithir is counted among the fuath. If an ordinary snake is killed, its head must be taken well away from its body and destroyed, or the two parts will rejoin and it will come back to life as a beithir. According to the de article a mountain south of Glencoe called 'Ben Vair' or 'Ben Vehir' recalls a beithir that sheltered there.",
