  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "beithir-c01",
      "source_id": "beithir-s1",
      "quote": "In Scottish folklore, the beithir is a large snakelike creature or dragon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia (Scottish folklore) beithir makhluk besar mirip ular atau naga; di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "In Scottish folklore the beithir is a large snakelike creature or dragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beithir-c02",
      "source_id": "beithir-s1",
      "quote": "The Scottish Gaelic word beithir has been defined variously as \"serpent\", \"lightning\", and \"thunderbolt\". It is also referred to as beithir-nimh (\"venomous serpent\") and nathair (\"serpent\" and \"adder\"). The word may also mean \"wild beast\" and may be derived from the Norse for \"bear\" according to Celtic mythology scholar James MacKillop.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gaelik Skotlandia (Scottish Gaelic) beithir: ular, kilat, petir; beithir-nimh, nathair; menurut James MacKillop binatang buas, dari Norse (Norse) beruang.",
        "en": "Scottish Gaelic beithir: serpent, lightning, thunderbolt; beithir-nimh, nathair; per James MacKillop wild beast, from Norse bear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beithir-c03",
      "source_id": "beithir-s1",
      "quote": "The beithir is described as \"the largest and most deadly kind of serpent\", or as a dragon (but without certain typical European folklore draconic features such as wings or fiery breath). It dwells in mountainous caves and corries (valleys) and is equipped with a venomous sting.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular terbesar dan paling mematikan, naga tanpa sayap atau napas api; di gua gunung dan corrie; sengat beracun.",
        "en": "The largest and deadliest serpent, a dragon without wings or fire; lives in mountain caves and corries; venomous sting."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beithir-c04",
      "source_id": "beithir-s1",
      "quote": "If a person is stung by the beithir then they must head for the nearest body of water such as a river or loch. If they can reach it before the beithir does then they are cured, but if the monster reaches it first then the victim is doomed. Another cure for the sting is water in which the head of another snake has been placed. The beithir is considered one of the fuath",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yang tersengat harus ke air lebih dulu dari beithir; salah satu fuath (Fuath).",
        "en": "The stung must reach water before the beithir; it is one of the fuath."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beithir-c05",
      "source_id": "beithir-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that if a normal snake is killed then the head must be separated a proper distance from its body and destroyed. Otherwise, both parts will come together and the snake will return to life as a beithir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepala ular harus dipisah jauh dan dihancurkan, atau ia hidup kembali sebagai beithir.",
        "en": "A killed snake's head must be kept apart and destroyed, or it revives as a beithir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beithir-c06",
      "source_id": "beithir-s2",
      "quote": "Ein Berg südlich von Glencoe heißt „Ben Vair“ oder „Ben Vehir“ und soll an einen Beithir erinnern, der dort Zuflucht suchte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gunung (Berg) di selatan Glencoe, Ben Vair atau Ben Vehir, mengenang beithir.",
        "en": "A mountain (Berg) south of Glencoe, Ben Vair or Ben Vehir, recalls a beithir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "beithir-c07",
      "source_id": "beithir-s1",
      "quote": "Donald Alexander Mackenzie in Scottish Folklore and Folk Life (1935) drew a possible connection between the beithir and the mythological hag known as the Cailleach Bheur.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Donald Alexander Mackenzie (1935) mengaitkan beithir dengan Cailleach Bheur (Cailleach).",
        "en": "Donald Alexander Mackenzie (1935) linked the beithir with the Cailleach Bheur."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Biróg adalah leanan sídhe atau roh pendamping perempuan Cian dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia, yang membantunya mendekati Eithne, putri Balor, dan menyelamatkan bayi Lugh.",
    "en": "Biróg is the leanan sídhe or female familiar of Cian in Irish folklore, who helps him woo Balor's daughter Eithne and rescues the infant Lugh.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "birog-c01",
      "birog-c03",
      "birog-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Biróg (Biroge of the Mountain, Birog) dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia (Irish folklore) adalah leanan sídhe atau roh pendamping perempuan Cian yang membantunya dalam kisah meminang Eithne, putri Balor. Dalam penceritaan ulang Lady Gregory dan T. W. Rolleston ia dijadikan seorang druid perempuan. Menurut kisah rakyat yang dicatat John O'Donovan pada 1835, Balor dari bangsa Fomorian mengurung putri tunggalnya Eithne di menara Pulau Tory untuk menggagalkan nubuat bahwa ia akan dibunuh cucunya sendiri, tetapi Biroge of the Mountain membantu Mac Cinnfhaelaidh memasuki menara dan merayunya.",
      "en": "Irish folklore makes Biróg, also Biroge of the Mountain or Birog, the leanan sídhe or female familiar of Cian, aiding him in the tale of his wooing of Balor's daughter Eithne. Lady Gregory and T. W. Rolleston recast her as a druidess. In a version of the tale recorded by John O'Donovan in 1835, the Fomorian Balor locks his only daughter Eithne in a tower on Tory Island to thwart a prophecy that his grandson would kill him, but Biroge of the Mountain helps Mac Cinnfhaelaidh get into the tower and seduce her.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "birog-c01",
        "birog-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Eithne melahirkan kembar tiga, dan Balor menyuruh utusannya menenggelamkan mereka di pusaran air; dua bayi tenggelam, tetapi satu terjatuh di pelabuhan dan diselamatkan Biróg, yang membawanya kepada ayahnya untuk diasuh saudaranya, Gavida si pandai besi. Anak itu, yang kelak membunuh Balor, dalam penceritaan Lady Gregory dan Rolleston disebut Lugh. Menurut artikel de, Birog adalah druid perempuan atau Síde, dan menurut Lebor Gabála Érenn serta Cath Maige Tuired ia menyelamatkan putra Cian dari kematian karena tenggelam.",
      "en": "Eithne bears triplets, and Balor has a messenger drown them in a whirlpool; two drown, but one falls into the harbour and is saved by Biróg, who takes him to his father to be fostered by his brother Gavida the smith. The boy, who grows up to kill Balor, is named Lugh in Lady Gregory's and Rolleston's retellings. According to the de article Birog is a druidess or Síde, and in the Lebor Gabála Érenn and Cath Maige Tuired she saves Cian's son from drowning.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "birog-c07",
        "birog-c03",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cormoran adalah raksasa cerita rakyat Cornwall yang dikaitkan dengan St. Michael's Mount, raksasa pertama yang dibunuh Jack dalam dongeng \"Jack the Giant Killer\".",
    "en": "Cormoran is a giant of Cornish folklore tied to St. Michael's Mount, the first giant slain by Jack in the tale 'Jack the Giant Killer'.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cormoran-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Cormoran adalah raksasa yang dikaitkan dengan St. Michael's Mount dalam cerita rakyat Cornwall (Cornwall). Tradisi setempat menyebutnya pencipta pulau itu, dalam sebagian versi dibantu istrinya Cormelian, dan menjadikannya pangkalan untuk merampok ternak dari daratan. Ia muncul dalam dongeng Inggris (English) \"Jack the Giant Killer\" sebagai raksasa pertama yang dibunuh Jack. Nama Cormoran tidak terdapat dalam tradisi awal; ia pertama muncul dalam versi chapbook kisah Jack yang dicetak di Newcastle-upon-Tyne dan Nottingham, dan mungkin berhubungan dengan Corineus, tokoh asal nama Cornwall yang mengalahkan Gogmagog.",
      "en": "Cornish folklore ties the giant Cormoran to St. Michael's Mount; people there say he was the island's builder, sometimes helped by his wife Cormelian, and has him use it as a base for stealing cattle from the mainland. In the English tale of Jack the Giant Killer, he is the very first giant to fall to Jack. The name Cormoran is absent from early tradition, first appearing in chapbook versions of the Jack story printed in Newcastle-upon-Tyne and Nottingham, and may be connected with Corineus, Cornwall's namesake who defeated Gogmagog.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cormoran-c01",
        "cormoran-c02",
        "cormoran-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat, ia membawa granit putih dari daratan saat air surut untuk membangun pulau; dalam satu versi istrinya membawa batu hijau dari tempat yang lebih dekat, ia menendangnya, dan batu yang jatuh dari celemeknya membentuk Chapel Rock. Ia dikatakan berjari enam di setiap tangan dan kaki. Dalam English Fairy Tales karya Joseph Jacobs (Gutenberg), Cormoran setinggi delapan belas kaki, berlingkar pinggang sekitar tiga yard, tinggal di gua di tengah Mount, dan menyeberang ke daratan untuk mengambil ternak, memanggul setengah lusin lembu sekaligus.",
      "en": "The tales say he hauled white granite over from the mainland whenever the tide was out to raise the island; in one version his wife brought greenstone from nearer by, he kicked her, and the stones spilling from her apron became Chapel Rock. He was said to have six fingers and toes on each hand and foot. In Joseph Jacobs's English Fairy Tales (Gutenberg), Cormoran is eighteen feet tall and about three yards round the waist, lives in a cave in the middle of the Mount, and wades to the mainland for cattle, carrying half a dozen oxen on his back at once.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cormoran-c04",
        "cormoran-c08",
        "cormoran-c05",
        "cormoran-c06",
        "cormoran-c09",
        "cormoran-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "is a giant associated with St. Michael's Mount in the folklore of Cornwall.       "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cormoran raksasa St. Michael's Mount cerita rakyat Cornwall; pencipta pulau, istri Cormelian; merampok ternak; dalam dongeng Inggris (English) Jack the Giant Killer dibunuh Jack; di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "Cormoran is a giant of St. Michael's Mount in Cornish folklore; he built the island with his wife Cormelian and raided cattle; in the English tale Jack the Giant Killer he is Jack's first victim."

    {
      "id": "cormoran-c02",
      "source_id": "cormoran-s1",
      "quote": "The name \"Cormoran\" is not found in the early traditions; it first appears in the chapbook versions of the \"Jack the Giant Killer\" story printed in Newcastle-upon-Tyne and Nottingham, and is not of Cornish origin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The name Cormoran first appears in Jack the Giant Killer chapbooks (Newcastle-upon-Tyne, Nottingham), not Cornish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cormoran-c03",
      "source_id": "cormoran-s1",
      "quote": "The name may be related to Corineus, the legendary namesake of Cornwall. Corineus is associated with St. Michael's Mount, and is credited with defeating a giant named Gogmagog",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama mungkin terkait Corineus yang mengalahkan Gogmagog.",
        "en": "The name may relate to Corineus, who defeated Gogmagog."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cormoran-c04",
      "source_id": "cormoran-s1",
      "quote": "According to the folklore, he carried white granite from the mainland at low tide to build the island.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Membawa granit putih dari daratan saat surut untuk membangun pulau.",
        "en": "He carried white granite at low tide to build the island."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cormoran-c05",
      "source_id": "cormoran-s1",
      "quote": "From his post at St. Michael's Mount, Cormoran raided the countryside for cattle. He was distinguished by having six digits on each hand and foot.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Merampok ternak; berjari enam di tiap tangan dan kaki.",
        "en": "He raided cattle; six digits on each hand and foot."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cormoran-c06",
      "source_id": "cormoran-s2",
      "quote": "In those days the Mount of Cornwall was kept by a huge giant named Cormoran. He was eighteen feet in height, and about three yards round the waist, of a fierce and grim countenance, the terror of all the neighbouring towns and villages.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Joseph Jacobs (English Fairy Tales, Gutenberg): Cormoran raksasa delapan belas kaki, pinggang tiga yard, teror desa; Mount of Cornwall.",
        "en": "Joseph Jacobs (English Fairy Tales): Cormoran, eighteen feet tall, three yards round, terror of the villages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cormoran-c07",
      "source_id": "cormoran-s2",
      "quote": "he seized on their cattle, making nothing of carrying half-a-dozen oxen on his back at a time",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Memanggul setengah lusin lembu sekaligus; tinggal di gua di tengah Mount.",
        "en": "He carried half a dozen oxen on his back at a time."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cormoran-c08",
      "source_id": "cormoran-s1",
      "quote": "When she was halfway back, Cormoran awoke to discover Cormelian bringing different stones than he wanted, and kicked her. The stones fell from her apron and formed Chapel Rock.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cormoran menendang Cormelian; batu dari celemeknya membentuk Chapel Rock.",
        "en": "Cormoran kicked Cormelian; the stones from her apron formed Chapel Rock."

    {
      "id": "cormoran-c09",
      "source_id": "cormoran-s2",
      "quote": "He lived in a cave in the midst of the Mount, and whenever he wanted food he would wade over to the main-land",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dahut adalah putri dalam legenda dan sastra Breton yang dikaitkan dengan legenda kota tenggelam Ys, yang kehancurannya disebabkan olehnya.",
    "en": "Dahut is a princess of Breton legend and literature linked with the legend of the drowned city of Ys, whose ruin she caused.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dahut-c01",
      "dahut-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dahut, juga dieja Dahud, adalah putri dalam legenda dan sastra Breton yang dikaitkan dengan legenda kota tenggelam Ys. Namanya berasal dari Breton Kuno (Old Breton) da, \"baik\", dan hud, \"sihir\". Sebutan tertua tentang Ys dan rajanya Gradlon dari abad ke-15 tidak menyebut Dahut, dan raja sendirilah yang disalahkan; Dahut pertama disebut dalam edisi ketiga Vie des Saincts de la Bretagne Armorique karya Albert Le Grand (1680), sebagai putri \"tak tahu malu\" yang hendak membunuh ayahnya dan mencuri kunci lambang kerajaannya.",
      "en": "Dahut, also spelled Dahud, is a princess of Breton legend and literature tied to the tale of the drowned city of Ys. Her name comes from Old Breton da, 'good', and hud, 'magic'. The earliest 15th-century mentions of Ys and its king Gradlon leave Dahut out and blame the king himself; her first appearance is in Albert Le Grand's Vie des Saincts de la Bretagne Armorique, in its third edition of 1680, as a 'shameless' daughter plotting to kill her father and stealing the key that stood for his kingship.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dahut-c01",
        "dahut-c02",
        "dahut-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kebanyakan versi, Ys dilindungi tanggul yang kuncinya dipegang Raja Gradlon; Dahut, perempuan muda jahat dan bernafsu, mencuri kunci perak atau emas dan saat bersenang-senang dengan kekasihnya, kadang Iblis sendiri, membuka gerbang sehingga laut membanjiri kota. Gradlon melarikan diri berkuda dengan Dahut di belakangnya, dan begitu Dahut jatuh ke air ia dapat selamat. Dalam beberapa versi Dahut berubah menjadi Mari-morgan atau putri duyung yang nyanyiannya masih terdengar. Menurut artikel fr, Dahut kerap dikacaukan dengan Ahès dan kemungkinan berasal dari sosok perempuan Dunia Lain dalam mitologi Kelt, mungkin dewi ibu atau peri penjaga pintu air.",
      "en": "In most versions Ys is guarded by a dike whose keys King Gradlon holds; Dahut, wicked and lustful, steals the silver or gold keys and, carousing with her lover, sometimes the Devil himself, opens the gate so the sea floods the city. Gradlon flees on horseback with Dahut behind him, and once she falls into the water he escapes. In some versions Dahut becomes a Mari-morgan or mermaid whose singing can still be heard. According to the fr article Dahut is often confused with Ahès and probably comes from an Otherworld woman of Celtic myth, perhaps a mother goddess or a fairy guarding the sluice gates.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dahut-c04",
        "dahut-c08",
        "dahut-c07",
        "dahut-c05",
        "dahut-c06"
      ]
    }

      "id": "dahut-c02",
      "source_id": "dahut-s1",
      "quote": "From Old Breton da, \"good\" (cf. Welsh da, same meaning), and hud, \"magic\" (cf. Welsh hud, same meaning).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari Breton Kuno (Old Breton) da baik dan hud sihir.",
        "en": "From Old Breton da, good, and hud, magic."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dahut-c03",
      "source_id": "dahut-s1",
      "quote": "Dating to the 15th century, the earliest mentions of Ys and its king, Gradlon, do not mention Gradlon's daughter Dahut, and the king himself is to blame for the destruction of the city. Dahut was first mentioned in the third edition of Albert Le Grand's Vie des Saincts de la Bretagne Armorique (1680).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Abad ke-15 tanpa Dahut; pertama disebut Albert Le Grand, Vie des Saincts de la Bretagne Armorique (1680); Gradlon.",
        "en": "15th-century mentions lack Dahut; she first appears in Albert Le Grand's Vie des Saincts de la Bretagne Armorique (1680)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dahut-c04",
      "source_id": "dahut-s1",
      "quote": "In most retellings, the city of Ys is protected from floods by a dike, with King Gradlon possessing the keys to its gate. His daughter, Dahut, is a wicked and lustful young woman. In some versions she has many lovers, whom she murders, until the Devil himself comes to seduce her.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ys dilindungi tanggul, kunci dipegang Raja (King) Gradlon; Dahut jahat dan bernafsu; Iblis (Devil) merayunya.",
        "en": "Ys is guarded by a dike, King Gradlon holds the keys; Dahut is wicked and lustful; the Devil seduces her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dahut-c05",
      "source_id": "dahut-s1",
      "quote": "In some versions, Dahut transforms into a Mari-morgan or mermaid who haunts the area and can still be heard singing.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dahut menjadi Mari-morgan (Morgen) atau putri duyung.",
        "en": "Dahut becomes a Mari-morgan or mermaid."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dahut-c06",
      "source_id": "dahut-s2",
      "quote": "Dahut ou Dahud, parfois confondue avec Ahès, est un personnage majeur de la légende de la ville d'Ys, l'un des récits les plus connus du légendaire breton. Issue d'une figure féminine de l'Autre Monde propre à la mythologie celtique",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dahut (Dahud) kadang dikacaukan dengan Ahès; dari sosok perempuan Dunia Lain (Autre Monde) mitologi Kelt (celtique); breton.",
        "en": "Dahut (Dahud) is sometimes confused with Ahès; she derives from an Otherworld (Autre Monde) woman of Celtic (celtique) myth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dahut-c07",
      "source_id": "dahut-s1",
      "quote": "Gradlon attempts to flee on his horse with Dahut riding behind him, but the water almost overtakes him. He either throws Dahut off or she falls off. As soon as Dahut falls into the water, Gradlon is able to escape to safety.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gradlon kabur berkuda; Dahut jatuh ke air dan Gradlon selamat.",
        "en": "Gradlon flees on horseback; Dahut falls into the water and Gradlon escapes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dahut-c08",
      "source_id": "dahut-s1",
      "quote": "Dahut steals the silver or gold keys, and during her carousing with her lover winds up opening the gate. The sea floods the city.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dahut mencuri kunci perak atau emas dan membuka gerbang; laut membanjiri kota.",
        "en": "Dahut steals the silver or gold keys and opens the gate; the sea floods the city."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gancanagh (\"si pembicara cinta\") adalah peri lelaki dalam mitologi Irlandia yang terkenal merayu perempuan.",
    "en": "The gancanagh ('love talker') is a male fairy of Irish myth known for seducing women.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gancanagh-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gancanagh (dari Irlandia/Irish gean cánach, \"pembicara cinta\") adalah peri lelaki dari mitologi Irlandia yang terkenal merayu perempuan. Namanya dieja beragam, termasuk geancánach atau ganconer, dan menurut sumber yang dikumpulkan Irish Folklore Commission di County Meath kata itu juga dapat berarti anak kecil atau anak yang dewasa sebelum waktunya. Pada 1888 W. B. Yeats mencatat bahwa gancanagh tidak ada dalam kamus dan kurang dikenal di Connacht.",
      "en": "The gancanagh (from Irish gean cánach, 'love talker') is a male fairy of Irish myth known for seducing women. The name is spelled many ways, among them geancánach and ganconer, and sources gathered in County Meath by the Irish Folklore Commission say the word could also mean a small or precocious child. In 1888 W. B. Yeats noted that the gancanagh was missing from dictionaries and little known in Connacht.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gancanagh-c01",
        "gancanagh-c02",
        "gancanagh-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah tahun 1825 di The Dublin and London Magazine, ganconer adalah sebutan bagi para peri di Irlandia Utara, lelaki kecil yang tinggal di gua dan dipimpin Captain Dearg. Menurut Nicholas O'Kearney (1855), Geancanach lelaki kecil mirip leprechaun tetapi lebih malas, muncul di lembah sepi dengan pipa tanah liat pendek (dudeen) dan merayu gembala perempuan serta pemerah susu; bertemu dengannya dianggap sangat sial. Penyair Ethna Carbery menggambarkan \"Love-Talker\" sebagai peri tampan bermata hitam mirip incubus yang tak berbayang dan merayu gadis hingga mereka layu dan mati. Menurut artikel es, ia roh lelaki dari mitologi Irlandia Utara.",
      "en": "In a tale published in The Dublin and London Magazine in 1825, ganconer is a name for the fairies of the North of Ireland, little men living in caves under Captain Dearg. Per Nicholas O'Kearney (1855), the Geancanach is a little man like the leprechaun but lazier, appearing in lonely valleys with a short clay pipe (dudeen) and seducing shepherdesses and milkmaids; meeting him was thought very unlucky. The poet Ethna Carbery portrays the 'Love-Talker' as a handsome, black-eyed, incubus-like fairy who casts no shadow and leaves the girls he seduces to waste away and die. According to the es article he is a male spirit of Northern Irish myth.",

      "source_id": "gancanagh-s1",
      "quote": "According to Nicholas O’Kearney writing in 1855, the Geancanach was a little man similar to the Leprechaun, but lazier. He appeared in lonesome valleys with a dudeen, a short clay pipe which was consequently known as “the Geancanagh’s pipe.”",

    "id": "Yan-gant-y-tan adalah iblis dari Brittany yang berkeliaran malam di Finistère dengan lima lilin di lima jari tangan kanannya; bertemu dengannya pertanda buruk.",
    "en": "Yan-gant-y-tan is a demon of Brittany who wanders Finistère at night with five candles on the five fingers of his right hand; meeting him is an evil omen.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "jan-gant-i-tan-c01",
      "jan-gant-i-tan-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Yan-gant-y-tan adalah nama iblis dari Brittany, Prancis (France). Dalam Dictionnaire Infernal, Collin de Plancy mengartikan namanya \"Pengembara Malam\", tetapi terjemahan dari bahasa Breton tampaknya sekerabat dengan \"John dengan Api\", sebanding dengan Will o' the Wisp. Bertemu dengannya dikatakan pertanda buruk. Menurut artikel fr, ia sering digambarkan sebagai makhluk tinggi kurus berwajah mengerikan dan aneh atau lelaki liar berbulu.",
      "en": "Brittany in France tells of a demon called Yan-gant-y-tan. In the Dictionnaire Infernal, Collin de Plancy gives his name as 'Wanderer in the Night', though from Breton it seems closer to 'John with the Fire', not unlike the Will o' the Wisp; an encounter with him is held to bode ill. According to the fr article he is often pictured as a tall, thin creature with a hideous, grotesque face, or as a hairy wild man.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jan-gant-i-tan-c01",
        "jan-gant-i-tan-c02",
        "jan-gant-i-tan-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Yan-gant-y-tan berkeliaran malam di Finistère sambil memegang lima lilin di lima jari tangan kanannya dan memutarnya seperti roda api, sehingga ia tak dapat berbalik cepat karena takut lilinnya padam. Cara pasti menangkal pertanda buruknya adalah meninggalkan kantong kecil berisi emas atau rantai emas di tiang, yang akan dicurinya.",
      "en": "Yan-gant-y-tan roams Finistère at night holding five candles on the five fingers of his right hand and spinning them like a flaming wheel, so he cannot turn quickly for fear of putting them out. A sure way to ward off his bad omen is to leave a little bag of gold or a gold chain on a post for him to steal.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jan-gant-i-tan-c03",
        "jan-gant-i-tan-c06"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jan-gant-i-tan-c01",
      "source_id": "jan-gant-i-tan-s1",
      "quote": "Yan-gant-y-tan in the Dictionnaire Infernal, 1863. Yan-gant-y-tan is the name of a demon from Brittany, France.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yan-gant-y-tan iblis dari Brittany, Prancis (France); dalam Dictionnaire Infernal (1863).",
        "en": "Yan-gant-y-tan is a demon from Brittany, France; in the Dictionnaire Infernal (1863)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jan-gant-i-tan-c02",
      "source_id": "jan-gant-i-tan-s1",
      "quote": "Collin de Plancy, in the Dictionnaire Infernal, gives the meaning of his name as 'Wanderer in the Night', but the translation of his name from Breton seems to be cognate to 'John with the Fire' (compare Will o' the Wisp). Meeting him is said to be an evil omen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Collin de Plancy: Wanderer in the Night (Pengembara Malam); dari Breton: John with the Fire, seperti Will o' the Wisp (Will-o'-the-wisp); pertanda buruk.",
        "en": "Collin de Plancy: Wanderer in the Night; from Breton John with the Fire, like Will o' the Wisp; an evil omen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jan-gant-i-tan-c03",
      "source_id": "jan-gant-i-tan-s1",
      "quote": "Yan-gant-y-tan wanders the nights in Finistère. He holds five candles on the five fingers of his right hand (compare Hand of Glory) and spins them about like a flaming wheel, as a result of which he is unable to turn quickly for fear of extinguishing their light.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berkeliaran malam di Finistère dengan lima lilin di jari tangan kanan, seperti roda api.",
        "en": "He roams Finistère at night with five candles on his right hand, spun like a flaming wheel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jan-gant-i-tan-c04",
      "source_id": "jan-gant-i-tan-s2",
      "quote": "Le Yan-gant-y-tan est souvent représenté comme une créature grande et maigre au visage hideux et grotesque, ou bien à un homme sauvage velu.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk tinggi kurus (grande et maigre) berwajah mengerikan atau lelaki liar berbulu (homme sauvage velu).",
        "en": "A tall thin creature (grande et maigre) with a hideous face, or a hairy wild man (homme sauvage velu)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jan-gant-i-tan-c05",
      "source_id": "jan-gant-i-tan-s2",
      "quote": "Dans le Dictionnaire Infernal, l’occultiste et essayiste Collin de Plancy donne pour signification de son nom jan gant, « Vagabond de la Nuit »",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Collin de Plancy: jan gant, Vagabond de la Nuit; nama lain Jan-gant-i-tan.",
        "en": "Collin de Plancy: jan gant, Vagabond de la Nuit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jan-gant-i-tan-c06",
      "source_id": "jan-gant-i-tan-s1",
      "quote": "A sure way to ward off the bad omen of Yan-gant-y-tan is to leave a small bag of gold or of gold chain around a travelers post which Yan-gant-y-tan will steal and leave the house for another day.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kantong emas di tiang menangkal pertanda buruknya.",
        "en": "A bag of gold on a post wards off his bad omen."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Llamhigyn y Dŵr (\"Pelompat Air\") adalah makhluk jahat cerita rakyat Wales yang tinggal di rawa dan kolam, berupa katak raksasa bersayap kelelawar dan berekor bersengat.",
    "en": "Llamhigyn y Dŵr ('Water Leaper') is an evil creature of Welsh folklore living in swamps and ponds, a giant frog with bat's wings and a stinging tail.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "llamhigyn-y-dwr-c01",
      "llamhigyn-y-dwr-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Llamhigyn y Dŵr (bahasa Wales/Welsh untuk \"Pelompat Air\", Water Leaper) adalah makhluk jahat dari cerita rakyat Wales yang tinggal di rawa dan kolam. Ia digambarkan sebagai katak raksasa bersayap kelelawar tanpa kaki sama sekali, dengan ekor panjang seperti kadal yang bersengat di ujungnya. Ia melompat di atas air dengan sayapnya, sehingga dinamai demikian. Ia disalahkan atas berbagai masalah, dari putusnya tali pancing hingga dimakannya ternak, bahkan nelayan.",
      "en": "Llamhigyn y Dŵr (Welsh for 'Water Leaper') is an evil creature of Welsh folklore that lived in swamps and ponds. It is pictured as a giant frog with a bat's wings and no legs at all, and a long lizard-like tail ending in a sting. It skips across the water on its wings, hence the name. It was blamed for everything from snapped fishing lines to devoured livestock and even fishermen.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "llamhigyn-y-dwr-c01",
        "llamhigyn-y-dwr-c02",
        "llamhigyn-y-dwr-c03"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel fr, sayap kelelawarnya menggantikan kaki depan, ia tidak berkaki belakang, dan ekor kadalnya bersengat; ia memutuskan tali pancing, memakan ternak, dan kadang nelayan. Menurut artikel es, Saltador de agua adalah makhluk folklore Wales (Gales) yang hidup di rawa dan kolam, melompat di atas air dengan sayapnya.",
      "en": "According to the fr article its bat's wings take the place of front legs, it has no hind legs, and its lizard tail bears a sting; it breaks fishing lines, eats livestock and sometimes fishermen. According to the es article the Saltador de agua is a creature of Welsh (Gales) folklore living in swamps and ponds that leaps over the water on its wings.",

  "era": null,
  "habitats": [],
  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [
    {
      "value": "shapeshifter",
      "claim_ids": [
        "melion-c01",
        "melion-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Melion adalah ksatria Raja Arthur dalam lai Breton anonim yang berubah menjadi serigala dengan cincin ajaib dan dikhianati istrinya.",
    "en": "Melion is a knight of King Arthur in an anonymous Breton lai who turns into a wolf with a magic ring and is betrayed by his wife.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "melion-c01",
      "melion-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Melion adalah lai Breton anonim yang mengisahkan seorang ksatria yang berubah menjadi serigala demi cinta istrinya, yang kemudian mengkhianatinya. Lai itu diperkirakan disusun antara 1190 dan 1204 dan terdapat dalam satu naskah dari 1268 (Bibliothèque de l'Arsenal, Paris) berdialek Picard. Melion, ksatria Raja Arthur (King Arthur), bersumpah tak akan menikahi perempuan yang pernah mencintai lelaki lain, lalu menikahi putri Raja Irlandia (King of Ireland) yang mengaku hanya mencintainya.",
      "en": "Melion is an anonymous Breton lai telling of a knight who turns into a werewolf for love of his wife, who then betrays him. It was probably composed between 1190 and 1204 and survives in one 1268 manuscript (Bibliothèque de l'Arsenal, Paris) in the Picard dialect. Melion, a knight of King Arthur, vows never to wed a woman who has loved another man, then marries the King of Ireland's daughter, who says she has loved no one but him.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "melion-c01",
        "melion-c02",
        "melion-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Saat berburu, istrinya berkata ia akan mati jika tak memakan daging seekor rusa jantan; Melion memintanya menyentuh kepalanya dengan batu cincin ajaib sehingga ia menjadi serigala untuk memburu rusa itu, tetapi istrinya membawa pakaian dan cincinnya lalu kabur ke Irlandia (Ireland) bersama seorang pengawal. Melion, masih berwujud serigala, menyelinap ke kapal ke Irlandia, bergabung dengan sepuluh serigala lain dan membunuh ternak serta orang, hingga Raja Arthur datang dan mengenali sikapnya yang jinak; sang istri dipaksa membawa cincin itu untuk memulihkannya. Menurut artikel fr, ia kembali menjadi manusia bila disentuh dengan batu merah (pierre vermeille).",
      "en": "Out hunting, his wife declares she will die unless she eats a certain stag's flesh; Melion has her touch his head with the stone of a magic ring so he becomes a wolf to chase it, but she takes his clothes and the ring and flees to Ireland with a squire. Still a wolf, Melion stows away to Ireland, joins ten other wolves and kills livestock and people, until King Arthur arrives and notices his tame courtly manner; the wife is made to bring the ring to restore him. According to the fr article he becomes a man again when touched with a red stone (pierre vermeille).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "melion-c04",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "melion-c01",
      "source_id": "melion-s1",
      "quote": "Melion is an anonymous Breton lai that tells the story of a knight who transforms into a werewolf for the love of his wife who betrays him.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Melion lai Breton anonim tentang ksatria yang menjadi serigala jadian demi cinta istrinya yang mengkhianatinya.",
        "en": "Melion is an anonymous Breton lai about a knight who becomes a werewolf for love of his treacherous wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "melion-c02",
      "source_id": "melion-s1",
      "quote": "The actual date of composition is estimated between 1190 and 1204. The lai of Melion is contained in one existing manuscript: C. Paris, Bibliothèque de l'Arsenal, 3516, f. 343r, col. 1 - 344r, col. 4. This manuscript dates from 1268. The text is written in the Picard dialect.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Disusun 1190–1204; satu naskah 1268 di Bibliothèque de l'Arsenal, Paris, dialek Picard.",
        "en": "Composed 1190–1204; one 1268 manuscript in the Bibliothèque de l'Arsenal, Paris, in Picard dialect."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "melion-c03",
      "source_id": "melion-s1",
      "quote": "Melion tells the story of a knight named Melion who serves King Arthur and who vows that he will never marry a woman who has loved another man. In the age of courtly love, it is impossible for Melion to find such a woman at court. One day while out hunting, Melion meets the daughter of the King of Ireland who tells him that she has never loved a man other than him.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Melion ksatria Raja Arthur (King Arthur), menikahi putri Raja Irlandia (King of Ireland); di Britania dan Irlandia.",
        "en": "Melion, a knight of King Arthur, marries the daughter of the King of Ireland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "melion-c04",
      "source_id": "melion-s1",
      "quote": "Melion promises her the meat and asks her to help him transform into a wolf using a magical ring. The wife touches Melion's head with the stone of the ring, and he heads off into the forest after the stag. Meanwhile, the wife takes Melion's clothes and the ring, and she elopes to Ireland with the squire.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istri menyentuh kepalanya dengan batu cincin ajaib, ia menjadi serigala; istri membawa cincin dan kabur ke Irlandia (Ireland) dengan pengawal.",
        "en": "His wife touches his head with the ring's stone and he becomes a wolf; she takes the ring and flees to Ireland with the squire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "melion-c05",
      "source_id": "melion-s1",
      "quote": "Melion bands together with ten other wolves and begins killing livestock and people. The people complain to the king, who hunts down ten of the eleven, leaving Melion alone. King Arthur arrives in Ireland, and Melion goes to him.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Melion bergabung dengan sepuluh serigala; Raja Arthur (King Arthur) tiba di Irlandia (Ireland).",
        "en": "Melion joins ten wolves; King Arthur arrives in Ireland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "melion-c06",
      "source_id": "melion-s2",
      "quote": "Mélion est un personnage issu d'un lai du même nom, qui se transforme en loup grâce à l'enchantement d'un anneau. Il demande à sa femme de le toucher ensuite avec une pierre vermeille pour qu'il redevienne un homme.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mélion berubah menjadi serigala (loup) karena cincin (anneau); kembali manusia dengan batu merah (pierre vermeille).",
        "en": "Mélion becomes a wolf (loup) through a ring (anneau); a red stone (pierre vermeille) restores him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "melion-c07",
      "source_id": "melion-s1",
      "quote": "The squire confesses, and the daughter is forced to come to court with the magical ring to restore Melion.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengawal mengaku; sang putri dipaksa membawa cincin ajaib untuk memulihkan Melion.",
        "en": "The squire confesses; the daughter must bring the ring to restore Melion."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sluagh (\"rombongan\") atau Sluagh na marbh adalah rombongan arwah orang mati yang tak diampuni dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia dan Skotlandia, yang terbang di udara.",
    "en": "The Sluagh ('host') or Sluagh na marbh are the hosts of the unforgiven dead in Irish and Scottish folklore, flying through the air.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sluagh-c01",
      "sluagh-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sluagh (Irlandia Kuno/Old Irish: slúag; Inggris: \"rombongan, pasukan, kerumunan\"), atau Sluagh na marbh (\"rombongan orang mati\"), adalah rombongan arwah orang mati yang tak diampuni dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia (Irish) dan Skotlandia (Scottish). Menurut folklorist Britania Lewis Spence, di Western Isles Skotlandia Sluagh atau rombongan peri dianggap terdiri atas jiwa orang mati yang terbang di udara, dan pesta orang mati pada Halloween juga perayaan para peri. Nama Gaelik Skotlandia Sluagh berasal dari Irlandia Kuno slúag dan akar Proto-Kelt *slougo-.",
      "en": "In Irish and Scottish folklore the Sluagh, from Old Irish slúag ('host, army, crowd'), also called Sluagh na marbh or 'host of the dead', were bands of the unforgiven dead. The British folklorist Lewis Spence explained that on Scotland's Western Isles this fairy host was believed to consist of dead souls travelling through the sky, and the Hallowe'en feast of the dead was also the festival of the fairies. The Scottish Gaelic name comes from Old Irish slúag and the Proto-Celtic root *slougo-.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sluagh-c01",
        "sluagh-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Biasanya terbang dalam bentuk bulan sabit seperti kawanan burung, mereka konon dapat mendekati seseorang dari arah mana pun, mengangkatnya, dan membawanya jauh melalui udara dari satu pulau ke pulau lain. Meski kadang menyelamatkan manusia dari celah batu berbahaya, mereka umumnya digambarkan berbahaya bagi manusia. Menurut artikel fr, Sluagh digambarkan sebagai bayangan terbang berputar dengan jari panjang kurus berselaput dan kaki cacat bercakar panjang, dan kawanannya menimbulkan bayangan, bunyi kepakan sayap, serta bau daging busuk. Dalam legenda Irlandia ia menyerang orang sekarat yang masih lemah di ranjangnya.",
      "en": "Usually flying in a crescent like a flight of birds, they could come at a person from any direction, lift him and carry him far through the air from one island to another. Though they sometimes rescued people from dangerous rock clefts, they were generally shown as a danger to mortals. According to the fr article the Sluagh appears as a flying, whirling shadow with long thin webbed fingers and twisted legs ending in long claws, and its flocks bring shadow, the sound of beating wings and a smell of rotting meat. In Irish legend it preyed on the dying still weak in their beds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sluagh-c02",
        "sluagh-c04",
        "sluagh-c06",
        "sluagh-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "or Sluagh na marbh ('host of the dead'), were the hosts of the unforgiven dead in Irish and Scottish folklore. In the words of British folklorist Lewis Spence, \"In the Western Isles of Scotland the Sluagh, or fairy host, was regarded as composed of the souls of the dead flying through the air, and the feast of the dead at Hallowe'en was likewise the festival of the fairies.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sluagh (Irlandia Kuno/Old Irish slúag; Inggris: host) atau Sluagh na marbh rombongan arwah tak diampuni cerita rakyat Irlandia (Irish) dan Skotlandia (Scottish); Lewis Spence (British): Western Isles of Scotland, jiwa orang mati, Hallowe'en; di Britania dan Irlandia. Halloween.",
        "en": "The Sluagh or Sluagh na marbh were hosts of the unforgiven dead in Irish and Scottish folklore; per Lewis Spence, in the Western Isles of Scotland the souls of the dead flying, with the Hallowe'en feast also the fairies' festival."

    {

      "source_id": "sluagh-s1",
      "quote": "Usually taking a crescent form, similar to a flight of birds, they were said to be able to approach and pick up a person from any direction and then transport them far away through the air, from one island to another. Although they would sometimes rescue humans from dangerous rock clefts, they were generally portrayed as dangerous to mortals.",

      "source_id": "sluagh-s2",
      "quote": "Le Sluagh était décrit comme une ombre volante et tourbillonnante avec de longs doigts maigres et palmés et des jambes déformées qui se terminaient par de longues griffes.       "locator": null,

      "quote": "Le Sluagh s'en prenait aux mourants alors qu'ils étaient encore dans leur lit et faibles.",

  "era": null,
  "habitats": [],
  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sooterkin adalah makhluk kecil dongeng mirip tikus mondok yang dahulu dipercaya dapat dilahirkan perempuan tertentu, dikaitkan dengan perempuan Belanda yang duduk di atas tungku.",
    "en": "The sooterkin is a fabled small mole-like creature that some women were once believed able to give birth to, blamed on Dutch women sitting over stoves.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sooterkin-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sooterkin adalah makhluk kecil dongeng mirip tikus mondok yang dahulu dipercaya dapat dilahirkan perempuan tertentu. Asal khayalan yang mula-mula bersifat lelucon ini berasal dari abad ke-18, dan sebagian dokter terkemuka masa itu menganggapnya nyata. Ia dikaitkan dengan kebiasaan perempuan Belanda (Dutch) yang sering duduk di atas tungku atau memakai penghangat di bawah rok mereka, yang konon membiakkan hewan kecil yang kemudian tumbuh dan lahir.",
      "en": "Folk belief once held that some women could give birth to a sooterkin, a small mole-like creature of fable. This at-first jokey fancy dates from the 18th century, and some leading physicians of the time took it as fact. It was blamed on Dutch women's habit of sitting on stoves or warming themselves with them under their petticoats, supposedly breeding a small animal that would grow and be born.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sooterkin-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dokter Inggris (English) John Maubray dalam The Female Physician menyatakan perempuan dapat melahirkan sooterkin; ia pendukung teori \"maternal impression\" dan terlibat dalam kasus Mary Toft, yang tampak membuktikan teorinya dengan \"melahirkan\" kelinci, meski seluruh perkara itu akhirnya terbongkar sebagai tipuan. Seseorang dengan nama samaran Dr Giovanni lalu menerbitkan The Sooterkin Dissected yang menyatakan percaya padanya adalah kebodohan. Menurut artikel de, John Cleveland pada 1654 menulis bahwa perempuan Belanda konon melahirkan sooterkin \"tidak jauh berbeda dengan tikus\" bersama anak-anak mereka, dan makhluk ini juga disebut Suyger.",
      "en": "The English physician John Maubray, in The Female Physician, held that women could bear sooterkins; he championed 'maternal impression' and was drawn into the case of Mary Toft, who seemed to prove his theory by 'giving birth' to rabbits, though the whole affair was finally exposed as a hoax. Someone under the alias Dr Giovanni then published The Sooterkin Dissected, calling belief in it stupidity. According to the de article John Cleveland wrote in 1654 that Dutch women were said to bear a sooterkin 'not unlike to a Rat' together with their children, and the creature is also called Suyger.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sooterkin-c02",
        "sooterkin-c03",
        "sooterkin-c05",
        "sooterkin-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "source_id": "sooterkin-s1",

      "source_id": "sooterkin-s2",
      "quote": "Sooterkin, auch Suyger, ist eine sagenumwobene kleine Kreatur von der Größe einer Maus, von der manche Menschen in der Zeit vor der Aufklärung glaubten, dass Frauen sie unter bestimmten Umständen als Missgeburten gebären könnten.",

        "en": "John Cleveland (1654): Holland Women deliver a Sooterkin, not unlike a Rat."

      "id": "sooterkin-c07",
      "source_id": "sooterkin-s2",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tangie adalah roh laut pengubah wujud dalam cerita rakyat Orkney dan Shetland, berwujud kuda atau lelaki tua berselimut rumput laut.",
    "en": "The tangie is a shape-shifting sea spirit of Orkney and Shetland folklore, appearing as a horse or an old man covered in seaweed.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tangie-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tangie (atau tongie) adalah roh laut pengubah wujud dalam cerita rakyat Kepulauan Orkney dan Shetland di Skotlandia (Scotland). Sebagai kuda laut atau duyung lelaki, ia berwujud kuda atau lelaki tua. Biasanya ia digambarkan berselimut rumput laut, dan namanya berasal dari \"tang\", rumput laut genus Fucus. Ia terkenal meneror pelancong yang sendirian, terutama perempuan muda di jalan dekat danau (loch) pada malam hari, yang diculiknya dan dimakannya di bawah air.",
      "en": "Island lore from Orkney and Shetland in Scotland tells of the tangie (or tongie), a sea spirit that changes shape; as a sea horse or merman it appears as either a horse or an old man. It is usually said to be draped in seaweed, and its name comes from 'tang', seaweed of the genus Fucus. It is known for frightening lone travellers, above all young women on roads near the lochs at night, whom it carries off and devours underwater.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tangie-c01",
        "tangie-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mirip tetapi berbeda dari nuggle yang lebih kecil dan kurang berbahaya, tangie dapat membuat manusia dan hewan gila. Tangie berperan besar dalam legenda Shetland tentang Black Eric, pencuri domba: tangie yang ditungganginya memberinya bantuan gaib saat ia menyerbu pertanian sekitar. Dalam pertarungan terakhirnya dengan petani Sandy Breamer, Black Eric jatuh ke laut dan tewas, tetapi tangie terus meneror daerah itu. Menurut artikel ru, Танги juga disebut pengubah wujud laut yang dapat membuat orang atau hewan gila.",
      "en": "Similar to yet distinct from the smaller, milder nuggle, the tangie can drive people and animals mad. It plays a big part in Shetland's tale of Black Eric the sheep thief, whose tangie steed gave him supernatural help as he raided nearby crofts. In his last fight with the crofter Sandy Breamer, Black Eric fell into the sea and died, but the tangie went on terrorising the area. According to the ru article the Танги is likewise a sea shapeshifter able to drive a person or animal mad.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tangie-c03",
        "tangie-c04",

        "tangie-c06"

      "statement": {
        "id": "Tangie (tongie) roh laut pengubah wujud cerita rakyat Kepulauan Orkney dan Shetland, Skotlandia (Scotland); kuda laut atau duyung lelaki (Kelpie); kuda atau lelaki tua; rumput laut Fucus; di Britania Raya.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Adsullata adalah dewi sungai bangsa Kelt Daratan yang dikaitkan dengan Sungai Savus (Sava) di Noricum.",
    "en": "Adsullata was a Celtic river goddess honoured by the Celts of the continent and tied to the Savus, today's Sava, in Noricum.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "adsullata-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Kelt (Celtic mythology), Adsullata adalah dewi sungai bangsa Kelt Daratan (Continental Celts) yang dikaitkan dengan Sungai Savus (Sava) di Noricum. Kemudian ia dikatakan sampai ke Brittany dari Galia Kelt (Celtic Gaul) dan diyakini dewi sumber air panas serta asal-usul dewi matahari Anglo-Kelt (Anglo-Celtic) Sul. Namanya tampaknya berasal dari Proto-Kelt (Proto-Celtic) *Ad-sūg-lat-ā, harfiah \"cairan isap\", yang mungkin sebutan untuk \"cairan pemberi susu\"; bentuk Romawi-Britania (Romano-British)-nya kemungkinan *Adsuglata.",
      "en": "Celtic myth knows Adsullata as a river goddess of the Continental Celts, bound up with the Savus (Sava) in Noricum. Said to have later passed from Celtic Gaul into Brittany, she was thought to preside over hot springs and to lie behind Sul, the Anglo-Celtic sun goddess. Her name seems to come from Proto-Celtic *Ad-sūg-lat-ā, literally 'sucking liquid', perhaps a byword for 'suck-giving liquid'; its Romano-British form would likely have been *Adsuglata.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adsullata-c01",
        "adsullata-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel sr, Adsullata adalah dewi sungai dalam mitologi Kelt yang kemungkinan besar dewi Sungai Sava, dan namanya berarti \"dia yang menimba air\". Menurut artikel tr, ia dikenal hanya dari satu prasasti yang ditemukan di Saudörfel, Austria.",
      "en": "According to the sr article Adsullata was a river goddess of Celtic myth, most likely of the Sava, and her name means 'she who draws water'. According to the tr article she is known from a single inscription found at Saudörfel in Austria.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adsullata-c03",
        "adsullata-c04",
        "adsullata-c05"
      ]
    }

      "id": "adsullata-c01",
      "source_id": "adsullata-s1",
      "quote": "In Celtic mythology, Adsullata was a river goddess of the Continental Celts associated with the River Savus (Sava) in Noricum. Later she came to Brittany from Celtic Gaul and was believed to be a goddess of hot springs and the origin of the Anglo-Celtic sun goddess, Sul.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Kelt (Celtic mythology) Adsullata dewi sungai Kelt Daratan (Continental Celts), Sungai Savus (Sava), Noricum; ke Brittany dari Galia Kelt (Celtic Gaul); dewi air panas, asal dewi matahari Anglo-Kelt (Anglo-Celtic) Sul (Sulis).",
        "en": "In Celtic myth Adsullata was a river goddess of the Continental Celts tied to the Savus (Sava) in Noricum; later a hot-spring goddess in Brittany and origin of the Anglo-Celtic sun goddess Sul."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adsullata-c02",
      "source_id": "adsullata-s1",
      "quote": "This theonym appears to be derived from Proto-Celtic *Ad-sūg-lat-ā. That derivation literally means (allative) \"sucking liquid\", which may have been a byword for the notion of \"suck-giving liquid\" The Romano-British form of this Proto-Celtic reconstruction would likely have been *Adsuglata.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari Proto-Kelt (Proto-Celtic) *Ad-sūg-lat-ā, cairan isap; bentuk Romawi-Britania (Romano-British) *Adsuglata.",
        "en": "From Proto-Celtic *Ad-sūg-lat-ā, sucking liquid; Romano-British *Adsuglata."

    },
    {
      "id": "adsullata-c03",
      "source_id": "adsullata-s2",
      "quote": "Адсулата (лат. Adsullata) је била речна богиња у келтској митологији. Највероватније је била богиња Саве.",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel sr, Адсулата (Adsullata) dewi sungai (речна богиња) mitologi Kelt (келтској), kemungkinan dewi Sava (Саве).",
        "en": "Per the sr article, Адсулата (Adsullata) was a river goddess (речна богиња) of Celtic (келтској) myth, most likely of the Sava (Саве)."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Arkan sonney (\"landak keberuntungan\") adalah sebutan Manx untuk landak sekaligus hewan peri berwujud babi putih yang membawa keberuntungan bagi yang menangkapnya.",
    "en": "Arkan sonney ('lucky urchin') is the Manx word for hedgehog and a fairy animal in the form of a white pig that brings luck to whoever catches it.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "arkan-sonney-c01",
      "arkan-sonney-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Arkan sonney (harfiah \"landak keberuntungan\" atau \"babi kecil yang berlimpah\") adalah sebutan Manx untuk landak. Arkan sonney juga sejenis hewan peri berwujud babi putih yang membawa keberuntungan bagi yang berhasil menangkapnya; bahkan sekadar melihat \"babi keberuntungan\" itu dianggap pertanda baik. Konon orang yang menangkapnya akan selalu menemukan koin perak di sakunya, tetapi menahannya terlalu lama akan mendatangkan sial.",
      "en": "Arkan sonney (literally 'lucky urchin' or 'plentiful little pig') is the Manx word for hedgehog. The arkan sonney is also a kind of fairy animal shaped like a white pig that brings good fortune to anyone who catches it; merely seeing the 'lucky piggy' was a good omen. Whoever caught one was said always to find a silver coin in his pocket, though keeping it too long brought bad luck.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "arkan-sonney-c01",
        "arkan-sonney-c02",
        "arkan-sonney-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Fairy Tales From the Isle of Man (1951) karya Dora Broome, babi putih itu bermata dan bertelinga merah, dan meski dapat mengubah ukurannya ia tak dapat mengubah wujudnya. Menurut artikel no, dalam cerita rakyat Manx ada makhluk seperti nisse atau elf yang tampak seperti landak bertelinga panjang; menangkapnya membawa keberuntungan, karena itu mereka disebut \"babi keberuntungan\", tetapi karena itu pula mereka menghindari manusia. Menurut artikel fr, dalam folklore Isle of Man arkan sonney berarti landak.",
      "en": "In Dora Broome's Fairy Tales From the Isle of Man (1951) the white pig has red eyes and ears, and though it can change its size it cannot change its shape. According to the no article Manx folk tales tell of a being like a nisse or elf that looks like a long-eared hedgehog; catching one brings luck, hence the name 'lucky pig', and for that very reason they shun people. According to the fr article, in Isle of Man folklore arkan sonney means a hedgehog.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "arkan-sonney-c04",
        "arkan-sonney-c05",
        "arkan-sonney-c06"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "Arkan sonney (/ˈɑːrkən ˈsɒni/; Manx pronunciation: ['aːrkan ˈsonə] , literally \"lucky urchin\" or \"plentiful little pig\") is the Manx term for hedgehog.",

    {
      "id": "arkan-sonney-c02",
      "source_id": "arkan-sonney-s1",
      "quote": "The arkan sonney is a type of fairy animal that takes the form of a white pig that brings good fortune to those who manage to catch it. It was even considered a favourable omen just to have seen the \"lucky piggy\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "arkan-sonney-c03",
      "source_id": "arkan-sonney-s1",
      "quote": "It was also said that if you caught one you would always find a silver coin in your pocket. However, holding on to it for too long will result in bad fortune.",

      "source_id": "arkan-sonney-s1",

        "en": "Per the no article, Manx (mansk) tales tell of a nisse- or elf-like (alv) being like a long-eared hedgehog (piggsvin); catching one brings luck."

    "id": "Bauchan adalah sejenis hobgoblin rumah tangga dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia, sering jahil dan kadang berbahaya, tetapi sangat membantu bila diperlukan.",
    "en": "The bauchan is a kind of domestic hobgoblin of Scottish folklore, often mischievous and sometimes dangerous, but very helpful when needed.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bauchan-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bauchan (Skotlandia/Scottish: bòcan; Inggris: bauchan, buckawn, atau bogan) adalah sejenis hobgoblin rumah tangga dalam cerita rakyat Skotlandia (Scottish folklore). Ia sering jahil dan kadang berbahaya, tetapi juga sangat membantu bila diperlukan. Menurut artikel es, bauchan adalah makhluk mitologi Kelt (celta), sejenis brownie, yang dapat berwujud lelaki atau perempuan, berbulu lebat, jahil, dan penyendiri.",
      "en": "The bauchan (Scottish bòcan; English bauchan, buckawn or bogan) is a kind of domestic hobgoblin in Scottish folklore. Often mischievous and at times dangerous, it can also be most helpful when need arises. According to the es article the bauchan is a creature of Celtic (celta) myth, a type of brownie, which may be male or female, and is very hairy, mischievous and solitary.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bauchan-c01",
        "bauchan-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "John Francis Campbell dalam Popular Tales of the West Highlands menuturkan kisah Callum Mor MacIntosh, yang pertaniannya di Lochaber dihantui bauchan. Hubungan keduanya bertentangan: bauchan itu suka berkelahi dan menantang, tetapi sering membantu pekerjaan pertanian. Ketika Callum beremigrasi ke New York City, bauchan itu ikut dan membantunya membuka lahan barunya. Dalam kisah ini bauchan dapat berubah wujud dan menjelma menjadi kambing.",
      "en": "John Francis Campbell, in Popular Tales of the West Highlands, recounts how a bauchan haunted the Lochaber farm of one Callum Mor MacIntosh. Their relationship was contradictory: the bauchan was quarrelsome and combative yet often helped with farm work. After Callum moved across the ocean to New York City, the creature followed and lent a hand clearing his fresh plot. In this tale the bauchan is a shapeshifter able to turn into a goat.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bauchan-c02",
        "bauchan-c05",
        "bauchan-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "bauchan-c01",
      "source_id": "bauchan-s1",
      "quote": "The bauchan (Scottish: bòcan English: bauchan, buckawn or bogan) is a type of domestic hobgoblin in Scottish folklore. It is often mischievous and sometimes dangerous, but is also very helpful when the need arises.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bauchan (Skotlandia/Scottish bòcan; Inggris/English bauchan, buckawn, bogan) hobgoblin (Hobgoblin) rumah tangga cerita rakyat Skotlandia (Scottish folklore); jahil, berbahaya, membantu; di Britania Raya.",
        "en": "The bauchan (Scottish bòcan; English bauchan, buckawn, bogan) is a domestic hobgoblin of Scottish folklore, mischievous, sometimes dangerous, but helpful."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bauchan-c02",
      "source_id": "bauchan-s1",
      "quote": "John Francis Campbell in his Popular Tales of the West Highlands tells the story of Callum Mor MacIntosh whose farm in Lochaber was haunted by a bauchan. The relationship between Callum and the bauchan was noted as being contradictory in nature.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "John Francis Campbell (Popular Tales of the West Highlands): pertanian Callum Mor MacIntosh di Lochaber dihantui bauchan.",
        "en": "John Francis Campbell (Popular Tales of the West Highlands): Callum Mor MacIntosh's farm in Lochaber was haunted by a bauchan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bauchan-c03",
      "source_id": "bauchan-s1",
      "quote": "When Callum emigrated to New York City the bauchan went with him and helped him clear his new plot of land. In this tale the bauchan is a shapeshifter and is able to transform into a goat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ikut Callum ke New York City dan membantu membuka lahan; dapat menjadi kambing.",
        "en": "It followed Callum to New York City and helped clear his land; it can become a goat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bauchan-c04",
      "source_id": "bauchan-s2",
      "quote": "Un bauchan (también bòcan [Ir.], bogan o buckawn) es una criatura de la mitología celta, un tipo de brownie. Podían ser tanto masculino como femenino. Se caracterizarían por tener mucho pelo, ser traviesos y solitarios.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel es, bauchan (bòcan, bogan, buckawn) makhluk mitologi Kelt (celta), sejenis brownie (Brownie), lelaki atau perempuan, berbulu, jahil, penyendiri.",
        "en": "Per the es article the bauchan (bòcan, bogan, buckawn) is a Celtic (celta) creature, a type of brownie, male or female, hairy, mischievous, solitary."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bauchan-c05",
      "source_id": "bauchan-s1",
      "quote": "While the bauchan was belligerent and combative, he often provided assistance in various farm-related tasks.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bauchan suka berkelahi tetapi membantu pekerjaan pertanian.",
        "en": "The bauchan was belligerent yet helped with farm tasks."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Black Lady of Bradley Woods adalah hantu yang konon menghantui hutan dekat desa Bradley, Lincolnshire, Inggris, perempuan muda berjubah hitam yang tak pernah mencelakai siapa pun.",
    "en": "The Black Lady of Bradley Woods is a ghost said to haunt the woods near the village of Bradley, Lincolnshire, England, a young woman in a black cloak who has never harmed anyone.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "black-lady-of-bradley-woods-c01",
      "black-lady-of-bradley-woods-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Black Lady of Bradley Woods adalah hantu yang konon menghantui hutan dekat desa Bradley, Lincolnshire, Inggris (England). Para saksi menggambarkannya muda dan cantik, tinggi sekitar 168 cm, berjubah hitam panjang dengan tudung hitam yang menutupi rambutnya tetapi memperlihatkan wajahnya yang pucat, sedih, dan basah air mata. Menurut legenda ia tak pernah mencelakai siapa pun dan hanya pemandangan yang memilukan meski meresahkan. Kisah ini telah diceritakan turun-temurun dan dulu dipakai orang tua untuk menakuti anak-anak: \"the black lady will get you!\"",
      "en": "The Black Lady of Bradley Woods is a ghost said to haunt the woods near the village of Bradley, Lincolnshire, England. Witnesses describe her as young and pretty, about 168 cm tall, in a flowing black cloak and black hood that hides her hair but shows her pale, mournful, tear-stained face. Legend says she has never harmed anyone and is only a pitiful if unsettling sight. The story has been told for generations and was once used by parents to scare children: 'the black lady will get you!'",
      "claim_ids": [
        "black-lady-of-bradley-woods-c01",
        "black-lady-of-bradley-woods-c02",
        "black-lady-of-bradley-woods-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut mitosnya, pada masa Perang Mawar (Wars of the Roses) atau Perang Para Baron (Barons' Wars), seorang penebang kayu muda tinggal bersama istri dan bayinya di sebuah pondok di Bradley Woods. Ia pergi berperang, dan setiap hari istrinya menunggu di tepi hutan, hingga pasukan musuh menyeberangi Humber; tiga penunggang kuda memperkosanya dan merampas bayinya. Ia mengembara mencari anak dan suaminya dengan sia-sia, dan setelah kematiannya orang mulai melihatnya berkeliaran di hutan. Menurut artikel sv, kisah ini telah diceritakan selama banyak generasi.",
      "en": "By the legend, during the Wars of the Roses or the Barons' Wars a young woodsman lived with his wife and baby son in a cottage in Bradley Woods. He went to war, and every day his wife waited at the wood's edge, until an enemy army crossed the Humber; three horsemen assaulted her and seized her baby. Grief-stricken, she roamed the wood looking for her son and husband but never found them, and after her death people began to see her roaming there. According to the sv article the story has been told for many generations.",
      "claim_ids": [

      ]

      "quote": "Alleged eyewitnesses have described her as being young and pretty, around 5 ft 6 in (168 cm) tall, dressed in a flowing black cloak and a black hood that obscures her hair but reveals her mournful, pale, tear-soaked face. According to the legend she has never harmed anyone",

      "quote": "It was once used by parents to frighten children; this appears to have been a common practice among parents in the area, and children were warned that if they were not safely in bed by a certain time, \"the black lady will get you!\".",

      "source_id": "black-lady-of-bradley-woods-s1",
      "quote": "During the Wars of the Roses, or alternatively the Barons' Wars, a young woodsman and his wife lived with their baby son in a cottage in Bradley Woods. Eventually the woodsman went off to fight in the wars, leaving his wife to bring up the baby alone.",
      "locator": null,

      "source_id": "black-lady-of-bradley-woods-s1",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bleiddwn (\"lelaki serigala\") adalah salah satu dari tiga putra Gwydion dan Gilfaethwy dalam Cabang Keempat Mabinogi, lahir dari pasangan serigala lalu diubah menjadi manusia oleh Math.",
    "en": "Bleiddwn ('wolf-man') is one of the three sons of Gwydion and Gilfaethwy in the Fourth Branch of the Mabinogi, born of a wolf pair and turned human by Math.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bleiddwn-c01",
      "bleiddwn-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bleiddwn (dari kata Wales/Welsh blaidd, \"serigala\") muncul dalam cabang keempat dari Four Branches of the Mabinogi dan merupakan salah satu dari tiga putra Gwydion dan Gilfaethwy. Sebagai hukuman atas pemerkosaan Goewin, Math membuang kedua keponakannya dengan mengubah mereka menjadi sepasang rusa selama setahun, lalu babi, dan akhirnya serigala. Mereka mendapat tiga anak dalam tiga tahun itu: Hyddwn, Hychddwn, dan Bleiddwn, yang lahir dari pasangan serigala.",
      "en": "Named from Welsh blaidd, 'wolf', Bleiddwn is one of three sons born to Gwydion and Gilfaethwy in the last of the Mabinogi's Four Branches. As punishment for raping Goewin, Math cast out his two nephews, changing them for a year each into a mated pair of deer, of pigs and finally of wolves. Over those three years they had three young: Hyddwn, Hychddwn and Bleiddwn, the last born of the wolf pairing.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bleiddwn-c01",
        "bleiddwn-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Math mengambilnya dan mengubahnya menjadi manusia, lalu membaptisnya Bleiddwn (\"Wolf-man\"). Menurut artikel fr, Bleiddwn (\"serigala kecil\") adalah anak serigala ajaib dalam mitologi Kelt Britonik yang muncul dalam Cabang Keempat Mabinogi, \"Math fab Mathonwy\", dan diperanakkan oleh Gwydion dan Gilfaethwy ketika keduanya berwujud serigala jantan dan betina karena sihir paman mereka Math, penguasa Gwynedd.",
      "en": "Math took him and changed him into a human being, naming him Bleiddwn ('Wolf-man'). According to the fr article Bleiddwn ('little wolf') is a fabulous wolf cub of Brittonic myth appearing in the Fourth Branch of the Mabinogi, 'Math son of Mathonwy', fathered by the brothers Gwydion and Gilfaethwy while they were a wolf and she-wolf through the magic of their uncle Math, ruler of Gwynedd.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bleiddwn-c03",
        "bleiddwn-c04",
        "bleiddwn-c05",
        "bleiddwn-c06"
      ]
    }

    {

      "source_id": "bleiddwn-s1",
      "quote": "As punishment for the rape of Goewin, Math banished his nephews, turning them into a breeding pair of deer for a year, then pigs, and finally wolves. They had three children over the three years: Hyddwn, Hychddwn, and Bleiddwn. Bleiddwn is the offspring of the wolf pairing.",

    "id": "Bloody Bones adalah sosok momok dalam cerita rakyat Inggris dan Amerika Utara, tercatat sejak sekitar 1548, yang dipakai untuk menakuti anak-anak agar berkelakuan baik.",
    "en": "Bloody Bones is a bogeyman of English and North American folklore, recorded from about 1548, used to frighten children into good behaviour.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bloody Bones adalah sosok momok (bogeyman) dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English) dan Amerika Utara (North American) yang kemunculan tertulis pertamanya sekitar 1548; seperti momok lain, ia dipakai untuk menakuti anak agar berkelakuan baik. Ia kadang disebut Rawhead, Tommy Rawhead, atau Rawhead-and-Bloody-Bones. John Locke pada 1693 mencatat bahwa nama \"Raw-Head and Bloody-Bones\" dipakai untuk menakuti anak, dan Samuel Johnson dalam kamusnya (1755) mengartikan Rawhead sebagai \"nama hantu untuk menakuti anak\". Kisahnya berasal dari Britania Raya (Great Britain), terutama Lancashire dan Yorkshire, lalu menyebar ke Amerika Serikat bagian selatan (Southern United States).",
      "en": "Bloody Bones is a bogeyman of English and North American folklore whose first written appearance dates to about 1548; like all bogeymen he served to scare children into behaving. He is also called Rawhead, Tommy Rawhead or Rawhead-and-Bloody-Bones. John Locke noted in 1693 that 'Raw-Head and Bloody-Bones' was used to awe children, and Samuel Johnson's 1755 Dictionary glossed Rawhead as a spectre named to frighten children. The stories began in Great Britain, especially Lancashire and Yorkshire, and spread to the Southern United States.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bloody-bones-c01",
        "bloody-bones-c02",
        "bloody-bones-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia kadang dianggap iblis air yang menghantui kolam dalam dan lubang marl tua, menyeret anak ke kedalaman seperti grindylow dan Jenny Greenteeth. Ruth Tongue dalam Somerset Folklore menulis bahwa ia tinggal di lemari gelap di bawah tangga. Old Bloody Bones adalah versi Cornwall yang menghuni Knockers Hole dekat desa Baldhu. Menurut artikel de, ia menghadiahi anak yang sangat baik tetapi menghukum anak nakal dengan menyeret mereka ke saluran air atau ke dalam air dan menenggelamkannya.",
      "en": "He is sometimes seen as a water demon haunting deep ponds and old marl pits, dragging children into the depths like the grindylow and Jenny Greenteeth. Ruth Tongue, in Somerset Folklore, wrote that he lived in a dark cupboard under the stairs. Old Bloody Bones is a Cornish version living in Knockers Hole near the village of Baldhu.     "id": "Bluecap adalah peri atau hantu dalam cerita rakyat Inggris yang menghuni tambang dan tampak sebagai nyala api biru kecil, menuntun penambang ke deposit kaya.",
    "en": "The bluecap is a fairy or ghost of English folklore that dwells in mines and appears as a small blue flame, leading miners to rich deposits.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bluecap-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bluecap adalah peri atau hantu mitos dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore) yang menghuni tambang dan tampak sebagai nyala api biru kecil. Jika para penambang memperlakukannya dengan hormat, bluecap menuntun mereka ke deposit mineral yang kaya. Seperti knocker atau kobold, bluecap juga dapat memperingatkan penambang akan runtuhan. Mereka terutama dikaitkan dengan perbatasan Inggris-Skotlandia (Anglo-Scottish borders).",
      "en": "The bluecap is a mythical fairy or ghost of English folklore that lives in mines and shows itself as a small blue flame. If miners treat them with respect, bluecaps guide them to rich mineral deposits. Like knockers or kobolds, they can also warn miners of cave-ins. They are mostly linked with the Anglo-Scottish borders.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bluecap-c01",
        "bluecap-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bluecap dianggap pekerja keras yang diharapkan dibayar upah setara buruh biasa, sama dengan seorang putter (penambang pendorong gerobak); upah itu ditinggalkan di sudut tambang yang sepi, dan mereka tak mau menerima lebih atau kurang dari haknya. Penambang kadang bercerita melihat bluecap berkelip hinggap di atas tong batu bara penuh dan memindahkannya seolah didorong otot terkuat. Makhluk sejenis yang kurang membantu bernama Cutty Soames, yang memotong tali pengikat pendorong gerobak. Menurut artikel zh, 藍帽子 adalah peri kecil atau hantu rekaan dalam cerita rakyat Inggris yang tinggal di tambang.",
      "en": "Bluecaps were seen as hard workers expecting a working man's pay, equal to an ordinary putter (who pushed the mine wagons); the money was left in a lonely corner of the mine, and they would take neither more nor less than their due. Miners sometimes told of a flickering bluecap settling on a full tub of coal and moving it as if driven by the strongest muscles. A less helpful being of the same kind was Cutty Soames, who cut the rope-traces yoking the assistant putter to the tub. According to the zh article the 藍帽子 is a small fairy or ghost of English folklore living in mines.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia, Buarainech (juga dieja Buareinech) adalah raksasa, ayah Balor, raja kaum Fomori (Fomorians), makhluk setengah dewa yang konon mendiami Irlandia pada zaman purba. Artikel de menyebutnya putra dewa perang Irlandia Neit. Dalam daftar pembangun rath dan benteng termasyhur di Book of Leinster, Balor disebut \"Balor son of Buarainech\", sedangkan Cath Maige Tuired menyebut Balor putra Néit.",
      "en": "In Irish mythology Buarainech (also spelled Buareinech) is a giant, father of Balor, king of the Fomorians, half-divine beings said to have lived in Ireland in remote ages. The de article makes him a son of the Irish war god Neit. A list of famous rath- and castle-builders in the Book of Leinster names Balor as son of Buarainech, whereas the Cath Maige Tuired calls Balor a son of Néit.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "buarainech-c01",
        "buarainech-c02",
        "buarainech-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel de, ketika Buarainech menyuruh para druidnya meramu ramuan ajaib, Balor yang masih kanak-kanak diam-diam mengintip ke dalam kuali, kehilangan satu mata, dan menjadi Zyklop dengan tatapan mematikan. Squire menerjemahkan namanya sebagai \"berwajah sapi\" (Kuh-Gesicht), yang cocok dengan rupa aneh kaum Fomori dalam tradisi.",
      "en": "The de article relates that while Buarainech had his druids brew a magic potion, the child Balor secretly looked into the cauldron, lost an eye and so became a cyclops (Zyklop) with a deadly gaze. Squire renders the name as 'cow-faced' (Kuh-Gesicht), fitting the odd looks traditionally given to the Fomorians.",

        "buarainech-c06"
      ]
